# Imperfection, Readings, and Plurality

Power Dynamics assumes that consequential human and institutional judgments are imperfect.

Competence, ethics, credibility, legitimacy, truth, branch quality, and social value cannot be captured perfectly by one score, authority, or implementation. This is not a reason to avoid evaluation. It is a reason to make evaluation **explicitly governed**.

`phenomenon ≠ evidence ≠ policy interpreting evidence ≠ reading/score ≠ institutional use of that reading`

This permits multiple branches or readings without requiring relativism. A branch may be better supported on one dimension and weaker on another. Different Kristals may preserve different evidence, contexts, ontologies, or scopes.

The objective is not perfect governance but **governable imperfection**.

> **Canonical repo source:** `framework/07-imperfection-and-plurality.md`

---

[Home](Home) · [Power Show](Power-Show) · [Glossary](Glossary)

AUTHORITY: reference
CONTENT_ROLE: knowledge

CONTENT_BYTES: 906
================================================================================================

# Improvement, Support, Adoption, and Dominance

An alternative should be able to say not only “we disagree,” but **what changed and what value was added**.

Possible demonstrations include stronger evidence, better provenance, lower error, lower cost, higher autonomy, better accessibility, improved multilinguality, better outcomes, stronger privacy, better local fit, or improved interoperability.

Support, adoption, and dominance are distinct. A branch may be intellectually supported but rarely deployed, or dominant because of history, compatibility, network effects, official status, or funding.

> **Dominance ≠ Truth ≠ Quality ≠ Legitimacy**

No universal scalar is required. Improvement can be multidimensional and context-dependent.

> **Canonical repo source:** `branch-ecology/improvement-support-dominance.md`

---

# Information, Attention & Media Power

> **Core question:** Who can make something visible, discoverable, ranked, amplified, moderated, or ignored?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **publish, search, rank, distribute, recommend, route, amplify, and preserve access to information**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Visibility power, agenda power, moderation power, amplification power, discoverability power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `attention → affiliation`
- `visibility → credibility / opportunity`
- `media reach → agenda`
- `discoverability → adoption / funding`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- explicit discovery and ranking policies
- alternative discovery paths and plural networks
- export / portability where feasible
- distinguish visibility from truth and adoption from legitimacy

## kOA touchpoints

- Konnaxion is the main distribution / discovery surface
- SmartVote discoverability mechanisms can influence what rises
- brand / narrative can increase attention while remaining non-sovereign

## Analytical note

A system can preserve formal decision equality while still concentrating considerable pre-political power in ranking and discoverability.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `domains/README.md`, `README.md`

# KingCoin as a Legacy Stress Test

Legacy KingCoin proposals include global governance, sanctions, blacklisting, automated penalties, and monetary enforcement.

These mechanisms create strong monetary and enforcement power and expose tensions with later commitments to non-domination, local sovereignty, branch alternatives, and effective exit.

The case is retained as an evolutionary stress test, not silently harmonized with current-core principles.

> **Canonical repo source:** `cases/kingcoin-legacy-stress-test.md`

# Know → Choose → Act → Remember

The kOA loop is a capability-regeneration cycle.

### Know
Transform dispersed contributions into structured, provenance-preserving knowledge without prematurely erasing ambiguity.

### Choose
Transform knowledge into legible orientation while preserving reasons, options, disagreement, and multiple readings.

### Act
Transform orientation into responsibilities, tasks, routing, validation, escalation, and outcomes.

### Remember
Transform experience into reusable memory that lowers the cost of future action.

The political value lies in the **transitions**, because each transition can become a bottleneck or locus of power.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/know-choose-act-remember.md`

# kOA Propagation and Adoption

kOA can expose multiple entry paths while converging on one inspectable architecture.

```text
notice
→ understand
→ examine
→ contextualize
→ try
→ deploy
→ local value
→ independent mastery
→ optional federation
```

Different mechanisms attack different barriers:

- Worlds: contextual / empty-platform barrier;
- cinematic tours: explanation barrier;
- public code and documentation: trust / inspection barrier;
- Capsule Manager / Koali: deployment barrier;
- self-host / offline / fork: lock-in barrier;
- Kintsugi: integration and replacement barrier;
- UCKK: operator barrier;
- SemantiK: language barrier;
- Managed Koali: operations-responsibility barrier;
- SmartVote / Kompendio: discovery and prioritization barrier.

The strongest propagation evidence is not media reach. It is **independent loop closure** by actors who do not require founder mediation.

# kOA Returns and Compounding

kOA can produce returns in several domains: validated knowledge, reusable Kristals, code, operational experience, contributor credibility, network connections, institutional memory, brand recognition, economic resources, standards, and interoperability.

These returns can feed other systems and lower future coordination cost, creating a **power ecology of compounding capability**.

The same loop can create concentration. Recognition can generate visibility, visibility opportunity, and opportunity more recognition. Power Dynamics therefore tracks who receives returns and whether alternatives can accumulate comparable assets.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/returns-compounding.md`

AUTHORITY: reference

# kOA System Map

## v6 power-surface reading

Read each component not only as a function, but as a **capability surface** that can become a **power surface** when control over it changes access, standing, visibility, allocation, timing, interpretation, or continuity. See [Power Surface](Power-Surface) and [Component Power Matrix](Component-Power-Matrix).


This is the bridge between the Power Show and the actual kOA architecture. Each capability surface is paired with the power relations that may emerge if it becomes widely relied upon.

The high-level capability chain is:

> **Contribution → Resolution of meaning → Structured knowledge → Discovery / credibility → Collective choice → Governed work → Coordinated action → Experience → Reusable memory → New cycle**

## Capability map

```text
SemantiK / SenTient
  semantic structure
        ↓
Kristal
  knowledge + provenance + memory
        ↓
Konnaxion
  circulation + people + discovery
        ↓
Koali / EkoH
  credibility context
        ↓
SmartVote
  comparison + advice + choice
        ↓
Orgo
  routing + responsibility + execution
        ↓
Outcomes
        ↓
Kristal / operational memory
  reusable learning
        ↺
```

Cross-cutting layers:

- **AI** — cognitive amplification;
- **Capsules / federation** — portability, deployment, substitution, branch formation;
- **brand / narrative** — recognition, affiliation, transmission;
- **multilingual semantics** — linguistic access and semantic participation.

## Power overlay

Each capability surface can also become a power surface:

| Capability surface | Possible relational power |
|---|---|
| semantic structuration | classification / framing / ontology power |
| knowledge validation | epistemic authority / standard-setting |
| discoverability | ranking / visibility / agenda power |
| credibility interpretation | recognition / standing / opportunity power |
| decision support | default / weighting / advisory influence |
| workflow | routing / timing / escalation / closure power |
| memory | precedent / institutional-memory power |
| federation infrastructure | signing / update / compatibility / registry power |
| AI | hidden interpretation / prioritization power |
| brand | attention / affiliation / legitimacy-by-association |

The constitutional problem is therefore not whether kOA should be powerful. Its purpose is to generate capability. The problem is whether the relations created by successful capability remain **scoped, inspectable, contestable, substitutable, and evolvable**.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/system-map.md`

# Koali / EkoH

Koali and EkoH address a problem of **latent competence**: people may possess valuable experience or expertise that is difficult for others to discover, verify, or mobilize.

The intended transformation is:

`competence evidence → legibility → credibility signal → discoverability → opportunity → participation`

The constitutional limit is explicit:

> **Score ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ truth ≠ sovereignty.**

Signals should be domain-bounded, evidence-linked, versioned, contestable, and portable. Multiple distributions or policies can coexist around common interoperability, supporting **plurality without fragmentation**.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/koali-ekoh.md`

---

[Home](Home) · [Power Show](Power-Show) · [Glossary](Glossary)


## Power-sharing direction

The design direction is not one universal owner of credibility.

Koali is intended to become **duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions**, so useful credibility infrastructure can move from personal property toward an interoperable protocol ecology.

Read [Koali: From Property to Protocol](Koali-From-Property-to-Protocol).

FILE: Power-Dynamics.wiki/Koali-From-Property-to-Protocol.md
AUTHORITY: reference
CONTENT_ROLE: knowledge

# Koali: From Property to Protocol

Koali is a direct test of whether kOA creates a new credibility owner or **shares the capability to organize credibility**.

The current architect-confirmed design intention is:

> **Koali should not remain an exclusive personal property on which everyone else must depend. Its useful capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions.**

This is a design intention, not yet a claim that the complete technical mechanism is implemented.

## Why it matters

Credibility infrastructure can affect discoverability, standing, opportunity, certification, access, trust, collaboration, and advisory influence.

If one operator owns the only meaningful credibility layer, recognition can become gatekeeping.

## Protocolization target

```text
founder-created concept
→ explicit kernel / contracts
→ portable evidence and history
→ policy identity
→ multiple distributions
→ interoperability
→ independent operation
→ branch comparison and evolution
```

The political move is:

> **Personal ownership of a capability → stewardship of an interoperable protocol ecology**

## Plurality without fragmentation

Different Koali distributions can legitimately use different criteria for different domains or communities, provided their policies remain interpretable.

A mature design should support reimplementation, policy legibility, evidence portability, contestation, interoperability, discoverability of alternatives, and branch mobility.

The constitutional limit remains:

> **Credibility signal ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ ethics ≠ truth ≠ sovereignty.**

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/koali-protocolization.md`

# Konnaxion

Konnaxion creates **associative, communicative, discovery, and coordination puissance** by making people, projects, knowledge, deliberation, and actions visible and connectable.

Power relations may arise through moderation, ranking, recommendation, search, routing, identity, access controls, default feeds, and institutional recognition.

Because discoverability is governance, Konnaxion should be analyzed as a pre-political mediation infrastructure, not merely a social interface.

# Kristal

Kristal creates **epistemic and mnemonic puissance** by structuring claims, provenance, status, versioning, lineage, validation, and reusable artifacts.

Potential power relations arise around authority registries, trust roots, validation thresholds, canonical/reference promotion, reader policies, release/version selection, and federation/import rules.

Plurality is constitutionally important: competing or complementary Kristals can preserve different evidence, interpretations, scopes, or policies. The system should make lineage and differences legible rather than silently declaring one permanent universal truth.

The strongest technical audit still requires direct current specifications for trust roots, policies, registries, signatures, and federation behavior.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/kristal.md`

AUTHORITY: reference
CONTENT_ROLE: knowledge

CONTENT_BYTES: 3931

# Limitations and Open Questions

Power Dynamics is deliberately ambitious, but the v6 repository does not claim that every capability, measurement, governance mechanism, or deployment path is complete or final.

## Measurement remains approximate

Competence, ethics, truth, credibility, contribution, legitimacy, and branch quality cannot be perfectly compressed into a score. The canonical rule is:

> **observed evidence → interpretation policy → scoped reading → uncertainty / limitations → governed use**

Therefore `score ≠ person`, `score ≠ competence`, `credibility ≠ truth`, and `dominance ≠ quality`.

## Architecture is not deployment evidence

A concept can be coherent before all implementation surfaces have been audited. The [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) identifies places where the theory should eventually be tied to concrete trust roots, registries, reader policies, RBAC, routing, escalation, signing keys, defaults, moderation rules, update mechanisms, and portability tests.

## Forkability can be formal but ineffective

Open source does not automatically guarantee practical branch mobility. Network effects, trademarks, signing keys, identity systems, discoverability, hosting, data portability, and migration costs can leave alternatives formally permitted but practically weak.

## Successful capability creates new power

Every empowerment mechanism can create a new locus of power. Discoverability, credibility, memory, AI, workflow, federation infrastructure, and brand all need continued constitutional analysis as they scale.

## Scale changes the problem

A mechanism that is harmless or easily contestable in a small community may become infrastructural at large scale. Externalities, lock-in, standard-setting, and coordination dependence can emerge from success rather than malicious intent.

## Branch evolution has trade-offs

Plurality can produce learning, but excessive fragmentation can destroy interoperability and shared memory. The framework therefore seeks **plurality without fragmentation**, not maximal divergence.

## Current direction

The strongest next layer of evidence is technical: show precisely where power is encoded in schemas, defaults, policies, keys, registries, moderation, routing, ranking, signatures, update paths, and exit mechanisms.

> **Canonical repo sources:** `STATUS.md`, `sources/technical-evidence-map.md`, `framework/07-imperfection-and-plurality.md`, `branch-ecology/README.md`, `dynamics/11-externalities-and-scale.md`

---

[Home](Home) · [Power Show](Power-Show) · [Glossary](Glossary)

## Power-routing implementation gap

Several power-sharing mechanisms are architectural intentions whose effective implementation still needs direct evidence. In particular, Koali ownership decoupling requires specifications for plural distributions, portability, interoperability, registries, signing, discovery, and brand/protocol separation.


## Domain breadth increases evidence requirements

The v6 Power Surface is intentionally broad. A conceptual map of political, economic, monetary, semantic, technical, narrative, and other power surfaces is not evidence that every surface is currently implemented or equally mature. Current-state claims still require direct evidence about policies, registries, keys, defaults, workflows, infrastructure, and governance.

## Monetary analysis is partly legacy-derived

The strongest monetary examples in the corpus come from legacy KingCoin proposals. v6 retains them as **LEGACY-PROPOSAL / stress-test material**, not as current constitutional doctrine.

## Koali routing remains a design-direction test

Plural Koali distributions and ownership decoupling remain an architect-confirmed direction whose practical reality depends on portable evidence, explicit policy identity, interoperability, branchability, independent operation, discovery, and brand / protocol separation.

# Media and State: Domain-Specific Power

Media can possess enormous distribution, framing, and synchronization puissance. This can become visibility, agenda, reputation, and framing power at mass scale.

States may possess stronger legal, fiscal, coercive, territorial, or administrative authority.

The correct comparison is domain-specific rather than a one-dimensional “most powerful actor” ranking.

# Mnemonic & Temporal Power

> **Core question:** Who controls what is remembered, what becomes precedent, when resources are released, how long decisions persist, and how stewardship passes across time?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **preserve reasons, evidence, decisions, failures, outcomes, history, lineage, timing, and succession**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Precedent power, memory power, delay power, release power, succession power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `memory → future agenda`
- `precedent → legitimacy`
- `timing → bargaining leverage`
- `retained resources → release optionality`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- provenance and versioning
- alternative histories / competing representations
- explicit succession and stewardship
- preserve temporal optionality without opaque gatekeeping

## kOA touchpoints

- Kristal preserves structured memory and lineage
- Orgo preserves operational traces
- branch lineage preserves institutional evolution
- unpublished resources can function as reservoirs until deliberately released

## Analytical note

Memory is a production factor: preserved experience can lower future coordination cost and increase future puissance.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `domains/README.md`, `README.md`

AUTHORITY: reference

# Monetary, Credit & Liquidity Power

> **Core question:** Who controls the infrastructure and conditions of exchange: issuance, settlement, liquidity, reserves, credit, convertibility, and transaction access?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **issue or route units of exchange, provide liquidity, settle transactions, extend credit, manage reserves, define transaction access**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Issuance power, settlement power, rail power, reserve power, credit power, access / exclusion power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `monetary access → economic opportunity`
- `credit conditions → investment / dependency`
- `settlement rail → market access`
- `monetary infrastructure → political leverage when alternatives are weak`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- keep high-leverage monetary functions separable and scoped
- audit exclusion, dependency, and substitutability
- avoid stacking monetary rails with taxation, sanctions, allocation, and governance without strong counterpower
- treat legacy KingCoin as a stacking stress test, not current doctrine

## kOA touchpoints

- Current Power Dynamics mainly analyzes monetary power as a domain and constitutional risk surface
- legacy KingCoin material demonstrates the consequences of combining issuance, settlement, taxation, redistribution, sanctions, and governance

## Analytical note

Monetary power is narrower than economic power but can condition large parts of the economy because exchange infrastructure is highly connective.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `domains/README.md`, `README.md`

# Narrative, Symbolic & Brand Power

> **Core question:** Who can shape memory, identity, affiliation, framing, attention, and legitimacy-by-association?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **make complex systems memorable, transmissible, teachable, affiliative, and publicly legible**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Framing power, mobilization power, symbolic legitimacy, brand gatekeeping, affiliation power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `narrative → attention`
- `attention → affiliation`
- `brand → revenue`
- `symbolic recognition → access / mobilization`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- narrative self-binding through explicit ethics
- self-critique and refusal to sanctify the persona
- brand stewardship separated from protocol governance
- brand ≠ truth and narrative ≠ ultimate validity

## kOA touchpoints

- King Klown / fiction act as transmission mechanisms
- the source corpus publishes ethical limits inside the narrative channel itself
- official brand scarcity can coexist with a forkable protocol commons

## Analytical note

Narrative is legitimate as function, not sovereign. Its role is to translate and transmit the architecture, not become the ultimate source of validity.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

# Network & Affiliation Power

> **Core question:** Who can connect, broker, recruit, include, exclude, mobilize, or confer value through association?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **find collaborators, institutions, audiences, expertise, projects, opportunities, and communities**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Brokerage power, inclusion / exclusion power, access power, affiliation power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `network → collaborators`
- `affiliation → trust / attention`
- `relationships → execution capacity`
- `network → opportunity / funding`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- plural networks and multiple entry paths
- portable identity / relationships where feasible
- avoid one mandatory affiliation graph
- separate brand affiliation from truth or political standing

## kOA touchpoints

- Konnaxion connects people, projects, knowledge, and institutions
- Koali can make relevant expertise findable
- brand / narrative can create affiliation without owning the protocol

## Analytical note

Networks generate compounding returns: collaborators create results, results create reputation, and reputation can widen the network.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `domains/README.md`, `README.md`

# Non-Domination and Non-Terminality

Non-domination means more than absence of current interference. A relationship is problematic when an actor remains exposed to discretionary power it cannot meaningfully verify, contest, substitute, or exit.

The repository extends this into **non-terminality**: capture of one institution, branch, deployment, or maintainer should not automatically make the broader ecosystem unrecoverable.

`Instance Capture ≠ System Capture` is therefore a design objective, not a guarantee.

> **Canonical repo source:** `constitution/non-domination-terminality.md`

# Orgo

Orgo creates **operational and administrative puissance** by transforming signals and decisions into cases, tasks, owners, routing, escalation, time windows, closure, and operational memory.

Administrative power appears because control over workflow can alter timing, workload, access, escalation, completion, and practical implementation.

Key audit questions: who creates/closes cases, who assigns roles, who controls escalation, what deadlines/defaults exist, how conflicts are appealed, and whether administration can silently redefine the underlying mandate.

> **Canonical repo source:** `koa/orgo.md`

# Political & Constitutional Power

> **Core question:** Who can shape standing, agenda, representation, mandate, decision rights, amendment, veto, legitimacy, and collective self-government?

## Generated puissance

Effective capability to **deliberate, compare proposals, authorize, decide, amend, govern, coordinate public standing**.

## Relational power that can emerge

Agenda power, mandate power, veto power, legitimacy power, representation power, decision power.

## Typical conversion paths

- `meaning → agenda`
- `credibility → political influence`
- `economic resources → campaign / infrastructure leverage`
- `political mandate → administrative execution`

## Routing / counterpower direction

- political equality as baseline
- expertise as bounded advice rather than sovereign right
- scoped authority and explicit mandates
- verification, contestation, substitution, exit where applicable

## kOA touchpoints

- SemantiK / Kristal shape what becomes intelligible and evidentially stable
- Konnaxion affects discoverability and agenda formation
- Koali / EkoH make competence and contribution legible
- SmartVote exposes baseline and advisory readings
- Orgo connects decisions to execution
- memory makes decisions and reasons reusable
- branch ecology allows institutional alternatives to emerge

## Analytical note

Political power begins before the vote and continues after it. Formal equality at the final ballot does not eliminate upstream asymmetries in meaning, visibility, credibility, or agenda.

This is an analytical domain profile, not a claim that every listed mechanism is fully implemented. Use [Technical Evidence Map](Technical-Evidence-Map) when moving from architecture to current-state implementation claims.

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `domains/README.md`, `README.md`

# Political, Economic, and Monetary Power

These domains interact strongly but should not be collapsed.

## Political power

Political power concerns who can shape:

- political standing;
- agenda;
- deliberation;
- decision rights;
- representation;
- mandates;
- amendment;
- vetoes;
- legitimacy;
- allocation decisions;
- execution of public decisions.

kOA touches political power across the full chain:

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

This means a system can preserve formal equality at the ballot and still create major political asymmetries upstream.

### kOA mechanisms

- SemantiK / Kristal: what becomes intelligible and evidentially stable;
- Konnaxion: what becomes discoverable and discussable;
- Koali / EkoH: which competence or contribution becomes legible;
- SmartVote: how alternative readings of a decision become visible;
- Orgo: how a decision becomes implementation;
- memory: what becomes precedent;
- branch ecology: how institutional alternatives emerge.

## Economic power

Economic power concerns control or mobilization of scarce resources:

- money;
- property;
- productive assets;
- labor and time;
- infrastructure;
- funding;
- procurement;
- contracts;
- philanthropy;
- access to markets.

Money is a resource stock. When mobilizable, it becomes economic puissance. When used to alter another actor's options, bargaining position, access, or allocation, it becomes relational economic power.

```text
money
→ purchasing / funding / bargaining puissance
→ access / allocation / dependency effects
```

### kOA mechanisms

- Zero-Skim: common resource pooling and democratic allocation;
- brand / merchandise: symbolic → economic conversion;
- credibility: opportunity and funding access;
- networks: collaborators, customers, institutions, opportunities;
- execution: turning funding into actual capability;
- compounding: returns become future assets.

## Monetary power

Monetary power concerns the infrastructure and rules of exchange itself:

- issuance;
- unit of account;
- liquidity;
- settlement;
- payment rails;
- reserves;
- convertibility;
- credit and debt conditions;
- access to transaction systems.

This domain can create exceptional leverage because nearly every economic actor depends on exchange infrastructure.

### Legacy KingCoin as stress test

The older KingCoin material proposes highly centralized functions including monetary coordination, taxation, redistribution, sanctions, settlement, and public investment. In the current repository these are **LEGACY-PROPOSAL** material.

Their analytical value is to show the danger of:

```text
monetary authority
+ taxation
+ allocation
+ sanctions
+ governance
+ transaction infrastructure
= extreme power stacking
```

The evolution of Power Dynamics moves away from “design a sufficiently just center” toward:

> **Design powerful functions that do not become terminal arbitrary authority.**

## Conversion firewalls between the three domains

| Conversion | Status |
|---|---|
| money → funding | desirable |
| funding → infrastructure | desirable |
| infrastructure → broader capability | desirable |
| money → proportional political standing | blocked by principle |
| credibility → informed advice | desirable |
| credibility → sovereign decision right | blocked by principle |
| monetary infrastructure → arbitrary exclusion | constitutional risk |
| administrative execution → mandate redefinition | blocked by principle |

## The key question

> **How can material and monetary capability increase collective agency without converting wealth, rails, or administration into unaccountable political sovereignty?**

That is a core Power Dynamics question, not a side topic.


## Domain profiles

- [Political & Constitutional Power](Political-and-Constitutional-Power)
- [Economic & Allocation Power](Economic-and-Allocation-Power)
- [Monetary, Credit & Liquidity Power](Monetary-Credit-and-Liquidity-Power)
- [Cross-Domain Power Conversion](Cross-Domain-Power-Conversion)

> **Canonical v6 repo source:** `showcase/13-political-economic-monetary-power.md`

# Power Analysis Method

## v6 entry point: audit the power surface first

Before analyzing a single relation, inventory the domains the system touches, the controllers on each surface, cross-domain conversions, and routing destinations. Use [Power Surface Audit](Power-Surface-Audit) as the front-end instrument.


Use this sequence for any actor, institution, subsystem, or branch.

1. **Problem / baseline** — what incapacity, dependency, or coordination problem exists?
2. **Resources** — what stocks and assets are available?
3. **Potential** — what possibilities do they create?
4. **Capacity** — what is organized enough to be used?
5. **Puissance** — what can actually be mobilized now?
6. **Mobilization** — reservoir, release, throughput, channels, mediation, amplification.
7. **Power relations** — whose possibilities change, and how?
8. **Returns** — what assets are created and who receives them?
9. **Conversion pathways** — what can become what?
10. **Stacking / concentration** — what accumulates in one node?
11. **Dependency** — what becomes hard to substitute or exit?
12. **Counterpowers** — verification, contestation, substitution, exit, fork, branch mobility.
13. **Temporal analysis** — compounding, decay, succession, lock-in.
14. **Constitutional requirements** — what should be scoped, separated, portable, or reversible?

> **Canonical repo source:** `assessment/analysis-method.md`

# Power Balance Sheet

A power balance sheet maps accumulated assets and dependencies without pretending they have one common unit.

### Assets / stocks
financial, technical, epistemic, human, network, credibility, brand, institutional, operational, mnemonic, optionality.

### Dependencies / liabilities
single providers, single maintainers, remote services, legal permissions, proprietary formats, recognition monopolies, key-person knowledge, network effects, update/signing keys, funding concentration.

The purpose is to expose conversion pathways and terminal dependencies, not to produce a net “power score.”

> **Canonical repo source:** `assessment/power-balance-sheet.md`

# Power Constitution

The constitution exists **because the Power Show succeeds**.

The objective is not to make kOA weak. It is to make strong, cross-domain capability compatible with scoped, contestable, non-terminal authority.

> **Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.**

## Why v6 raises the constitutional stakes

The [Power Surface](Power-Surface) makes visible that kOA can touch many consequential domains at once:

```text
semantic → epistemic → credibility → attention → political
        economic → infrastructure → administrative
                monetary → economic → political
        narrative → affiliation → funding → adoption
```

The danger is therefore not only concentration inside one module. It is **cross-domain conversion and stacking**.

## Constitutional responsibilities

A power constitution governs how useful capability may be:

- generated;
- composed;
- converted;
- routed;
- concentrated;
- challenged;
- revoked;
- transferred;
- substituted;
- exited;
- forked;
- replaced.

## Constitutional core

- **political, economic, and monetary domains remain analytically distinct**;
- **power-conversion firewalls** prevent automatic authority inheritance;
- **power routing** redirects capability, returns, and optionality outward where feasible;
- **Verification + Contestation + Substitution + Exit** remain foundational counterpowers;
- authority is scoped by domain, mandate, duration, conditions, and review;
- functional centrality need not become total sovereignty;
- strong hubs are compatible with distributed capability when the whole remains independently reproducible;
- branch mobility prevents current dominance from becoming final authority.

## Conversion firewalls

```text
expertise → advice                  ✓
expertise → sovereignty             ✗

money → funding                     ✓
money → proportional political rule✗

brand → attention                   ✓
brand → truth                       ✗

infrastructure → capability         ✓
infrastructure → captivity          ✗

execution → implement mandate       ✓
execution → redefine mandate        ✗
```

## Route before merely restraining

The v6 constitution is not only a brake applied after concentration.

> **Generate → Route → Scope → Bind → Verify → Contest → Substitute → Exit → Fork → Evolve**

Public ethical self-binding, protocolization, Koali plurality, advice without sovereignty, portability, self-hosting, democratic allocation, and branch mobility are therefore constitutional mechanisms.

Read [Power Routing & Sharing](Power-Routing-and-Sharing), [Power Conversion Firewalls](Power-Conversion-Firewalls), and [Non-Domination & Terminality](Non-Domination-and-Terminality).

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `PRINCIPLES.md`, `POWER-ROUTING.md`, `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `constitution/README.md` as described in `TREE.md`.

# Power Conversion and Routing Map

This page combines the two v6 questions:

> **How does power move across domains?**  
> **Where is the resulting capability, authority, return, and optionality routed?**

## Master pattern

```text
SOURCE DOMAIN
    ↓
resource / capacity / puissance
    ↓
CONVERSION MECHANISM
    ↓
DESTINATION DOMAIN
    ↓
relational leverage
    ↓
ROUTING / FIREWALL / COUNTERPOWER
```

## Canonical examples

| Source | Conversion | Destination leverage | Routing / firewall |
|---|---|---|---|
| knowledge | evidence of competence | credibility / standing | domain-bounded signals; contestation |
| credibility | ranking / endorsement | visibility / opportunity | plural models; credibility ≠ truth |
| money | funding / purchase | infrastructure / execution | money ≠ political standing |
| monetary rail | settlement / access | economic dependency | separability, substitution, constitutional scrutiny |
| brand | attention / affiliation | revenue / adoption | brand stewardship ≠ protocol sovereignty |
| technical administration | defaults / updates | dependency / de facto policy | scoped mandate, audit, self-hosting |
| expertise | advisory reading | informed judgment | advice ≠ sovereign decision right |
| founder knowledge | protocolization | independent operating capability | documentation, tests, plural maintainers |
| narrative attention | public ethical commitments | self-binding / criticizability | function, not sovereign |
| dominant branch | fork / demonstration | alternative institutional support | branch mobility and interoperability |

## Four routing destinations

Every conversion should be audited for four separate outcomes:

```text
CAPABILITY
AUTHORITY
RETURNS
OPTIONALITY
```

A system can distribute capability while centralizing authority. It can decentralize operation while centralizing returns. It can provide formal exit while preserving practical dependency. These are different outcomes and should be measured separately.

## Capture warning

> **Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power**

Capture becomes more likely when conversion is hidden, cross-mandate, sticky, compounding, and difficult to contest or substitute.

## Constitutional target

> **Composition without automatic conversion.**  
> **Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.**

See [Power Surface](Power-Surface), [Cross-Domain Power Conversion](Cross-Domain-Power-Conversion), [Power Routing & Sharing](Power-Routing-and-Sharing), and [Power Conversion Firewalls](Power-Conversion-Firewalls).

# Power-Conversion Firewalls

A conversion firewall permits useful composition while blocking automatic cross-domain authority.

Canonical examples:

- `Expertise → Advice`, not `Expertise → Sovereignty`.
- `Money → Funding`, not `Money → More Votes`.
- `Brand → Attention`, not `Brand → Truth`.
- `Technical Administration → Maintenance`, not `Administration → Political Outcome Control`.
- `Credibility → Discoverability`, not `Credibility → Unquestionable Authority`.

Firewalls should be auditable and testable at implementation level, not only stated as principles.

> **Canonical repo source:** `constitution/conversion-firewalls.md`

# Power Domains

Domains answer **where** resources, capability, puissance, and relational power operate.

v6 makes one distinction especially explicit:

> **Political power ≠ Economic power ≠ Monetary power**

They can convert into one another, but they involve different resources, mechanisms, mandates, risks, and counterpowers.

## Domain map

| Domain | Core question | Profile |
|---|---|---|
| Political / constitutional | Who can decide, bind, represent, amend, veto, or define legitimate public standing? | [Political & Constitutional](Political-and-Constitutional-Power) |
| Economic / allocation | Who can mobilize scarce material resources and decide where they go? | [Economic & Allocation](Economic-and-Allocation-Power) |
| Monetary / credit / liquidity | Who controls exchange rails, issuance, settlement, reserves, liquidity, or credit conditions? | [Monetary, Credit & Liquidity](Monetary-Credit-and-Liquidity-Power) |
| Epistemic / semantic | Who can shape what is knowable, valid, categorized, translated, or admissible? | [Epistemic & Semantic](Epistemic-and-Semantic-Power) |
| Information / attention / media | Who can make things visible, discoverable, amplified, ranked, or ignored? | [Information, Attention & Media](Information-Attention-and-Media-Power) |
| Credibility / reputation | Who can shape standing, trust signals, certification, or opportunity? | [Credibility & Reputation](Credibility-and-Reputation-Power) |
| Network / affiliation | Who can broker access, recruit, include, exclude, connect, or mobilize relationships? | [Network & Affiliation](Network-and-Affiliation-Power) |
| Administrative / operational | Who can route, assign, delay, approve, escalate, execute, or close? | [Administrative & Operational](Administrative-and-Operational-Power) |
| Technical / infrastructural | Who can build, update, host, sign, standardize, interoperate, or withdraw infrastructure? | [Technical & Infrastructural](Technical-and-Infrastructural-Power) |
| Narrative / symbolic / brand | Who can shape memory, affiliation, framing, identity, or symbolic legitimacy? | [Narrative, Symbolic & Brand](Narrative-Symbolic-and-Brand-Power) |
| Cognitive / AI | Who or what can shape reasoning, synthesis, modeling, and automated interpretation? | [Cognitive & AI](Cognitive-and-AI-Power) |
| Mnemonic / temporal | Who controls memory, precedent, timing, release, delay, expiry, or succession? | [Mnemonic & Temporal](Mnemonic-and-Temporal-Power) |
| Evolutionary / counterpower | Who can contest, substitute, fork, recombine, and replace? | [Evolutionary & Counterpower](Evolutionary-and-Counterpower) |
| Property / territorial | Who controls scarce physical or legal assets and the right to exclude? | [Property & Territorial](Property-and-Territorial-Power) |

## Domain rule

Avoid claims like “X is more powerful than Y” unless the analysis specifies:

> **powerful in what domain, through what resource, capacity, relation, mechanism, duration, conversion path, and counterpower environment?**

A media organization may dominate attention while a state dominates legal enforcement. A payment rail may have monetary leverage without formal political sovereignty. A technical maintainer may have infrastructural leverage without owning the underlying knowledge.

## Cross-domain analysis

The most important questions often concern transitions:

```text
knowledge → credibility
credibility → visibility
visibility → agenda
money → infrastructure
infrastructure → dependency
brand → affiliation
administration → de facto policy
```

See [Cross-Domain Power Conversion](Cross-Domain-Power-Conversion) and the [Power Surface](Power-Surface).

> **Canonical v6 repo sources:** `domains/README.md`, `POWER-SURFACE.md`, `README.md`

# Power Ecology

## v6: ecology across domains

The ecology is now made explicit through the [Power Surface](Power-Surface): political, economic, monetary, semantic, technical, narrative, administrative, and other domains can convert into one another. The ecology must therefore be analyzed as a network of **cross-domain conversion paths**, not only as a list of actors.


A **power ecology** is a system in which resources, forms of puissance, relations of power, returns, dependencies, and alternatives continuously interact.

The kOA Power Show is best understood ecologically because no single capability explains the whole effect.

## Capability ecology

Typical capability-generating chains include:

`knowledge → understanding → judgment → action`

`network → collaborators → coordination → collective puissance`

`memory → lower relearning cost → better future judgment`

`AI + provenance + human governance → higher cognitive throughput`

`portable infrastructure → substitution → stronger exit capacity`

## Conversion ecology

Typical cross-domain chains include:

`knowledge → credibility → visibility → opportunity`

`execution → results → evidence → credibility`

`brand → attention → affiliation → revenue`

`money → infrastructure → reach → influence`

`technical control → defaults → dependency → agenda leverage`

These chains can be productive, extractive, regenerative, or dangerous depending on who receives returns and whether conversions remain scoped.

## Feedback ecology

Power Dynamics therefore asks:

- what resources are accumulated;
- what puissance they support;
- how capabilities compose;
- how they are mobilized;
- what power relations emerge;
- what effects and returns are produced;
- where returns accumulate;
- which nodes become bottlenecks;
- what dependencies form;
- which counterpowers remain effective;
- whether alternatives can survive and evolve.

## kOA as a designed ecology

kOA deliberately distributes capability across semantic, epistemic, social, decision, operational, mnemonic, technical, and federative layers. The design challenge is to obtain the **composition gain** without allowing one layer to silently convert its useful function into total cross-domain authority.

The constitutional aspiration is therefore:

> **Maximum composable capability; minimum terminal sovereignty.**

> **Canonical repo source:** `framework/04-power-ecology.md`

# Power Relations

Power Relations begins where the Power Show becomes socially consequential: when effective capability changes access, standing, visibility, allocation, authority, dependency, or another actor’s ability to act.

The Power Show maps **what actors become able to do**. This directory maps the next question:

> **Whose possibilities change because that capability exists or is controlled?**

Power is relational: mobilized puissance becomes power when it changes another actor's effective possibilities.

## Enabling and coordination power

Relations that expand possibilities by granting access, recognition, coordination, legal standing, interoperability, or infrastructure use. Even enabling relations can become dependency when one actor becomes the only viable gateway.

## Access, gatekeeping, and allocation power

Control over entry conditions and distribution of scarce resources, opportunities, credentials, services, funding, identity, or platform access.

## Visibility, ranking, agenda, and framing power

Pre-political relations that shape what becomes seen, prominent, discussable, interpretable, fundable, reusable, or adoptable.

## Recognition, standing, and certification power

Relations that affect who is treated as competent, trustworthy, qualified, legitimate, or institutionally relevant.

## Semantic, constitutive, and standard-setting power

Definitions, classifications, ontologies, thresholds, translations, formats, trust roots, interoperability contracts, and admissibility rules.

## Authority, administration, and enforcement power

Mandates, routing, assignment, timing, escalation, closure, sanctions, compulsion, and denial of consequences.

## Dependency, bottleneck, lock-in, and terminal power

Relational leverage created when alternatives are costly, slow, non-portable, unrecognized, or unavailable. Terminal power is the limiting case where capture or failure cannot be effectively verified, contested, substituted, reversed, or exited.

## Veto, blocking, and delay power

Preventing, slowing, queuing, withholding approval, or increasing procedural cost. Time is a political variable.

Power relations are not automatically illegitimate. Assessment depends on mandate, scope, proportionality, transparency, alternatives, dependency, counterpower, and reversibility.

See [Component Power Matrix](Component-Power-Matrix) for how these relations emerge around kOA components.

> **Canonical repo source:** `relations/README.md`

# Power Routing, Sharing, and Devolution

Power Dynamics does not stop at **how much power kOA can generate**.

It asks:

> **Where does the capability, leverage, authority, return, and optionality created by kOA actually go?**

A system can empower users while still making its operator indispensable. Power routing therefore examines four destinations separately:

```text
CAPABILITY → who can do more?
AUTHORITY  → who can decide or control more?
RETURNS    → who receives reusable value?
OPTIONALITY→ who can refuse, leave, reproduce, fork, or wait?
```

## Core routing mechanisms

| Source of leverage | Concentrating default | Routing / sharing mechanism | Intended destination |
|---|---|---|---|
| Founder knowledge | permanent founder dependency | documentation, schemas, protocolization, tests | maintainers, branches, independent operators |
| Narrative attention | founder/persona centrality | explicit ethics, self-critique, attribution, anti-domination | public principles, procedures, critical readers |
| Brand | trademark becomes protocol sovereignty | brand stewardship separated from protocol | protocol commons + bounded official brand |
| Koali credibility infrastructure | one owner defines standing | plural distributions, portable evidence, explicit policy identity | communities, domains, alternative operators |
| Expertise | expert becomes ruler | baseline + bounded advisory readings | informed public judgment |
| Knowledge validation | one owner of truth | provenance, reader policies, competing Kristals | plural epistemic capability |
| Infrastructure | operator becomes unavoidable | self-hosting, capsules, federation, rollback, substitution | local operators / communities |
| Money | wealth buys rule | one-member-one-vote / allocation firewalls | common funding without pay-to-rule |
| Workflow control | administrator becomes policymaker | scoped mandate, audit, appeal, revocability | execution without mandate capture |
| AI capability | model becomes hidden sovereign | explicit role boundaries, provenance, human authority | cognitive amplification without institutional sovereignty |
| Dominant branch | incumbency becomes final | branch mobility, lineage, recombination | viable alternatives |

## Ethical fiction as power routing

The fiction is not only a vehicle for symbolic puissance. It also publishes limits on that puissance.

The source corpus includes explicit commitments around:

- clarity of intention;
- respect for living beings;
- vigilance against monopoly, domination, and manipulation;
- recognition of contributors and sharing merit;
- self-mockery and refusal to sanctify the Klown;
- revision, criticism, adaptation, and transmission rather than dogma.

Power Dynamics calls this **narrative self-binding**.

```text
symbolic power
→ public ethical commitments
→ stable grounds for criticism
→ reduced freedom for arbitrary reinterpretation
```

Self-binding is not proof of compliance. It makes the exercise of power **more legible and criticizable**.

## Koali: from property to protocol ecology

Current architect-confirmed design direction:

> **Koali should not remain a terminal personal credibility authority. Its useful capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and operable through plural distributions around interoperable structures.**

The desired transition is:

```text
founder-created capability
→ explicit kernel / contracts
→ portable evidence and history
→ explicit policy identity
→ multiple distributions
→ interoperability
→ independent operation
→ branch mobility
```

This is not founder erasure. It is **ownership decoupling**: the useful function can outgrow exclusive ownership of the first implementation.

## Power sharing is not flatness

The goal is not to make everyone equally expert, equally responsible, or equally authoritative.

The goal is:

> **Useful asymmetry without terminal dependency.**

A maintainer may have more technical authority within a defined scope. An expert may have more epistemic relevance in a defined domain. A coordinator may have routing authority for a workflow. None of those roles should silently acquire unrelated sovereignty.

## Routing and conversion firewalls

Routing is strongest when paired with conversion limits:

```text
expertise → advice                  ✓
expertise → sovereign vote weight   ✗

money → funding                     ✓
money → political standing          ✗

brand → attention                   ✓
brand → truth                       ✗

infrastructure → capability         ✓
infrastructure → captivity          ✗

execution → implement mandate       ✓
execution → redefine mandate        ✗
```

## Canonical routing test

For every important kOA mechanism:

1. What puissance does it create?
2. Where would that puissance naturally concentrate?
3. What relational power does that concentration create?
4. What capability can be routed outward instead?
5. What authority must remain scoped?
6. Who receives the returns?
7. Who receives exit / substitution / fork optionality?
8. What evidence proves that the routing is practical rather than merely formal?

See [Power Routing Audit](Power-Routing-Audit).

# Power Routing Audit

After identifying what puissance a system creates, ask **where it goes**.

For each major capability, record:

```text
Generated puissance:
Concentration vector:
Routing mechanism:
Primary recipients:
Retained central rights:
Transferred / shared rights:
Return allocation:
Exit / substitution path:
Failure mode:
Evidence needed:
```

Audit six destinations:

1. **Capability** — who can actually do more?
2. **Authority** — who gains decision, validation, ranking, update, or veto rights?
3. **Returns** — who receives money, knowledge, reputation, network, code, data, and memory?
4. **Optionality** — who can leave, self-host, fork, wait, or substitute?
5. **Interpretation** — who controls categories, credibility policies, semantic mappings, and defaults?
6. **Visibility** — who controls discoverability, ranking, attention, and agenda?

Then test self-binding, protocolization, ownership decoupling, and effective exit.

> **Canonical repo source:** `assessment/power-routing-audit.md`

# The kOA Power Show

The first purpose of this repository is simple:

> **Show all the ways kOA can make people, groups, organizations, institutions, and federations more capable.**

Then ask what happens when those capabilities become socially consequential.

## The one-paragraph answer

kOA is immensely powerful because it can connect **meaning, knowledge, AI, discovery, credibility, networks, deliberation, political choice, economic allocation, coordination, execution, memory, infrastructure, federation, and institutional evolution** inside a recursive loop. The result is not merely more information. It is the possibility of turning distributed resources into collective agency, preserving the returns of action, reinvesting them, reproducing the capability elsewhere, and routing important forms of leverage away from terminal ownership.

> **Breadth × Composition × Closure × Memory × Compounding × Replication × Routing**

## 1. Breadth — many forms of puissance

kOA can generate or amplify:

- semantic puissance;
- epistemic puissance;
- cognitive / AI-mediated puissance;
- information and distribution puissance;
- attention / discoverability puissance;
- credibility / reputation puissance;
- network and affiliation puissance;
- collective puissance;
- political / deliberative / decision puissance;
- economic and allocation puissance;
- monetary / liquidity capability where monetary infrastructure exists;
- administrative and operational puissance;
- technical and infrastructural puissance;
- narrative / symbolic / brand puissance;
- mnemonic and temporal puissance;
- federation, exit, substitution, fork, and evolutionary puissance.

See [Power Surface](Power-Surface).

## 2. Composition — the whole can exceed the sum

```text
SemantiK / SenTient  → meaning
Kristal              → knowledge + provenance + memory
Konnaxion            → discovery + network + circulation
Koali / EkoH         → credibility legibility
SmartVote            → comparative judgment + decision support
Orgo                 → operational execution
AI                    → cognitive amplification
Capsules / federation→ replication + substitution
Brand / narrative    → transmission + affiliation
Economic rails       → funding + material capability

                  ↓ compose ↓

      coordinated collective puissance
```

A capable population is not automatically a capable collective. Composition creates the ability to use distributed capabilities **together**.

## 3. Closure — knowledge reaches action

> **Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better**

Many systems stop at one stage. kOA attempts to connect the stages.

```text
knowledge
→ judgment
→ decision
→ governed work
→ execution
→ result
→ memory
```

This turns intelligence into agency.

## 4. Memory — the past remains usable

kOA can preserve:

- evidence;
- provenance;
- definitions;
- decisions;
- reasons;
- workflows;
- failures;
- outcomes;
- contribution history;
- branch lineage.

Without memory, a collective repeatedly pays the cost of rediscovery. With memory, past action becomes infrastructure for future action.

## 5. Compounding — action creates future capability

```text
Puissance
→ Action
→ Result
→ Return
→ Asset
→ Reinvestment
→ Greater Future Puissance
```

Returns can include knowledge, code, tools, data, credibility, network, reputation, money, infrastructure, access, and optionality.

This is the **compounding engine**.

## 6. Cross-domain conversion — one capability unlocks another

```text
knowledge → credibility → visibility → opportunity
money → infrastructure → reach → influence
brand → affiliation → revenue → development
network → collaborators → execution → results
memory → lower coordination cost → faster action
```

Conversion is one reason kOA can become extremely powerful. It is also one reason it can become dangerous.

> **Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power**

See [Cross-Domain Power Conversion](Cross-Domain-Power-Conversion).

## 7. Political power — before, during, and after the vote

kOA touches:

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

That means political power is not confined to SmartVote or formal governance. Semantic definitions, search, ranking, credibility, workflow, and memory can all shape political possibility.

## 8. Economic power — resources become action

kOA can help convert:

```text
money / property / funding / labor / infrastructure
→ usable economic capacity
→ projects / services / coordination / production
```

It can also route economic resources through democratic allocation mechanisms rather than allowing wealth to automatically buy more political standing.

## 9. Monetary power — control of exchange rails

Monetary power concerns issuance, liquidity, settlement, payment rails, reserves, credit, debt conditions, and transaction access.

The legacy KingCoin material shows why this domain is so constitutionally sensitive: monetary control can stack with taxation, allocation, sanctions, and administration into highly terminal power.

The evolved doctrine is therefore not “centralize monetary power well,” but:

> **If powerful monetary functions exist, keep their authority scoped, contestable, separable, and substitutable.**

## 10. Scaling — individual to federation

```text
individual
→ team
→ organization
→ institution
→ federation
```

At each scale, kOA can add shared knowledge, coordination, decision, execution, memory, and infrastructure.

The design target is:

> **Capability can scale without sovereignty scaling at the same rate.**

## 11. Replication — capability can leave the center

Self-hosting, capsules, portability, federation, and branchability can transform:

```text
use a central service
```

into:

```text
possess / reproduce / operate the function locally
```

That is a major transfer of effective possibility.

## 12. Power routing — kOA can equip others instead of only itself

The project includes mechanisms intended to redirect the power it creates:

```text
expertise → advice, not sovereignty
money → funding, not more votes
narrative → mobilization + ethical self-binding
Koali → plural distributions, not terminal personal ownership
Kristal → portable knowledge, not one owner of truth
infrastructure → self-hosting / federation, not captivity
dominance → branch mobility, not final authority
```

This is why **power sharing** is part of the Power Show, not merely a later defensive layer.

## 13. The constitutional paradox

The stronger the Power Show becomes, the more necessary constitutional design becomes.

```text
more knowledge capability
→ more validation power

more discovery capability
→ more agenda power

more credibility capability
→ more standing power

more workflow capability
→ more administrative power

more infrastructure capability
→ more dependency power
```

Therefore:

> **The constitution exists because the Power Show succeeds.**

## 14. The mature doctrine

> **Generate immense puissance.**  
> **Compose it.**  
> **Close the loop.**  
> **Preserve the returns.**  
> **Compound future capability.**  
> **Make cross-domain conversions visible.**  
> **Route capability outward.**  
> **Block automatic conversion into unrelated sovereignty.**  
> **Preserve verification, contestation, substitution, exit, and forkability.**  
> **Allow the whole system to evolve.**

In compressed form:

> **Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.**

# Power Surface Audit

Use this audit to answer:

> **What kinds of power does this system generate, where can they convert, and where are they routed?**

## 1. Domain inventory

For each domain, record:

| Domain | Resources | Capacity | Puissance | Relational power | Main controllers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Political | | | | | |
| Economic | | | | | |
| Monetary | | | | | |
| Epistemic | | | | | |
| Semantic | | | | | |
| Information / attention | | | | | |
| Credibility | | | | | |
| Network | | | | | |
| Administrative | | | | | |
| Technical / infrastructure | | | | | |
| Narrative / brand | | | | | |
| Cognitive / AI | | | | | |
| Mnemonic / temporal | | | | | |
| Evolutionary / counterpower | | | | | |

## 2. Conversion inventory

For every major conversion:

```text
source domain
→ mechanism
→ destination domain
→ resulting leverage
```

Record whether the conversion is:

- explicit or hidden;
- intended or emergent;
- reversible or sticky;
- scoped or cross-mandate;
- contestable or terminal.

## 3. Stacking test

Which actor or node controls multiple high-leverage surfaces?

Look especially for combinations of:

- identity;
- credibility;
- discoverability;
- allocation;
- workflow;
- infrastructure;
- signing / updates;
- brand / certification;
- monetary rails.

## 4. Routing test

For each major capability, identify who receives:

- **capability**;
- **authority**;
- **returns**;
- **optionality**.

## 5. Counterpower test

Can affected actors effectively:

- verify?
- contest?
- substitute?
- exit?
- self-host?
- fork?
- preserve identity / history / evidence?
- gather support for an alternative?

## 6. Terminality test

Ask:

> If this controller fails, captures the function, changes policy arbitrarily, or disappears, can the ecosystem continue through another viable path?

If not, the surface may be **terminal power**.

## 7. Output

A complete audit should produce:

1. a domain map;
2. a conversion graph;
3. a stacking map;
4. a routing map;
5. a counterpower map;
6. a terminality assessment;
7. a list of technical evidence still required.


## Related instruments

- [Power Routing Audit](Power-Routing-Audit)
- [Power Analysis Method](Power-Analysis-Method)
- [Power Balance Sheet](Power-Balance-Sheet)
- [Constitutional Tests](Constitutional-Tests)

> **Canonical v6 repo source:** `assessment/power-surface-audit.md`

# The kOA Power Surface

A **power surface** is any function, interface, role, registry, workflow, standard, narrative, resource rail, or infrastructural boundary through which control can materially alter effective possibility.

The kOA Power Show is strongest when it makes the whole surface visible at once.

> **kOA is powerful because it can create capability across many domains and connect those domains into conversion and feedback loops.**

## Master map

| Domain | Generated puissance | Relational power that may emerge | Typical conversion outward | kOA routing / firewall direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Epistemic | validate, compare, preserve, reuse knowledge | validation, canon, trust-root | knowledge → credibility | portable artifacts, provenance, competing Kristals |
| Semantic | structure concepts, categories, translation, ambiguity | classification, framing, admissibility | meaning → agenda / enforcement | versioned semantics, local governance, contestation |
| Cognitive / AI | reasoning, synthesis, modeling, translation | hidden framing, prioritization, dependency | cognition → judgment / execution | AI under explicit governance, bounded authority |
| Information / attention | publish, search, rank, distribute, amplify | visibility, agenda, moderation | attention → affiliation / legitimacy / funding | explicit discovery policies, alternatives, export |
| Credibility / reputation | make evidence of competence/contribution legible | standing, certification, opportunity | credibility → visibility / access | domain-bounded signals, plural models, contestation |
| Network / affiliation | association, brokerage, recruitment, diffusion | inclusion, exclusion, access | network → execution / opportunity | portable identity/relationships where feasible, plural networks |
| Political / constitutional | deliberate, decide, authorize, amend, govern | mandate, veto, legitimacy, decision | political → administrative / allocation | political equality, scoped authority, contestation |
| Economic | mobilize scarce resources, assets, labor, funding | bargaining, ownership, allocation | money → infrastructure / reach | democratic allocation, anti-pay-to-rule firewalls |
| Monetary | liquidity, exchange, payment, credit, settlement | issuance, rail, reserve, access | monetary → economic / political dependency | treat high-stack monetary designs as constitutional risk |
| Administrative / operational | assign, route, schedule, escalate, close | timing, workload, approval, closure | administration → de facto policy | explicit workflows, audit, appeal, revocability |
| Technical / infrastructural | build, deploy, verify, operate, reproduce | compatibility, update, identity, dependency | infrastructure → standards / access | self-hosting, capsules, federation, substitution |
| Narrative / symbolic / brand | memory, attention, affiliation, transmission | framing, legitimacy-by-association, gatekeeping | brand → attention → revenue / adoption | ethical self-binding, brand ≠ truth, protocol separation |
| Mnemonic / temporal | preserve reasons, outcomes, precedent, timing | precedent, delay, succession | memory → future agenda / legitimacy | provenance, versioning, alternative histories |
| Evolutionary / counterpower | fork, substitute, recombine, replace | displacement of incumbents | alternative → support → dominance | branch mobility, lineage, interoperability |

## Why breadth matters

A system that is powerful in one domain can still be constrained by other systems. A system that touches many domains can create much larger **cross-domain leverage**.

For example:

```text
semantic control
→ epistemic interpretation
→ credibility
→ visibility
→ agenda
→ political influence
```

or:

```text
money
→ infrastructure
→ distribution
→ attention
→ affiliation
→ political leverage
```

or:

```text
technical control
→ defaults
→ dependency
→ administrative necessity
→ de facto governance
```

The same breadth that makes kOA capable is therefore the reason it needs explicit constitutional design.

## Political surface

kOA can influence political capacity through:

- issue formation and semantic framing;
- discoverability and agenda formation;
- credibility and advisory signals;
- deliberation and proposal comparison;
- voting / reading mechanisms;
- resource allocation;
- workflow and execution;
- memory and precedent;
- branch evolution and institutional alternatives.

Political power therefore begins **before the vote** and continues **after the vote**.

## Economic surface

Economic puissance includes the ability to mobilize:

- money;
- property;
- productive assets;
- labor and time;
- infrastructure;
- funding;
- contracts;
- procurement;
- philanthropy;
- bargaining position.

kOA can increase economic capability by improving coordination, credibility, discoverability, resource pooling, allocation, execution, and compounding.

## Monetary surface

Monetary power is narrower than economic power. It concerns control over:

- issuance;
- units of account;
- liquidity;
- payment rails;
- settlement;
- reserves;
- credit and debt conditions;
- convertibility;
- transaction access.

A monetary layer can become extraordinarily powerful because it conditions other domains. This is why the legacy KingCoin proposals are useful as a **stacking stress test**, not as current constitutional doctrine.

## The core constitutional question

For every surface, ask:

1. **Who gains capability?**
2. **Who gains authority?**
3. **Who receives returns?**
4. **Who gains optionality?**
5. **What other domain can this convert into?**
6. **What prevents that conversion from becoming arbitrary?**
7. **Can affected actors verify, contest, substitute, exit, or fork?**

The desired architecture is not flatness. It is a **governable ecology of strong capabilities and non-terminal asymmetries**.

# The Pre-Political Chain

Power begins before formal voting or command.

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

kOA touches nearly every step.

## Meaning

Categories determine what can be described, compared, included, or excluded. Semantic infrastructure can create semantic puissance; control over definitions can create semantic power.

## Knowledge

What becomes structured, provenance-bearing, canonical, disputed, or reusable affects later reasoning.

## Visibility

What is discoverable has a higher chance of being cited, funded, reused, adopted, or debated. Discoverability is therefore both capability and governance.

## Credibility

Signals about expertise or contribution can help groups navigate complexity. They can also affect opportunity and standing.

## Agenda

What is surfaced repeatedly becomes more likely to be treated as worthy of collective attention.

## Deliberation and decision

Decision tools can expand comparative capacity. Defaults, lenses, weighting, and presentation can also shape the decision environment.

## Execution

Routing, ownership, time, escalation, and closure determine whether a nominal decision becomes an effective outcome.

## Memory

What is preserved becomes precedent, evidence, training data, institutional learning, and the starting point for future decisions.

## Why this belongs in the Power Show

The impressive capability of kOA is precisely that it links these stages. The governance challenge is that a system spanning these stages could also accumulate pre-political leverage if the same node controls too many transitions.

> **Canonical repo source:** `showcase/06-pre-political-chain.md`

# Principles

Power Dynamics begins from an affirmative premise: **people and collectives should be able to become dramatically more capable**. The constitutional problem is how to preserve that capability without allowing successful functions to harden into arbitrary, cross-domain, or terminal sovereignty.

## Generate immense but usable puissance

1. **Increase effective possibility.** expand what actors can realistically understand, choose, build, coordinate, refuse, preserve, and accomplish.
2. **Turn dispersed resources into mobilizable capability rather than merely accumulating resources.** 
3. **Collective puissance can be generated.** coordinated capability can exceed the sum of isolated capabilities.
4. **Infrastructure should crystallize past effort into reusable future capacity.** 
5. **Knowledge, memory, tools, and institutional learning should become cumulative.** 
6. **Capability should scale from individual to federation without requiring sovereignty to scale at the same rate.** 
7. **Composition does not require ownership.** interoperating functions need not collapse into one owner or authority.

## Keep domains explicit

8. **Political power, economic power, and monetary power are distinct even when they convert into one another.** 
9. **Political power begins before formal decision.** meaning, visibility, credibility, and agenda are political surfaces.
10. **Economic puissance concerns mobilization of scarce resources.** economic power concerns relational effects of that control.
11. **Monetary power concerns the infrastructure and conditions of exchange.** issuance, rails, liquidity, settlement, reserves, credit, and access.
12. **Administrative power can alter outcomes without changing formal policy.** 
13. **Semantic and epistemic power can shape the field in which later political decisions become intelligible.** 
14. **Every domain analysis should name resources, capability, relation, conversion paths, duration, and counterpowers.** 

## Maintain ontological discipline

15. **Resource ≠ Potential ≠ Capacity ≠ Puissance ≠ Power.** 
16. **A resource is not automatically power.** 
17. **Puissance is effective mobilizable capacity under real conditions.** 
18. **Power is relational and domain-specific.** 
19. **Power to / with / through are primarily capability questions.** power over is primarily a relational question.
20. **Stock, reservoir, flow, reach, influence, authority, legitimacy, and sovereignty are not synonyms.** 

## Expose conversion and stacking

21. **Different forms of puissance should remain composable without automatically converting into unrelated authority.** 
22. **Expertise may inform.** it does not automatically govern.
23. **Money may fund.** it does not automatically buy political standing.
24. **Brand may attract attention.** it does not become truth.
25. **Technical administration may maintain infrastructure.** it does not silently inherit political authority.
26. **Credibility may improve discoverability.** it does not become unquestionable legitimacy.
27. **Execution may carry out a mandate.** it does not silently redefine the mandate.
28. **Infrastructure may create capability.** it does not thereby own its users.
29. **Power stacking must be assessed across domains, not function by function.** 
30. **Capture is a conversion problem.** Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power.

## Route and share power deliberately

31. **Power should be routed, not merely constrained after concentration.** 
32. **Generated capability should preferentially become reusable capability for participants and communities where feasible.** 
33. **Public ethical commitments can be a self-binding mechanism.** they do not prove compliance but create stable grounds for criticism.
34. **Narrative power should carry its own limits through explicit anti-domination, attribution, humility, and revision principles.** 
35. **Protocolization is power sharing.** tacit founder mastery should become explicit, testable, documented, and independently reproducible.
36. **Useful functions should be decoupled from exclusive ownership where feasible.** 
37. **Koali is a key ownership-decoupling test.** credibility capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions.
38. **Returns are part of power routing.** who receives knowledge, money, reputation, network, code, memory, and infrastructure is constitutional.
39. **Exit, substitution, self-hosting, and forkability distribute optionality.** 
40. **Sharing capability does not require flatness.** the target is non-terminal asymmetry.
41. **The strongest center is one that can create capability beyond itself.** 

## Make emerging power legible

42. **Architecture is governance before governance is written.** 
43. **Every technical boundary is also a potential political boundary.** 
44. **Every mechanism that distributes capability creates a new locus of power.** 
45. **Dependency is a gradient.** formal choice is not effective choice when switching costs are prohibitive.
46. **Terminal power is a primary constitutional risk.** 
47. **Functional centrality is not automatically domination, but centrality plus dependency plus weak counterpower can become domination.** 
48. **Instance capture must not imply system capture.** 

## Preserve counterpower

49. **Verification + Contestation + Substitution + Exit are foundational counterpowers.** 
50. **Formal exit is insufficient.** exit must be effective.
51. **Formal forkability is insufficient.** branch mobility matters.
52. **Revocability and reversibility are distinct.** 
53. **Strong hubs are compatible with distributed capability when the whole remains independently reproducible.** 
54. **Portability must include the artifacts, history, provenance, evidence, and configuration needed for meaningful continuity.** 

## Preserve epistemic humility

55. **No measure of competence, ethics, credibility, contribution, or legitimacy should be treated as perfectly complete or permanently final.** 
56. **Score ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ ethics ≠ truth.** 
57. **Support is not evidence.** 
58. **Dominance is not truth, quality, or final legitimacy.** 
59. **Uncertainty, disagreement, and competing representations must remain representable.** 
60. **Plurality is useful when it adds evidence, context, method, function, challenge, translation, or synthesis.** 

## Make capability regenerative

61. **Action should leave assets behind.** 
62. **Who receives the returns matters.** 
63. **Returns can compound future puissance.** 
64. **Compounding can also compound concentration and must be audited.** 
65. **Shared infrastructure should convert collective activity into broader future capability where feasible.** 
66. **Memory is a production factor, not merely an archive.** 

## Keep the system evolvable

67. **Imperfection is permanent.** authority need not be.
68. **Variation is normal institutional experimentation, not only an emergency response to capture.** 
69. **Complementarity can be superior to convergence.** 
70. **The stable kernel should protect evolution, not freeze outcomes.** 
71. **A better branch should be able to demonstrate value, gather support, remain interoperable, and influence or replace a dominant branch.** 
72. **Improvement claims should state the dimension, beneficiaries, conditions, evidence, and trade-offs.** 

## kOA constitutional orientation

73. **Strong distributed puissance.** Weak sovereignty.
74. **StrongHub + DistributedCapability + ReplicableWhole.** 
75. **AI assists under governance.** AI does not silently inherit institutional sovereignty.
76. **Semantic sovereignty requires practical control, not merely formal openness.** 
77. **Founder independence is a resilience criterion, not a requirement to erase the founder.** 
78. **Brand stewardship and protocol governance are distinct.** 
79. **The system should remain capable of learning from its own alternatives.** 



---
