# AI and Cognitive Puissance

AI can greatly increase cognitive throughput: reading, synthesis, comparison, generation, translation, semantic processing, and planning.

The repository treats AI as **cognitive puissance under governance**, not as an autonomous institutional sovereign.

Risks include hidden policy through prompts/defaults, opacity through complexity, semantic centralization, remote-service dependence, unreviewable automation, and substitution of model confidence for governed evidence.

AI should assist transformations whose authority remains externally governed and auditable.

# Brand, Narrative, and Affiliation

Brand and narrative create communicative, symbolic, and affiliation puissance. They can compress complex meaning into recognizable symbols, lower transmission cost, mobilize supporters, generate commerce, and make a system culturally legible.

`system value → brand recognition → attention / affiliation → revenue / network → reinvestment`

The constitutional firewall is that brand or narrative does not become truth, expertise, political legitimacy, or protocol sovereignty by itself.


## Narrative self-binding

The corpus also uses fiction to publish explicit ethical constraints: clarity of intention, vigilance against monopoly and domination, recognition of contributors, self-mockery, revision, and non-dogmatic transmission.

This creates a **narrative self-binding** mechanism. The symbolic layer does not only transmit affiliation; it also transmits limits that participants and critics can reuse against symbolic capture.

Public ethics do not replace enforceable governance, but they redirect some narrative power away from unquestionable persona authority and toward explicit principles.

See [`../cases/ethical-fiction-self-binding.md`](../cases/ethical-fiction-self-binding.md).

## Sharing symbolic capital

The narrative ethics do more than limit abuse. They also redirect symbolic returns: recognize contributors, thank allies publicly, share merit, practice self-mockery, reject dogma, document, transmit, and pass the baton.

That means the narrative layer can be designed to **share symbolic capital rather than merely accumulate followers around a persona**. The public-brand distinction between mobilization persona and architect identity reinforces the same direction: the persona functions as a diffusion instrument, not the source of protocol validity.

# Capsules and Federation

Capsules and federation create **infrastructural autonomy puissance**: local deployment, signed packages, verification, rollback, self-hosting, offline operation, and independent continuity.

Their political importance is counterpower. A system is less terminal when communities can continue operating, substitute a deployment, verify artifacts, or form branches without permanent permission from one remote operator.

The technical power audit should inspect signing keys, registries, update channels, compatibility contracts, and recovery procedures.

---
maturity: "CURRENT-CORE"
claim_type: "ANALYTICAL-RECONSTRUCTION"
scope: "GENERAL"
version: "6.1"
title: "Founder Power"
source_basis:
  - S18
  - S25
---
# Founder Power

Founder power can include authorship and vocabulary, repository and infrastructure control, domains and brand, signing/release keys, unique architectural knowledge, relationships and narrative authority, and public-identity optionality.

The question is not whether an early founder has concentrated puissance, but how that dependence changes over time.

`Founder capability → encoded architecture → documentation → distributed mastery → maintainer pluralism → independent reproducibility`


## Founder power can be converted into ecosystem capability

The desired trajectory is not merely to constrain founder power after it exists. It is to **convert founder-specific assets into shared capability**:

> **Founder-powered → Architecture-powered → Protocol-powered → Ecosystem-powered**

Koali duplicability, open specifications, reproducible deployments, branch mobility, and public ethical self-binding are examples of this transition.


## Founder fallibility as a power condition

Long-lived systems should assume that a founder will eventually be wrong about something. Founder resilience therefore has two separate tests:

1. **absence test** — can the system continue without the founder?
2. **opposition test** — can the system reject the founder's proposal while the founder remains influential?

The second test matters because symbolic or reputational authority can survive technical decentralization. A founder-independent protocol with a founder-dependent culture remains partially centralized.

---
maturity: "CURRENT-CORE"

title: "Know → Choose → Act → Remember"
source_basis:
  - S13
  - S25
---
# Know → Choose → Act → Remember

The kOA loop is a capability-regeneration cycle.

### Know
Transform dispersed contributions into structured, provenance-preserving knowledge without prematurely erasing ambiguity.

### Choose
Transform knowledge into legible orientation while preserving reasons, options, disagreement, and multiple readings.

### Act
Transform orientation into responsibilities, tasks, routing, validation, escalation, and outcomes.

### Remember
Transform experience into reusable memory that lowers the cost of future action.

The political value lies in the **transitions**, because each transition can become a bottleneck or locus of power.

source_basis:
  - S15

  - S28
---
# Koali / EkoH

Koali and EkoH address a problem of **latent competence**: people may possess valuable experience or expertise that is difficult for others to discover, verify, or mobilize.

The intended transformation is:

`competence evidence → legibility → credibility signal → discoverability → opportunity → participation`

The constitutional limit is explicit:

> **Score ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ truth ≠ sovereignty.**

Signals should be domain-bounded, evidence-linked, versioned, contestable, and portable. Multiple distributions or policies can coexist around common interoperability, supporting **plurality without fragmentation**.


## Power-sharing direction

A credibility layer becomes politically dangerous if useful recognition is inseparable from one owner, one operator, or one permanent scoring policy.

The current design direction is therefore to make Koali **duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions** rather than a terminal proprietary credibility authority.

See [`koali-protocolization.md`](koali-protocolization.md).

title: "Koali: From Property to Protocol"
source_basis:
  - S15

  - S28
---
# Koali: From Property to Protocol

Koali is a critical test of whether kOA merely creates a new credibility center or **shares the capacity to organize credibility**.

The current architect-confirmed design intention is explicit:

> **Koali should not remain an exclusive personal property on which everyone else must depend. Its useful capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions.**

This design intent is recorded here separately from source-derived implementation claims. A canonical Koali distribution specification should eventually replace this note as the primary technical evidence.

## Why this matters

Credibility infrastructure can influence:

- discoverability;
- standing;
- opportunity;
- certification;
- access;
- trust;
- collaboration;
- advisory influence.

If one actor permanently owns the only meaningful credibility layer, a mechanism designed to recognize contribution can become a new gatekeeper.

## Protocolization target

```text
founder-created concept
→ explicit kernel / contracts
→ portable evidence and history
→ policy identity
→ multiple distributions
→ interoperability
→ independent operation
→ branch comparison and evolution
```

The political transformation is:

> **Personal ownership of a capability → stewardship of an interoperable protocol ecology**

## What can remain distinct

Power sharing does not require one universal Koali.

Different distributions may legitimately use different criteria for different domains or communities, provided that their policies are explicit enough to interpret.

Examples might include academic, engineering, local-community, professional, artistic, or project-specific credibility distributions.

This is the intended meaning of:

> **Plurality without fragmentation.**

## Minimum power-sharing properties

A mature Koali architecture should make the following possible where technically appropriate:

1. **Reimplementation** — another actor can implement the common logic.
2. **Distribution plurality** — multiple operators can run different credibility policies.
3. **Policy legibility** — a score or signal identifies the policy under which it was produced.
4. **Evidence portability** — underlying evidence can be reused without recreating a person's entire history from zero.
5. **Contestability** — signals can be challenged or corrected.
6. **Interoperability** — distributions can exchange or compare structured evidence without pretending they are identical.
7. **Discoverability of alternatives** — one dominant distribution does not become the only socially visible one by default.
8. **Branch mobility** — an improved distribution can gather adoption and become viable.

## Constitutional limit

> **Credibility signal ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ ethics ≠ truth ≠ sovereignty.**

Koali is strongest as infrastructure when it helps other actors make informed judgments while refusing to become the unique owner of social legitimacy.

# Konnaxion

Konnaxion creates **associative, communicative, discovery, and coordination puissance** by making people, projects, knowledge, deliberation, and actions visible and connectable.

Power relations may arise through moderation, ranking, recommendation, search, routing, identity, access controls, default feeds, and institutional recognition.

Because discoverability is governance, Konnaxion should be analyzed as a pre-political mediation infrastructure, not merely a social interface.

source_basis:
  - S13

---
# Kristal

Kristal creates **epistemic and mnemonic puissance** by structuring claims, provenance, status, versioning, lineage, validation, and reusable artifacts.

Potential power relations arise around authority registries, trust roots, validation thresholds, canonical/reference promotion, reader policies, release/version selection, and federation/import rules.

Plurality is constitutionally important: competing or complementary Kristals can preserve different evidence, interpretations, scopes, policies, or even incompatible worldview axioms. The system should make lineage and differences legible rather than silently declaring one permanent universal truth.

This includes three distinct branch types that should not be conflated:

- **reference branches** — maintained as broadly useful encyclopedic or institutional references;
- **worldview branches** — intentionally adapt assumptions or interpretations to a community's beliefs;
- **fictional / experimental branches** — alter world axioms for narrative, simulation, or adversarial testing.

Forkability does not imply equal evidentiary support. A system can permit a branch to exist while still making provenance, contradiction, uncertainty, validation policy, and source independence visible.

The strongest technical audit still requires direct current specifications for trust roots, policies, registries, signatures, and federation behavior.

# Multilingualism, Reach, and Semantic Participation

Multilingualism creates two distinct capacities.

**Reach puissance** expands geographic and linguistic participation.

**Semantic participation puissance** lets communities participate without abandoning their language, categories, and local meaning structures.

Translation is therefore political. A system can claim global reach while imposing one dominant ontology through translation defaults. Traceability, uncertainty, local revision, and plural mappings are important safeguards.

# Orgo

Orgo creates **operational and administrative puissance** by transforming signals and decisions into cases, tasks, owners, routing, escalation, time windows, closure, and operational memory.

Administrative power appears because control over workflow can alter timing, workload, access, escalation, completion, and practical implementation.

Key audit questions: who creates/closes cases, who assigns roles, who controls escalation, what deadlines/defaults exist, how conflicts are appealed, and whether administration can silently redefine the underlying mandate.

# kOA Propagation and Adoption

kOA does not need one universal acquisition channel.

Its architecture supports multiple entry paths that can converge on the same knowledge-to-action infrastructure.

## Adoption path

```text
entry surface
→ contextual understanding
→ inspectable evidence
→ bounded trial
→ local value
→ operational continuity
→ independent mastery
→ optional federation
```

## Entry surfaces

- technical code / documentation;
- public-good / sovereignty framing;
- SmartVote / collective judgment;
- Kintsugi integration;
- cultural provenance / Kreative;
- multilingual access / SemantiK;
- education / UCKK;
- concrete operational workflow / Orgo;
- Worlds / scenario-specific demonstrations;
- cultural narrative and media.

## Why local value matters

The propagation model is strongest when an instance can be useful before network scale.

```text
local value
→ independent adoption
→ reproducible instance
→ compatibility
→ optional exchange
→ network value
```

This avoids making federation a prerequisite for first usefulness.

## Why existing systems can strengthen kOA

Kintsugi-oriented integration changes the competitive geometry.

A capable external tool can become:

- a preserved authority;
- an annexed sidecar;
- a data / artifact source;
- a Kompendio reference;
- a complementary capability.

The desired gravity is therefore **articulation by usefulness**, not forced replacement.

## Why SmartVote can strengthen adoption

SmartVote can act as a system-wide discoverability primitive.

As more evaluable objects, domains, evidence, and contribution histories exist, the platform can potentially generate more useful contextual rankings.

That creates a candidate loop:

`objects → evaluation → discovery → reuse → more objects`.

The loop remains legitimate only if raw participation remains visible, expertise is bounded, and ranking policy is inspectable and contestable.

## Why the movement is a test environment

Once multiple independent contributors exist, using kOA to coordinate kOA can test the architecture under real conditions.

The important threshold is not a label such as “movement.” It is **multi-actor autonomous capacity**:

- people can initiate work;
- coordinate;
- preserve memory;
- challenge decisions;
- operate infrastructure;
- train successors;
- continue during founder absence.

That threshold is both an adoption milestone and a founder-decentering milestone.

# kOA as a Capability and Power Ecology

This directory is the **systems core of the Power Show**.

kOA is analyzed first as an architecture that can make actors more capable, then as an architecture whose successful capabilities can create consequential relations of power.

The operative loop is:

> **Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better**

The Power Show asks:

1. **What new capability does each subsystem create?**
2. **How does that capability compose with other subsystems?**
3. **What returns does successful action preserve?**
4. **How does the result increase later capability?**
5. **Can those returns activate capability in another branch, actor, deployment, or community?**

Power Dynamics then asks:

6. **What power relations arise around successful use?**
7. **What conversion, stacking, capture, or dependency risks appear?**
8. **What counterpowers exist?**
9. **Can alternative branches remain viable and evolve?**

## kOA capability stack

- **SemantiK / SenTient** — semantic puissance;
- **Kristal** — epistemic and mnemonic puissance;
- **Konnaxion** — collective, network, communication, and discoverability puissance;
- **Koali / EkoH** — credibility legibility;
- **SmartVote** — comparative and deliberative puissance;
- **Orgo** — operational and administrative puissance;
- **AI** — cognitive amplification under governance;
- **Capsules / federation** — deployment, substitution, replication, and branch puissance;
- **brand / narrative** — recognition, affiliation, transmission, and economic conversion;
- **multilingual layer** — semantic access and distribution across languages.

The primary system map is [`system-map.md`](system-map.md). The broader showcase is [`../POWER-SHOW.md`](../POWER-SHOW.md).

- [`koali-protocolization.md`](koali-protocolization.md) — Koali as a power-sharing test: from personal property toward plural interoperable distributions.

## Power-surface reading

kOA should also be read across domains rather than only by component. The same component can generate epistemic, political, economic, administrative, narrative, or infrastructural effects through conversion.

See [`../POWER-SURFACE.md`](../POWER-SURFACE.md) and [`../domains/README.md`](../domains/README.md).
- [`propagation-adoption.md`](propagation-adoption.md) — how local value, demonstrations, deployment tooling, human replication, integration, multilingual access, and cross-branch returns can combine into an adoption and propagation ecology.

# kOA Returns and Compounding

kOA can produce returns in several domains: validated knowledge, reusable Kristals, code, operational experience, contributor credibility, network connections, institutional memory, brand recognition, economic resources, standards, and interoperability.

These returns can feed other systems and lower future coordination cost, creating a **power ecology of compounding capability**.

The same loop can create concentration. Recognition can generate visibility, visibility opportunity, and opportunity more recognition. Power Dynamics therefore tracks who receives returns and whether alternatives can accumulate comparable assets.

# SemantiK Architect and SenTient

SemantiK Architect concerns the architecture of meaning: concepts, categories, relations, contexts, and multilingual mappings. SenTient concerns reconciliation of ambiguous or uncertain propositions, entity relations, and semantic interpretation.

These functions create semantic puissance but can also create **constitutive power** if their categories become mandatory or opaque.

Semantic sovereignty therefore requires versioned ontologies, explicit uncertainty, local control where appropriate, traceable transformations, and meaningful alternatives.

source_basis:
  - S15

---
# SmartVote

SmartVote creates **comparative judgment and discoverability puissance** by preserving multiple readings of support rather than hiding evaluation inside one opaque score.

A critical separation is between baseline/raw democratic readings and expertise/ethics or other advisory readings.

The political baseline is not silently superseded by advisory weight. Domain-bounded credibility informs rather than automatically governs.

Power relations arise through lens selection, defaults, criteria, discoverability, ranking, and institutional interpretation of weighted results. Those elements require explicit registries, versioning, auditability, and branch alternatives.
## Academic constraints

The external-research layer documents several constraints directly relevant to SmartVote:

- expert performance is strongly domain- and task-bounded;
- nonexperts need evidence for deciding which experts to trust;
- calibrated / contribution-sensitive weighting can improve some aggregates;
- diversity can add problem-solving value that is lost by selecting only individually high-scoring participants;
- social-choice systems face manipulation and impossibility constraints under broad conditions;
- any reputation or weighting metric becomes a gaming surface once it affects opportunity or authority.

See [`../Academic-Lineage/06-expertise-smartvote-and-collective-judgment.md`](../Academic-Lineage/06-expertise-smartvote-and-collective-judgment.md) and [`../Academic-Lineage/07-metrics-reputation-and-gaming.md`](../Academic-Lineage/07-metrics-reputation-and-gaming.md).

These references constrain the design; they do not validate a particular SmartVote formula.

# kOA System Map

The high-level capability chain is:

> **Contribution → Resolution of meaning → Structured knowledge → Discovery / credibility → Collective choice → Governed work → Coordinated action → Experience → Reusable memory → New cycle**

## Capability map

```text
SemantiK / SenTient
  semantic structure
        ↓
Kristal
  knowledge + provenance + memory
        ↓
Konnaxion
  circulation + people + discovery
        ↓
Koali / EkoH
  credibility context
        ↓
SmartVote
  comparison + advice + choice
        ↓
Orgo
  routing + responsibility + execution
        ↓
Outcomes
        ↓
Kristal / operational memory
  reusable learning
        ↺
```

Cross-cutting layers:

- **AI** — cognitive amplification;
- **Capsules / federation** — portability, deployment, substitution, branch formation;
- **brand / narrative** — recognition, affiliation, transmission;
- **multilingual semantics** — linguistic access and semantic participation.

## Power overlay

Each capability surface can also become a power surface:

| Capability surface | Possible relational power |
|---|---|
| semantic structuration | classification / framing / ontology power |
| knowledge validation | epistemic authority / standard-setting |
| discoverability | ranking / visibility / agenda power |
| credibility interpretation | recognition / standing / opportunity power |
| decision support | default / weighting / advisory influence |
| workflow | routing / timing / escalation / closure power |
| memory | precedent / institutional-memory power |
| federation infrastructure | signing / update / compatibility / registry power |
| AI | hidden interpretation / prioritization power |
| brand | attention / affiliation / legitimacy-by-association |

The constitutional problem is therefore not whether kOA should be powerful. Its purpose is to generate capability. The problem is whether the relations created by successful capability remain **scoped, inspectable, contestable, substitutable, and evolvable**.

## Cross-domain overlay

The same system can be read as a multi-domain power surface:

```text
Semantics / knowledge
→ credibility / discoverability
→ political judgment
→ allocation
→ administrative execution
→ returns / memory
→ economic + institutional compounding
```

Cross-cutting economic, political, and monetary questions should therefore be audited separately:

- **political** — who gains standing, agenda, mandate, and decision influence?
- **economic** — who controls or receives scarce resources and returns?
- **monetary** — who controls exchange rails, liquidity, credit, settlement, or transaction access where those functions exist?
- **routing** — who receives capability, authority, returns, and optionality?

See [`../POWER-SURFACE.md`](../POWER-SURFACE.md).

# Power Routing, Sharing, and Devolution

Power Dynamics does not stop at **how much power kOA can generate**.

It asks:

> **Where does the capability, leverage, authority, return, and optionality created by kOA actually go?**

A system can empower users while still making its operator indispensable. Power routing therefore examines four destinations separately:

```text
CAPABILITY → who can do more?
AUTHORITY  → who can decide or control more?
RETURNS    → who receives reusable value?
OPTIONALITY→ who can refuse, leave, reproduce, fork, or wait?
```

## Core routing mechanisms

| Source of leverage | Concentrating default | Routing / sharing mechanism | Intended destination |
|---|---|---|---|
| Founder knowledge | permanent founder dependency | documentation, schemas, protocolization, tests | maintainers, branches, independent operators |
| Founder / persona authority | deference becomes policy or truth immunity | published fallibility, contestation, opposition rehearsal | independent public judgment |
| Reference knowledge branch | reference becomes truth monopoly | forkability, lineage, evidence comparison | plural epistemic capacity |
| Narrative attention | founder/persona centrality | explicit ethics, self-critique, attribution, anti-domination | public principles, procedures, critical readers |
| Brand | trademark becomes protocol sovereignty | brand stewardship separated from protocol | protocol commons + bounded official brand |
| Koali credibility infrastructure | one owner defines standing | plural distributions, portable evidence, explicit policy identity | communities, domains, alternative operators |
| Expertise | expert becomes ruler | baseline + bounded advisory readings | informed public judgment |
| Knowledge validation | one owner of truth | provenance, reader policies, competing Kristals | plural epistemic capability |
| Infrastructure | operator becomes unavoidable | self-hosting, capsules, federation, rollback, substitution | local operators / communities |
| Money | wealth buys rule | one-member-one-vote / allocation firewalls | common funding without pay-to-rule |
| Workflow control | administrator becomes policymaker | scoped mandate, audit, appeal, revocability | execution without mandate capture |
| AI capability | model becomes hidden sovereign | explicit role boundaries, provenance, human authority | cognitive amplification without institutional sovereignty |
| Dominant branch | incumbency becomes final | branch mobility, lineage, recombination | viable alternatives |

## Ethical fiction as power routing

The fiction is not only a vehicle for symbolic puissance. It also publishes limits on that puissance.

The source corpus includes explicit commitments around:

- clarity of intention;
- respect for living beings;
- vigilance against monopoly, domination, and manipulation;
- recognition of contributors and sharing merit;
- self-mockery and refusal to sanctify the Klown;
- revision, criticism, adaptation, and transmission rather than dogma.

Power Dynamics calls this **narrative self-binding**.

```text
symbolic power
→ public ethical commitments
→ stable grounds for criticism
→ reduced freedom for arbitrary reinterpretation
```

Self-binding is not proof of compliance. It makes the exercise of power **more legible and criticizable**.

## Koali: from property to protocol ecology

Current architect-confirmed design direction:

> **Koali should not remain a terminal personal credibility authority. Its useful capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and operable through plural distributions around interoperable structures.**

The desired transition is:

```text
founder-created capability
→ explicit kernel / contracts
→ portable evidence and history
→ explicit policy identity
→ multiple distributions
→ interoperability
→ independent operation
→ branch mobility
```

This is not founder erasure. It is **ownership decoupling**: the useful function can outgrow exclusive ownership of the first implementation.

## Power sharing is not flatness

The goal is not to make everyone equally expert, equally responsible, or equally authoritative.

The goal is:

> **Useful asymmetry without terminal dependency.**

A maintainer may have more technical authority within a defined scope. An expert may have more epistemic relevance in a defined domain. A coordinator may have routing authority for a workflow. None of those roles should silently acquire unrelated sovereignty.

## Routing and conversion firewalls

Routing is strongest when paired with conversion limits:

```text
expertise → advice                  ✓
expertise → sovereign vote weight   ✗

money → funding                     ✓
money → political standing          ✗

brand → attention                   ✓
brand → truth                       ✗

infrastructure → capability         ✓
infrastructure → captivity          ✗

execution → implement mandate       ✓
execution → redefine mandate        ✗
```

## Canonical routing test

For every important kOA mechanism:

1. What puissance does it create?
2. Where would that puissance naturally concentrate?
3. What relational power does that concentration create?
4. What capability can be routed outward instead?
5. What authority must remain scoped?
6. Who receives the returns?
7. Who receives exit / substitution / fork optionality?
8. What evidence proves that the routing is practical rather than merely formal?

See [`assessment/power-routing-audit.md`](assessment/power-routing-audit.md).

# The kOA Power Show

The first purpose of this repository is simple:

> **Show all the ways kOA can make people, groups, organizations, institutions, and federations more capable.**

Then ask what happens when those capabilities become socially consequential.

## The one-paragraph answer

kOA is immensely powerful because it can connect **meaning, knowledge, AI, discovery, credibility, networks, deliberation, political choice, economic allocation, coordination, execution, memory, infrastructure, federation, and institutional evolution** inside a recursive loop. The result is not merely more information. It is the possibility of turning distributed resources into collective agency, preserving the returns of action, reinvesting them, reproducing the capability elsewhere, and routing important forms of leverage away from terminal ownership.

> **Breadth × Composition × Closure × Memory × Compounding × Propagation × Replication × Routing**

## 1. Breadth — many forms of puissance

kOA can generate or amplify:

- semantic puissance;
- epistemic puissance;
- cognitive / AI-mediated puissance;
- information and distribution puissance;
- attention / discoverability puissance;
- credibility / reputation puissance;
- network and affiliation puissance;
- collective puissance;
- political / deliberative / decision puissance;
- economic and allocation puissance;
- monetary / liquidity capability where monetary infrastructure exists;
- administrative and operational puissance;
- technical and infrastructural puissance;
- narrative / symbolic / brand puissance;
- mnemonic and temporal puissance;
- federation, exit, substitution, fork, and evolutionary puissance.

See [`POWER-SURFACE.md`](POWER-SURFACE.md).

## 2. Composition — the whole can exceed the sum

```text
SemantiK / SenTient  → meaning
Kristal              → knowledge + provenance + memory
Konnaxion            → discovery + network + circulation
Koali / EkoH         → credibility legibility
SmartVote            → comparative judgment + decision support
Orgo                 → operational execution
AI                    → cognitive amplification
Capsules / federation→ replication + substitution
Brand / narrative    → transmission + affiliation
Economic rails       → funding + material capability

                  ↓ compose ↓

      coordinated collective puissance
```

A capable population is not automatically a capable collective. Composition creates the ability to use distributed capabilities **together**.

## 3. Closure — knowledge reaches action

> **Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better**

Many systems stop at one stage. kOA attempts to connect the stages.

```text
knowledge
→ judgment
→ decision
→ governed work
→ execution
→ result
→ memory
```

This turns intelligence into agency.

## 4. Memory — the past remains usable

kOA can preserve:

- evidence;
- provenance;
- definitions;
- decisions;
- reasons;
- workflows;
- failures;
- outcomes;
- contribution history;
- branch lineage.

Without memory, a collective repeatedly pays the cost of rediscovery. With memory, past action becomes infrastructure for future action.

## 5. Compounding — action creates future capability

```text
Puissance
→ Action
→ Result
→ Return
→ Asset
→ Reinvestment
→ Greater Future Puissance
```

Returns can include knowledge, code, tools, data, credibility, network, reputation, money, infrastructure, access, and optionality.

This is the **compounding engine**.


## 5A. Propagation — returns can activate capability elsewhere

A system can compound internally without becoming easier for anyone else to reproduce or adopt.

Propagation begins when reusable returns cross boundaries:

```text
revenue → development capacity
pilot → evidence → credibility → institutional access
training → operator → independent deployment
documentation → lower review cost → external feedback
integration → broader capability → new integration demand
```

When several such loops exchange returns, kOA can exhibit **coupled propagation**.

See [`PROPAGATION.md`](PROPAGATION.md) and [`showcase/15-propagation-engine.md`](showcase/15-propagation-engine.md).

## 6. Cross-domain conversion — one capability unlocks another

```text
knowledge → credibility → visibility → opportunity
money → infrastructure → reach → influence
brand → affiliation → revenue → development
network → collaborators → execution → results
memory → lower coordination cost → faster action
```

Conversion is one reason kOA can become extremely powerful. It is also one reason it can become dangerous.

> **Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power**

See [`framework/10-cross-domain-power-conversion.md`](framework/10-cross-domain-power-conversion.md).

## 7. Political power — before, during, and after the vote

kOA touches:

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

That means political power is not confined to SmartVote or formal governance. Semantic definitions, search, ranking, credibility, workflow, and memory can all shape political possibility.

## 8. Economic power — resources become action

kOA can help convert:

```text
money / property / funding / labor / infrastructure
→ usable economic capacity
→ projects / services / coordination / production
```

It can also route economic resources through democratic allocation mechanisms rather than allowing wealth to automatically buy more political standing.

## 9. Monetary power — control of exchange rails

Monetary power concerns issuance, liquidity, settlement, payment rails, reserves, credit, debt conditions, and transaction access.

The legacy KingCoin material shows why this domain is so constitutionally sensitive: monetary control can stack with taxation, allocation, sanctions, and administration into highly terminal power.

The evolved doctrine is therefore not “centralize monetary power well,” but:

> **If powerful monetary functions exist, keep their authority scoped, contestable, separable, and substitutable.**

## 10. Scaling — individual to federation

```text
individual
→ team
→ organization
→ institution
→ federation
```

At each scale, kOA can add shared knowledge, coordination, decision, execution, memory, and infrastructure.

The design target is:

> **Capability can scale without sovereignty scaling at the same rate.**

## 11. Replication — capability can leave the center

Self-hosting, capsules, portability, federation, and branchability can transform:

```text
use a central service
```

into:

```text
possess / reproduce / operate the function locally
```

That is a major transfer of effective possibility.

## 12. Power routing — kOA can equip others instead of only itself

The project includes mechanisms intended to redirect the power it creates:

```text
expertise → advice, not sovereignty
money → funding, not more votes
narrative → mobilization + ethical self-binding
Koali → plural distributions, not terminal personal ownership
Kristal → portable knowledge, not one owner of truth
infrastructure → self-hosting / federation, not captivity
dominance → branch mobility, not final authority
```

This is why **power sharing** is part of the Power Show, not merely a later defensive layer.

## 13. The constitutional paradox

The stronger the Power Show becomes, the more necessary constitutional design becomes.

```text
more knowledge capability
→ more validation power

more discovery capability
→ more agenda power

more credibility capability
→ more standing power

more workflow capability
→ more administrative power

more infrastructure capability
→ more dependency power
```

Therefore:

> **The constitution exists because the Power Show succeeds.**

## 14. The mature doctrine

> **Generate immense puissance.**  
> **Compose it.**  
> **Close the loop.**  
> **Preserve the returns.**  
> **Compound future capability.**  
> **Make cross-domain conversions visible.**  
> **Route capability outward.**  
> **Block automatic conversion into unrelated sovereignty.**  
> **Preserve verification, contestation, substitution, exit, and forkability.**  
> **Allow the whole system to evolve.**

In compressed form:

> **Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.**

# The kOA Power Surface

A **power surface** is any function, interface, role, registry, workflow, standard, narrative, resource rail, or infrastructural boundary through which control can materially alter effective possibility.

The kOA Power Show is strongest when it makes the whole surface visible at once.

> **kOA is powerful because it can create capability across many domains and connect those domains into conversion and feedback loops.**

## Master map

| Domain | Generated puissance | Relational power that may emerge | Typical conversion outward | kOA routing / firewall direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Epistemic | validate, compare, preserve, reuse knowledge | validation, canon, trust-root | knowledge → credibility | portable artifacts, provenance, competing Kristals |
| Semantic | structure concepts, categories, translation, ambiguity | classification, framing, admissibility | meaning → agenda / enforcement | versioned semantics, local governance, contestation |
| Cognitive / AI | reasoning, synthesis, modeling, translation | hidden framing, prioritization, dependency | cognition → judgment / execution | AI under explicit governance, bounded authority |
| Information / attention | publish, search, rank, distribute, amplify | visibility, agenda, moderation | attention → affiliation / legitimacy / funding | explicit discovery policies, alternatives, export |
| Credibility / reputation | make evidence of competence/contribution legible | standing, certification, opportunity | credibility → visibility / access | domain-bounded signals, plural models, contestation |
| Network / affiliation | association, brokerage, recruitment, diffusion | inclusion, exclusion, access | network → execution / opportunity | portable identity/relationships where feasible, plural networks |
| Political / constitutional | deliberate, decide, authorize, amend, govern | mandate, veto, legitimacy, decision | political → administrative / allocation | political equality, scoped authority, contestation |
| Economic | mobilize scarce resources, assets, labor, funding | bargaining, ownership, allocation | money → infrastructure / reach | democratic allocation, anti-pay-to-rule firewalls |
| Monetary | liquidity, exchange, payment, credit, settlement | issuance, rail, reserve, access | monetary → economic / political dependency | treat high-stack monetary designs as constitutional risk |
| Administrative / operational | assign, route, schedule, escalate, close | timing, workload, approval, closure | administration → de facto policy | explicit workflows, audit, appeal, revocability |
| Technical / infrastructural | build, deploy, verify, operate, reproduce | compatibility, update, identity, dependency | infrastructure → standards / access | self-hosting, capsules, federation, substitution |
| Narrative / symbolic / brand | memory, attention, affiliation, transmission | framing, legitimacy-by-association, gatekeeping | brand → attention → revenue / adoption | ethical self-binding, brand ≠ truth, protocol separation |
| Mnemonic / temporal | preserve reasons, outcomes, precedent, timing | precedent, delay, succession | memory → future agenda / legitimacy | provenance, versioning, alternative histories |
| Evolutionary / counterpower | fork, substitute, recombine, replace | displacement of incumbents | alternative → support → dominance | branch mobility, lineage, interoperability |

## Why breadth matters

A system that is powerful in one domain can still be constrained by other systems. A system that touches many domains can create much larger **cross-domain leverage**.

For example:

```text
semantic control
→ epistemic interpretation
→ credibility
→ visibility
→ agenda
→ political influence
```

or:

```text
money
→ infrastructure
→ distribution
→ attention
→ affiliation
→ political leverage
```

or:

```text
technical control
→ defaults
→ dependency
→ administrative necessity
→ de facto governance
```

The same breadth that makes kOA capable is therefore the reason it needs explicit constitutional design.

## Political surface

kOA can influence political capacity through:

- issue formation and semantic framing;
- discoverability and agenda formation;
- credibility and advisory signals;
- deliberation and proposal comparison;
- voting / reading mechanisms;
- resource allocation;
- workflow and execution;
- memory and precedent;
- branch evolution and institutional alternatives.

Political power therefore begins **before the vote** and continues **after the vote**.

## Economic surface

Economic puissance includes the ability to mobilize:

- money;
- property;
- productive assets;
- labor and time;
- infrastructure;
- funding;
- contracts;
- procurement;
- philanthropy;
- bargaining position.

kOA can increase economic capability by improving coordination, credibility, discoverability, resource pooling, allocation, execution, and compounding.

## Monetary surface

Monetary power is narrower than economic power. It concerns control over:

- issuance;
- units of account;
- liquidity;
- payment rails;
- settlement;
- reserves;
- credit and debt conditions;
- convertibility;
- transaction access.

A monetary layer can become extraordinarily powerful because it conditions other domains. This is why the legacy KingCoin proposals are useful as a **stacking stress test**, not as current constitutional doctrine.

## The core constitutional question

For every surface, ask:

1. **Who gains capability?**
2. **Who gains authority?**
3. **Who receives returns?**
4. **Who gains optionality?**
5. **What other domain can this convert into?**
6. **What prevents that conversion from becoming arbitrary?**
7. **Can affected actors verify, contest, substitute, exit, or fork?**

The desired architecture is not flatness. It is a **governable ecology of strong capabilities and non-terminal asymmetries**.

# Principles

Power Dynamics begins from an affirmative premise: **people and collectives should be able to become dramatically more capable**. The constitutional problem is how to preserve that capability without allowing successful functions to harden into arbitrary, cross-domain, or terminal sovereignty.

## Generate immense but usable puissance

1. **Increase effective possibility.** expand what actors can realistically understand, choose, build, coordinate, refuse, preserve, and accomplish.
2. **Turn dispersed resources into mobilizable capability rather than merely accumulating resources.** 
3. **Collective puissance can be generated.** coordinated capability can exceed the sum of isolated capabilities.
4. **Infrastructure should crystallize past effort into reusable future capacity.** 
5. **Knowledge, memory, tools, and institutional learning should become cumulative.** 
6. **Capability should scale from individual to federation without requiring sovereignty to scale at the same rate.** 
7. **Composition does not require ownership.** interoperating functions need not collapse into one owner or authority.

## Keep domains explicit

8. **Political power, economic power, and monetary power are distinct even when they convert into one another.** 
9. **Political power begins before formal decision.** meaning, visibility, credibility, and agenda are political surfaces.
10. **Economic puissance concerns mobilization of scarce resources.** economic power concerns relational effects of that control.
11. **Monetary power concerns the infrastructure and conditions of exchange.** issuance, rails, liquidity, settlement, reserves, credit, and access.
12. **Administrative power can alter outcomes without changing formal policy.** 
13. **Semantic and epistemic power can shape the field in which later political decisions become intelligible.** 
14. **Every domain analysis should name resources, capability, relation, conversion paths, duration, and counterpowers.** 

## Maintain ontological discipline

15. **Resource ≠ Potential ≠ Capacity ≠ Puissance ≠ Power.** 
16. **A resource is not automatically power.** 
17. **Puissance is effective mobilizable capacity under real conditions.** 
18. **Power is relational and domain-specific.** 
19. **Power to / with / through are primarily capability questions.** power over is primarily a relational question.
20. **Stock, reservoir, flow, reach, influence, authority, legitimacy, and sovereignty are not synonyms.** 

## Expose conversion and stacking

21. **Different forms of puissance should remain composable without automatically converting into unrelated authority.** 
22. **Expertise may inform.** it does not automatically govern.
23. **Money may fund.** it does not automatically buy political standing.
24. **Brand may attract attention.** it does not become truth.
25. **Technical administration may maintain infrastructure.** it does not silently inherit political authority.
26. **Credibility may improve discoverability.** it does not become unquestionable legitimacy.
27. **Execution may carry out a mandate.** it does not silently redefine the mandate.
28. **Infrastructure may create capability.** it does not thereby own its users.
29. **Power stacking must be assessed across domains, not function by function.** 
30. **Capture is a conversion problem.** Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power.

## Route and share power deliberately

31. **Power should be routed, not merely constrained after concentration.** 
32. **Generated capability should preferentially become reusable capability for participants and communities where feasible.** 
33. **Public ethical commitments can be a self-binding mechanism.** they do not prove compliance but create stable grounds for criticism.
34. **Narrative power should carry its own limits through explicit anti-domination, attribution, humility, and revision principles.** 
35. **Protocolization is power sharing.** tacit founder mastery should become explicit, testable, documented, and independently reproducible.
36. **Useful functions should be decoupled from exclusive ownership where feasible.** 
37. **Koali is a key ownership-decoupling test.** credibility capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions.
38. **Returns are part of power routing.** who receives knowledge, money, reputation, network, code, memory, and infrastructure is constitutional.
39. **Exit, substitution, self-hosting, and forkability distribute optionality.** 
40. **Sharing capability does not require flatness.** the target is non-terminal asymmetry.
41. **The strongest center is one that can create capability beyond itself.** 

## Make emerging power legible

42. **Architecture is governance before governance is written.** 
43. **Every technical boundary is also a potential political boundary.** 
44. **Every mechanism that distributes capability creates a new locus of power.** 
45. **Dependency is a gradient.** formal choice is not effective choice when switching costs are prohibitive.
46. **Terminal power is a primary constitutional risk.** 
47. **Functional centrality is not automatically domination, but centrality plus dependency plus weak counterpower can become domination.** 
48. **Instance capture must not imply system capture.** 

## Preserve counterpower

49. **Verification + Contestation + Substitution + Exit are foundational counterpowers.** 
50. **Formal exit is insufficient.** exit must be effective.
51. **Formal forkability is insufficient.** branch mobility matters.
52. **Revocability and reversibility are distinct.** 
53. **Strong hubs are compatible with distributed capability when the whole remains independently reproducible.** 
54. **Portability must include the artifacts, history, provenance, evidence, and configuration needed for meaningful continuity.** 

## Preserve epistemic humility

55. **No measure of competence, ethics, credibility, contribution, or legitimacy should be treated as perfectly complete or permanently final.** 
56. **Score ≠ person ≠ competence ≠ ethics ≠ truth.** 
57. **Support is not evidence.** 
58. **Dominance is not truth, quality, or final legitimacy.** 
59. **Uncertainty, disagreement, and competing representations must remain representable.** 
60. **Plurality is useful when it adds evidence, context, method, function, challenge, translation, or synthesis.** 

## Make capability regenerative

61. **Action should leave assets behind.** 
62. **Who receives the returns matters.** 
63. **Returns can compound future puissance.** 
64. **Compounding can also compound concentration and must be audited.** 
65. **Shared infrastructure should convert collective activity into broader future capability where feasible.** 
66. **Memory is a production factor, not merely an archive.** 

## Keep the system evolvable

67. **Imperfection is permanent.** authority need not be.
68. **Variation is normal institutional experimentation, not only an emergency response to capture.** 
69. **Complementarity can be superior to convergence.** 
70. **The stable kernel should protect evolution, not freeze outcomes.** 
71. **A better branch should be able to demonstrate value, gather support, remain interoperable, and influence or replace a dominant branch.** 
72. **Improvement claims should state the dimension, beneficiaries, conditions, evidence, and trade-offs.** 

## kOA constitutional orientation

73. **Strong distributed puissance.** Weak sovereignty.
74. **StrongHub + DistributedCapability + ReplicableWhole.** 
75. **AI assists under governance.** AI does not silently inherit institutional sovereignty.
76. **Semantic sovereignty requires practical control, not merely formal openness.** 
77. **Founder independence is a resilience criterion, not a requirement to erase the founder.** 
78. **Brand stewardship and protocol governance are distinct.** 
79. **The system should remain capable of learning from its own alternatives.** 



## Adversarial resilience

80. **Founder fallibility is expected.** A durable system must survive both founder absence and founder error.
81. **Counterpower should be rehearsable.** Formal opposition rights are weaker than practiced, socially legitimate opposition pathways.
82. **Forkability does not imply epistemic equivalence.** Incompatible worldviews may coexist while evidence, provenance, contradiction, uncertainty, and lineage remain comparable.
83. **Apparent multiplicity is not independent confirmation.** Source and credibility systems should distinguish surface count from causal independence where provenance permits.
84. **A reference branch must remain a reference, not a monopoly on reality.** Stewardship can maintain coherence without eliminating the right to branch, contest, reinterpret, or simulate alternatives.

## Propagate capability without propagating dependency

- **Compounding and propagation are distinct.** A system can become more capable without making capability easier to activate elsewhere.
- **Reusable returns should cross branch boundaries when doing so increases common capability without silently transferring unrelated authority.**
- **Healthy propagation lowers activation barriers for other actors.** It should not merely increase dependence on a central operator.
- **Cross-loop coupling must be legible.** If revenue, credibility, evidence, brand, tooling, or standards feed another domain, the conversion path should be inspectable.
- **Positive feedback requires governors.** Growth in expertise, brand, money, integration, federation, AI output, or founder recognition must not automatically become terminal sovereignty.
- **Founder absence is a capability test.** A mature system should continue to operate, reproduce, teach, contest, and evolve without requiring continuous founder intervention.
- **Simulation and demonstration are not adoption evidence.** Worlds, scenarios, and cinematic tours must remain epistemically separate from real use.
- **Contact is not endorsement.** Outreach may create future recognition, but `contacted ≠ read ≠ familiar ≠ supportive ≠ partner`.
- **Multiplicity at the periphery; simplicity at the center.** Many cultural, technical, linguistic, or institutional entry paths should converge on the same inspectable architecture and evidence.
- **The goal is not maximum acceleration.** The system should preserve the ability to pause, remain local, defer federation, fork, or operate at a sustainable pace.


================================================================================================

---

scope: "GENERAL"
version: "6.3"
title: "Propagation and Coupled Compounding"
source_basis:
  - S02
  - S13
  - S15
  - S16

  - S25
  - S28
  - S29
---
# Propagation and Coupled Compounding

Power Dynamics already explains how capability is formed, mobilized, composed, converted, preserved, compounded, routed, contested, and replicated.

This page adds the missing systems-level question:

> **How does capability in one branch make capability elsewhere easier to activate, and what happens when several such loops reinforce one another?**

## 1. Compounding is not propagation

**Compounding** means that action leaves returns that increase later capability in the same actor, branch, or system.

```text
A capability
→ action
→ result
→ reusable return
→ greater future A capability
```

**Propagation** means that capability or returns from one locus reduce the activation cost, increase the expected value, or supply resources for capability to emerge elsewhere.

```text
A capability
→ reusable return
→ lower activation barrier for B
→ B capability emerges or strengthens
```

**Coupled propagation** appears when several loops exchange returns:

```text
A strengthens B
B strengthens C
C returns assets to A
```

The system can then grow by **conversion between loops**, not only by repetition inside one loop.

## 2. The kOA propagation thesis

kOA is unusually propagation-oriented because different branches can generate different reusable returns:

- software and tooling;
- knowledge and documentation;
- credibility and evidence;
- relationships and institutional access;
- money and service revenue;
- operators and teaching capacity;
- language coverage;
- integrations and compatibility;
- content and demonstrations;
- reputation and brand recognition;
- machine-readable context;
- deployment experience and operational memory.

The important property is not simply that each branch can grow.

> **Returns generated in one branch can often be routed into another branch.**

Examples:

```text
service revenue
→ development capacity
→ better tooling
→ lower deployment cost
→ more deployments
→ more service revenue
```

```text
successful pilot
→ evidence
→ credibility
→ institutional access
→ additional pilots
→ more evidence
```

```text
new operator
→ independent deployment
→ reduced founder dependency
→ more simultaneous deployments
→ more training cases
→ better operator formation
```

```text
new integration
→ broader capability surface
→ more useful deployments
→ more reasons for other systems to integrate
```

## 3. The propagation stack

The current kOA design exposes several distinct scale surfaces.

| Scale surface | Primary mechanism | Main returned asset |
|---|---|---|
| Construction | AI-assisted engineering + internal tooling | code, methods, automation |
| Qualification | diagnostics, tests, explicit contracts | confidence, lower regression cost |
| Contextualization | Worlds / scenario seeding | legibility, domain-specific demonstration |
| Explanation | cinematic tours, multi-format media | understanding, attention, recall |
| Deployment | Capsule Manager / Koali | lower activation and operations cost |
| Local value | offline / self-host / local governance | usefulness before federation |
| Discovery | SmartVote + EkoH + Kompendio | higher-signal ranking and attention |
| Ecosystem integration | Kintsugi / annexation / explicit boundaries | added capability without rebuilding |
| Human replication | UCKK / documentation / operator formation | independent operators |
| Linguistic expansion | SemantiK / GF-oriented tooling | new communities and semantic reach |
| Machine comprehension | structured docs / context packs / `llms.txt`-style surfaces | lower due-diligence cost |
| Economic continuity | public good + paid responsibility | operating revenue without permission tolls |
| Cultural diffusion | books, music, podcasts, fiction, public essays | multi-audience entry paths |
| Federation | portable artifacts + independent instances | network value without mandatory centralization |

S29 records the architect-confirmed current tooling and design directions behind the rows that are not fully represented in the earlier source corpus.

## 4. Coupling matters more than the number of loops

A list of fourteen independent flywheels is not yet a propagation architecture.

The stronger condition is **cross-loop convertibility**.

```text
TECHNICAL CAPABILITY
        ↓
useful product
        ↓
WORLD / DEMO
        ↓
understanding
        ↓
pilot
        ↓
real evidence
   ↙         ↘
credibility   operational demand
   ↓               ↓
access          managed service
   ↓               ↓
new pilots       revenue
   ↘               ↙
    greater development capacity
               ↓
       greater technical capability
               ↺
```

A second loop can attach:

```text
pilot
→ documented method
→ UCKK / operator formation
→ independent operator
→ additional deployment
→ more cases for training
```

A third can attach:

```text
deployment
→ more objects / judgments / evidence
→ SmartVote signal
→ better discovery
→ higher utility
→ more deployment interest
```

The multiplication effect comes from **shared returns**.

## 5. Branch asymmetry: local failure, exportable success

A modular branch ecology can create a useful asymmetry:

```text
branch failure
→ primarily local loss

branch success
→ potentially exportable returns
  (money, evidence, code, credibility, users, methods, integrations)
→ benefits elsewhere
```

This is only true when boundaries remain real. Hidden coupling can turn local failure into systemic failure.

Propagation therefore depends on the same constitutional requirements as the rest of Power Dynamics:

- explicit authority boundaries;
- portable evidence and artifacts;
- no automatic cross-domain sovereignty;
- substitutability and exit;
- local operational autonomy where practical;
- inspectable conversion paths.

## 6. Reflexive propagation: kOA can be used to develop kOA

A special case is **movement / ecosystem self-use**.

If a multi-actor kOA initiative uses its own systems for proposals, ranking, work routing, knowledge preservation, training, and publication, then the deployment environment becomes a continuous integration test for the sociotechnical architecture.

```text
contributors
→ Konnaxion / SmartVote
→ priorities
→ Orgo
→ work
→ results
→ Kristal / documentation
→ UCKK
→ new operators
→ more contributors
→ better kOA
→ more capable collaboration
↺
```

This loop is not evidence until multiple actors use it in practice. It is nevertheless a particularly strong falsifiable design target.

## 7. Founder decentering is part of propagation, not only governance

Founder-specific knowledge can be converted into shared capability:

```text
founder knowledge
→ documentation / schemas / tests / protocols
→ independent mastery
→ more parallel work
→ less founder bottleneck
```

The decisive resilience property is not pseudonymity.

A persona or pseudonym can manage exposure and preserve narrative optionality. It does **not** make the system independent.

System independence requires:

- other maintainers;
- independent deployment;
- portable keys / governance where appropriate;
- explicit protocols;
- reproducible builds and recovery;
- documentation sufficient for outsiders;
- legitimate disagreement and fork paths.

A useful test is:

> **Can the founder become temporarily unavailable without halting operation, interpretation, deployment, governance, or transmission?**

## 8. Positive feedback must have governors

Propagation without damping becomes capture, runaway growth, noise, or fragility.

Important governors include:

```text
expertise grows
→ keep expertise domain-bounded

credibility grows
→ credibility ≠ sovereignty

money grows
→ funding ≠ political standing

brand grows
→ brand ≠ protocol authority

integration grows
→ preserve system authority boundaries

AI throughput grows
→ AI remains bounded and non-authoritative

content grows
→ provenance / validation / publication gates

federation grows
→ local exit / fork / autonomy remain practical

founder recognition grows
→ founder ≠ constitutional authority
```

The target is not maximum acceleration.

> **Healthy propagation increases distributed capability faster than it increases terminal dependency.**

## 9. Evidence ladder

Propagation claims should move through explicit evidence levels.

```text
DESIGNED
→ DEMONSTRATED
→ USED
→ REPEATED
→ INDEPENDENTLY REPLICATED
→ FEDERATED / CROSS-BRANCH
```

A World is evidence that a context can be represented.
It is not proof of adoption.

A cinematic tour is evidence that a demonstration can be generated.
It is not proof that a viewer understood or converted.

A pilot is evidence of bounded use.
It is not proof of independent replication.

The strongest propagation evidence appears when another actor reproduces useful capability without founder mediation.

## 10. Canonical propagation tests

For any proposed loop, ask:

1. What is the input asset?
2. What conversion occurs?
3. What reusable return is produced?
4. Who receives that return?
5. Which other loop can consume it?
6. Does the return lower activation cost elsewhere?
7. Does the loop reduce or increase dependency?
8. What damping mechanism prevents runaway conversion?
9. What observation would falsify the loop?
10. Can the loop close without founder intervention?

See [`propagation/`](propagation/README.md) and [`assessment/propagation-audit.md`](assessment/propagation-audit.md).

# Propagation: Field Definition

## Compounding

> **A return from prior action increases later capability in the same actor, branch, or system.**

`A(t) → action → return → A(t+1)`

## Propagation

> **A return generated in one locus lowers the activation cost, raises the expected value, supplies resources, or improves the viability of capability elsewhere.**

`A → return → lower barrier / greater possibility for B → B`

## Coupled propagation

> **Two or more feedback loops exchange returns such that progress in one loop increases the closure probability or throughput of another.**

`A → B → C → A`

## Propagation asset

A **propagation asset** is a reusable return that can cross an actor, branch, domain, deployment, language, or institutional boundary.

Examples:

- code;
- protocol;
- deployment automation;
- evidence;
- credibility;
- documentation;
- money;
- trained operators;
- templates;
- integrations;
- language resources;
- brand recognition;
- source corpora;
- standards;
- institutional relationships.

## Activation barrier

An **activation barrier** is any requirement that must be crossed before a new actor can obtain useful capability.

Examples:

- inability to understand the system;
- lack of relevant local data;
- deployment complexity;
- lack of trust;
- language mismatch;
- lack of trained operators;
- perceived lock-in;
- insufficient economic resources;
- integration friction;
- founder dependence.

Propagation mechanisms matter when they reduce one or more of these barriers without merely relocating dependency to a new center.

## Healthy propagation

Healthy propagation satisfies two conditions simultaneously:

1. more actors become effectively capable;
2. terminal dependency does not grow faster than distributed capability.

A system can spread rapidly while becoming more centralized. That is diffusion, not necessarily empowering propagation.

# Coupled Feedback Loops

A single flywheel can compound one asset. A propagation ecology exists when multiple loops exchange assets.

## Basic form

```text
Loop A produces return X
        ↓
X is usable by Loop B
        ↓
Loop B produces return Y
        ↓
Y raises the throughput of Loop A
```

The coupling can be stronger than either loop alone because each removes a bottleneck for the other.

## Common coupling classes

### Economic ↔ technical

`revenue → development capacity → better product → more operational demand → revenue`

### Evidence ↔ institutional access

`pilot → evidence → credibility → evaluation access → more pilots`

### Human capacity ↔ deployment

`training → operator → independent deployment → real cases → better training`

### Integration ↔ adoption

`integration → broader usefulness → adoption → demand for more integration`

### Knowledge ↔ execution

`structured knowledge → better action → observed result → better knowledge`

### Discovery ↔ contribution

`better ranking → more useful discovery → more participation → more evaluation data → better ranking`

### Documentation ↔ independent review

`structured documentation → lower review cost → external feedback → better documentation`

## Coupling strength

A loop is more strongly coupled when:

- the returned asset is portable;
- another loop can consume it with low conversion cost;
- the asset retains provenance;
- the asset is not locked behind one operator;
- the conversion is repeatable;
- the return is not exhausted by reuse.

Knowledge, software, standards, templates, and reputation can therefore propagate very differently from scarce physical assets.

## Coupling risk

The same property can create systemic fragility.

If every loop depends on one identity provider, one signer, one hosting company, one founder, one ranking policy, or one funding source, apparent compounding can hide a single point of failure.

Every coupled-loop analysis should therefore model:

- positive feedback;
- dependency concentration;
- failure propagation;
- conversion firewalls;
- exit / substitution paths;
- decay and saturation.

# kOA Adoption Stack

Adoption is not one event. It is a sequence of barriers.

```text
NOTICE
→ UNDERSTAND
→ TRUST ENOUGH TO EXAMINE
→ SEE LOCAL RELEVANCE
→ TRY
→ DEPLOY
→ GET LOCAL VALUE
→ REPEAT
→ OPERATE INDEPENDENTLY
→ CONNECT / FEDERATE IF USEFUL
```

Different kOA mechanisms attack different barriers.

| Barrier | Candidate mechanism | Intended effect |
|---|---|---|
| Notice | books, music, podcasts, public essays, targeted outreach | create an entry path |
| Complexity | progressive documentation, cinematic tours, concise briefs | reduce explanation cost |
| Abstractness | Worlds / scenario seeding | make the system legible in a familiar context |
| Trust | public code, tests, provenance, explicit limitations | reduce belief dependence |
| Local relevance | Scenario Mosaic / domain patterns | connect general architecture to a real workflow |
| Deployment | Capsule Manager / Koali tooling | reduce technical activation cost |
| Lock-in fear | self-hosting, export, fork, local authority | preserve optionality |
| Language | SemantiK / multilingual tooling | reduce semantic access barriers |
| Operator scarcity | UCKK / runbooks / documentation | reproduce human capability |
| Integration cost | Kintsugi boundaries / annexation | reuse existing systems instead of replacing them |
| Ongoing operations | managed service | sell responsibility rather than permission |

The strongest adoption architecture does not require every actor to pass through the same corridor.

A researcher may enter through knowledge infrastructure.
A language organization may enter through multilingual capability.
A municipality may enter through a concrete workflow.
A developer may enter through code.
A cultural institution may enter through provenance and stewardship.

These paths should converge on the same inspectable core rather than becoming disconnected narratives.

# Cross-Branch Returns

A modular ecosystem becomes strategically unusual when branch returns can cross branch boundaries.

## Return classes

| Return | Example origin | Example destination |
|---|---|---|
| Money | managed hosting / support / merchandise | engineering, documentation, public-good infrastructure |
| Code | internal tooling | multiple repositories |
| Evidence | institutional pilot | credibility for another branch |
| Credibility | academic / technical validation | institutional access elsewhere |
| Relationships | one sector | introductions into another sector |
| Knowledge | operational use | documentation, Kristal, training |
| Operators | UCKK / apprenticeship | deployments, support, integration |
| Language assets | SemantiK work | Konnaxion, UCKK, public communication |
| Integrations | Kintsugi | wider system capability |
| Content | Worlds / scenarios | demos, education, tests |
| Brand attention | cultural layer | discovery of technical assets |
| Technical legitimacy | public repositories / tests | confidence in nontechnical initiatives |

## Strategic asymmetry

A desirable branch architecture tries to make:

```text
failure costs local
success returns portable
```

This is never automatic. Shared infrastructure, brand, funding, identity, or governance can make failure systemic.

The audit question is therefore:

> **Which branch dependencies are intentionally shared, and which are only accidentally shared?**

## Cross-subsidy without capture

Cross-branch funding can be productive:

`Branch A revenue → shared infrastructure → Branch B capability`

But economic return should not silently purchase unrelated authority.

`funding → capability` can be legitimate.

`funding → political or epistemic sovereignty` requires an explicit constitutional basis and should normally be blocked.

# Distribution Surfaces

A complex infrastructure can fail to propagate even when it is technically useful because explanation itself does not scale.

kOA uses or proposes several distinct **distribution surfaces**.

## Human-facing surfaces

- institutional briefs;
- public websites;
- technical repositories and wikis;
- Medium-style essays and academic articles;
- books;
- albums / songs;
- podcasts;
- fictional / symbolic narrative;
- scenario mosaics;
- seeded Worlds;
- deterministic cinematic demonstrations.

The purpose of multiple surfaces is not to repeat the same pitch everywhere. It is to let different audiences enter through a format they can parse while preserving a shared factual spine.

## Machine-facing surfaces

- structured Markdown;
- explicit source maps;
- context packs;
- stable schemas;
- machine-readable indexes;
- `llms.txt`-style entry points or equivalent concise machine-oriented maps.

These can reduce the cost for a third party to use its own AI system to inspect the corpus.

## Distribution loop

```text
structured corpus
→ lower explanation / due-diligence cost
→ more independent examination
→ more criticism / questions / evidence
→ better corpus
→ lower future explanation cost
```

## Worlds and cinematic demonstrations

Architect-confirmed current tooling includes seeded Worlds and a cinematic demonstration pipeline. Their propagation role is specific:

```text
real sources
→ structured World
→ populated environment
→ scripted interaction
→ understandable demonstration
```

A World can solve the **empty-platform demonstration problem** by showing a context already populated with recognizable objects.

A cinematic pipeline can reduce the marginal cost of explaining that World to many audiences.

Neither mechanism proves adoption. Their value is measured by downstream conversion:

`demo → examination → pilot → deployment`.

## Convergence requirement

Many entry points create a maze risk.

The communication architecture should therefore follow:

> **Multiplicity at the periphery; simplicity and traceability at the center.**

Every narrative or media corridor should make it possible to reach the canonical architecture, evidence, status, and limitations.

# Autonomy and Founder Absence

Founder decentering is both a constitutional requirement and a throughput mechanism.

If every deployment, interpretation, integration, conflict, or public explanation requires one founder, growth increases bottleneck pressure faster than capacity.

## Founder-to-protocol conversion

```text
founder skill
→ method
→ documentation
→ schema / test / tool
→ independent operator
→ parallel capability
```

The objective is not founder disappearance. It is to make founder participation **optional rather than structurally mandatory**.

## Absence test

A strong propagation system should eventually answer yes to questions such as:

- Can another person install it?
- Can another person restore it?
- Can another person explain its core model accurately?
- Can another person train the next operator?
- Can governance continue without founder voting or interpretation?
- Can a branch reject a founder preference?
- Can an institution leave without founder permission?
- Can development continue during founder inactivity?

## Persona / pseudonym distinction

An architect may use a pseudonym or narrative persona to preserve creative freedom, manage exposure, or create a separate cultural surface.

That does **not** by itself make the system resilient to criticism or personal attack.

Resilience comes from:

- independent evidence;
- distributed maintainership;
- forkability;
- source availability;
- explicit authority boundaries;
- reproducible operations;
- plural interpretation;
- continued function during founder absence.

The useful separation is therefore:

```text
persona → exposure / narrative optionality
protocolization → technical and institutional autonomy
```

# Damping and Runaway Prevention

Positive feedback is not automatically desirable.

Without damping, compounding can become:

- incumbency;
- lock-in;
- metric gaming;
- prestige reproduction;
- founder centrality;
- monopolistic standards power;
- misinformation amplification;
- administrative overreach;
- uncontrolled complexity;
- operational overload.

## Governor pattern

A propagation mechanism should be paired with at least one countervailing mechanism.

| Positive loop | Runaway risk | Governor |
|---|---|---|
| SmartVote recognition | prestige becomes permanent authority | domain bounds, time decay, raw baseline, audit |
| Managed-service revenue | money buys unrelated control | authority separation; funding ≠ votes |
| Brand attention | persona becomes truth source | traceability to evidence; brand ≠ protocol |
| Kintsugi integration | interface layer becomes mandatory choke point | replaceable adapters, explicit boundaries, direct exit |
| Federation | de facto center dominates local nodes | local authority, optional federation, forkability |
| AI production | quantity overwhelms validation | provenance, tests, human review, publication gates |
| Documentation growth | information overload | canonical entry points, progressive disclosure |
| Founder recognition | personal interpretation becomes constitutional | founder decentering, opposition, maintainer pluralism |
| Language expansion | nominal support outruns quality | conformance tiers, native review, published coverage |
| World generation | simulation mistaken for evidence | explicit simulation/reconstruction/live labels |
| Outreach | contact mistaken for endorsement | evidence-state tracking |

## Pace is also a control surface

A healthy architecture does not require continuous acceleration.

Branches can pause, remain local, defer federation, or allow the founder or maintainers to step back temporarily.

This is a resilience feature when the system can preserve state and continue safely without constant central intervention.

## Canonical condition

> **The system should be able to accumulate capability without requiring the same rate of accumulation in centralized authority, dependency, or founder attention.**

source_basis:

# Propagation Metrics and Falsification

Propagation should be measured as a set of transitions, not asserted from architecture alone.

## Activation metrics

- time to first locally useful state;
- time from first exposure to qualified examination;
- World / demo → evaluator conversion;
- evaluator → bounded pilot conversion;
- pilot → deployment conversion;
- deployment → repeated use;
- deployment → referral / routing to another organization.

## Replication metrics

- installation time for a non-founder operator;
- founder minutes required per new instance;
- restore / migration time;
- time to second independent instance;
- percentage of deployments completed without founder intervention;
- number of operators able to train another operator.

## Cross-branch metrics

- percentage of revenue reinvested into shared capability;
- number of branches consuming an asset produced by another branch;
- reuse count per internal tool / integration / template;
- credibility spillover: one branch's validated result leading to qualified review of another;
- integration reuse across domains.

## Network / discovery metrics

- number of objects evaluable by SmartVote;
- number of active domains with meaningful expertise data;
- ranking usefulness / downstream selection quality;
- percentage of high-ranked items later reused, funded, taught, or deployed;
- disagreement between baseline and advisory lenses and how users interpret it.

## Multilingual metrics

- languages by conformance tier;
- test coverage per language;
- native-review status;
- population / institutions actually reached, not merely nominal language count;
- contribution rate from newly supported language communities.

## Independence metrics

- founder intervention per month / deployment / governance cycle;
- number of maintainers with release capability;
- number of independently operated instances;
- number of successful exits / migrations / forks;
- proportion of core decisions reproducible from public rules rather than private interpretation.

## Falsification examples

A proposed propagation loop is weakened when:

- demonstrations generate views but not examinations;
- examinations generate no pilots;
- pilots require permanent founder operation;
- revenue does not reduce future deployment cost;
- integrations increase maintenance faster than utility;
- more SmartVote data decreases decision quality or increases gaming;
- language count rises while usable quality or adoption does not;
- federation increases dependency on one central service;
- documentation volume increases due-diligence time.

The objective is to discover **which loops actually close in practice**.
