# kOA Propagation Loop Catalog

This catalog is a map of **candidate and partially evidenced loops**, not a claim that all loops are already active at scale.

| Loop | Core sequence | Primary governor |
|---|---|---|
| Build | tools → faster building → better tools | test / architecture discipline |
| Knowledge | use → result → memory → better next cycle | provenance / plurality |
| Economic | public software → adoption → paid responsibility → revenue → common capability | money ≠ authority |
| Credibility | real result → evidence → credibility → access → more real tests | evidence-state discipline |
| Demonstration | sources → World → cinematic demo → examination → pilot → better World | simulation ≠ evidence |
| SmartVote | objects → evaluations → signal → discovery → utility → more objects | domain bounds / raw baseline |
| Kintsugi | integration → capability → adoption → integration demand | replaceability / sidecar boundaries |
| Replication | packaging → independent instance → operational learning → easier next instance | local autonomy / no mandatory center |
| Human capacity | training → operator → deployment → experience → better training | independent assessment |
| Multilingual | language support → community access → contribution → better language resources | conformance tiers |
| Narrative | architecture → cultural form → understanding → technical exploration → new evidence/story | narrative ≠ factual authority |
| Machine-readable review | structured docs → AI-assisted review → questions / critique → better docs | source / status discipline |
| Movement self-use | contributors → kOA workflow → results → better kOA → more capable contributors | counterpower / fork / founder decentering |
| Founder decentering | founder work → protocol / docs / tools → autonomous operators → lower founder bottleneck | opposition + independent reproduction |
| Federation | local value → independent instances → interoperable exchange → network value → more local adoption | optional federation / exit |
| Cross-branch subsidy | branch return → shared asset → another branch → new return | conversion firewalls |

## High-value couplings

### Economic × Build × Deployment

`managed service → revenue → tooling → lower operating cost → more managed service`

### Credibility × Demonstration × Institutional Access

`pilot → evidence → credibility → better-targeted demonstration → qualified institutional review → pilot`

### UCKK × Capsule × Founder Decentering

`training → operator → reproducible deployment → less founder labor → more deployments → training cases`

### Kintsugi × SmartVote × Kompendio

`more connected objects/systems → more objects to compare → better contextual discovery → more integration utility`

### SemantiK × Community × Knowledge

`language support → new contributors → new knowledge / validation → more multilingual value → stronger language support`

The next stage for Power Dynamics is not to add more loops indefinitely. It is to instrument these transitions and determine which couplings materially alter adoption, replication, and autonomy.

# Propagation

This directory studies how capability becomes easier to activate **elsewhere**.

Power Dynamics already treats returns and reinvestment as sources of compounding. Propagation adds a different question:

> **When can a return generated in one actor, branch, domain, or deployment increase the effective possibility of capability in another?**

## Reading order

1. [`00-field-definition.md`](00-field-definition.md) — compounding vs propagation vs coupled propagation.
2. [`01-coupled-feedback-loops.md`](01-coupled-feedback-loops.md) — why cross-loop links matter.
3. [`02-adoption-stack.md`](02-adoption-stack.md) — activation barriers from understanding to operation.
4. [`03-cross-branch-returns.md`](03-cross-branch-returns.md) — money, evidence, code, credibility, people, and access moving between branches.
5. [`04-distribution-surfaces.md`](04-distribution-surfaces.md) — Worlds, cinematic demonstrations, narrative, machine-readable docs, and multiple entry paths.
6. [`05-autonomy-and-founder-absence.md`](05-autonomy-and-founder-absence.md) — growth that lowers rather than deepens founder dependency.
7. [`06-damping-and-runaway-prevention.md`](06-damping-and-runaway-prevention.md) — negative feedback, firewalls, optionality, and local sovereignty.
8. [`07-metrics-and-falsification.md`](07-metrics-and-falsification.md) — what to measure.

The top-level canonical summary is [`../PROPAGATION.md`](../PROPAGATION.md).

# Forms of Puissance

This directory names the **positive capability side of the Power Show**.

**Puissance** is effective mobilizable capacity. Before asking who controls whom, Power Dynamics asks what an actor, group, institution, or infrastructure can actually do.

## Agency and cognitive puissance

Choose, refuse, learn, reason, synthesize, model, plan, create, evaluate, and use governed AI assistance.

## Epistemic and semantic puissance

Produce, structure, validate, compare, translate, preserve, contest, and evolve knowledge and meaning.

## Collective, network, and coordination puissance

Find people, combine distributed expertise, form groups, coordinate roles, synchronize action, and create capability no participant possessed alone.

## Deliberative and decision puissance

Compare alternatives, preserve reasons, incorporate advisory expertise, and choose through explicit procedures.

## Operational and administrative puissance

Route work, assign responsibility, manage timing, escalate, execute, review, and close loops.

## Mnemonic puissance

Preserve provenance, decisions, actions, failures, outcomes, and lineage so the next cycle starts from reusable memory.

## Economic and allocation puissance

Mobilize material resources, funding, productive assets, and allocation mechanisms.

## Technical and infrastructural puissance

Build, inspect, modify, test, reproduce, repair, deploy, self-host, and maintain systems that lower future coordination costs.

## Distribution, brand, and affiliation puissance

Transmit knowledge, make people or projects discoverable, create public recognition, affiliation, and reach across communities and languages.

## Federation, exit, and evolutionary puissance

Operate independently, substitute dependencies, fork, gather support, preserve interoperability, recombine, and replace dominant branches.

These forms can compose. They become **power relations** only when their exercise materially changes another actor's access, options, standing, visibility, allocation, authority, dependency, interpretation, or ability to act.

See [`../showcase/01-capability-spectrum.md`](../showcase/01-capability-spectrum.md) for the kOA-facing presentation.

# Power Dynamics

**The kOA Power Show — and a systems theory of how capability becomes power, compounds across domains, and can be deliberately redistributed.**

## Why is kOA immensely powerful?

> **Because kOA does not amplify one human capability. It connects almost the entire lifecycle by which distributed resources become collective agency, reusable assets, and greater future capability.**

The shortest formula is:

> **Breadth × Composition × Closure × Memory × Compounding × Propagation × Replication × Routing**

```text
People + knowledge + AI + networks + credibility + money + tools
                         ↓
                STRUCTURE + CONNECT
                         ↓
Meaning → Knowledge → Discovery → Credibility → Judgment
                         ↓
              Decision → Allocation
                         ↓
            Coordination → Execution
                         ↓
            Results → Memory → Returns
                         ↓
                  Reinvestment
                         ↓
            Greater future puissance
                         ↓
             Replication / federation
                         ↓
        Sharing / contestation / evolution
```

kOA is therefore not best understood as a collection of apps. It is an attempt to build a **full-stack capability infrastructure** for the cycle:

> **Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better**

A collective that can reliably close that cycle can become more capable **because it has acted before**.

## The kOA power surface

kOA can generate or amplify capability across many domains at once:

| Domain | What kOA can make more possible | Power relation that can emerge |
|---|---|---|
| **Epistemic** | validate, compare, preserve, reuse knowledge | validation / canon / trust-root power |
| **Semantic** | define, relate, translate, version meaning | classification / framing / admissibility power |
| **Cognitive / AI** | reason, synthesize, model, translate, plan | hidden framing / prioritization / dependency power |
| **Information / attention** | publish, discover, rank, distribute | visibility / agenda / amplification power |
| **Credibility / reputation** | make demonstrated expertise and contribution legible | standing / certification / opportunity power |
| **Network / affiliation** | find people, collaborators, institutions, audiences | brokerage / inclusion / affiliation power |
| **Political / constitutional** | deliberate, advise, decide, amend, organize public standing | agenda / decision / mandate / legitimacy power |
| **Economic** | mobilize money, assets, labor, funding, infrastructure | bargaining / property / allocation power |
| **Monetary** | organize liquidity, payment rails, settlement, credit or currency functions | issuance / access / settlement / reserve power |
| **Administrative / operational** | assign, route, escalate, execute, close | timing / workload / approval / closure power |
| **Technical / infrastructural** | build, deploy, verify, self-host, reproduce | compatibility / access / update / dependency power |
| **Narrative / symbolic / brand** | make systems memorable, transmissible, affiliative | attention / legitimacy-by-association / mobilization power |
| **Mnemonic / temporal** | preserve reasons, precedent, failure, history, timing | precedent / memory / delay / succession power |
| **Evolutionary / counterpower** | contest, fork, substitute, recombine, replace | incumbent displacement / branch mobility / exit power |

See [`POWER-SURFACE.md`](POWER-SURFACE.md) for the full map.

## Political, economic, and monetary power are not the same

Power Dynamics now separates these explicitly.

### Political power

Political power concerns **standing, agenda, mandate, decision rights, legitimacy, representation, amendment, veto, and collective self-government**.

kOA touches political power before and after formal voting:

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

A system can preserve one-person-one-vote while still concentrating enormous power upstream in classification, discovery, credibility, defaults, or agenda formation. That is why kOA treats the pre-political chain as part of governance.

### Economic power

Economic power concerns **money, property, productive assets, funding, labor, infrastructure, bargaining position, and resource allocation**.

Money is not identical to power. It is a highly convertible resource that can become economic puissance and then relational leverage.

```text
money → economic puissance → purchasing / bargaining / allocation effects
```

### Monetary power

Monetary power is narrower and more infrastructural. It concerns control over **units of account, issuance, payment rails, settlement, reserves, liquidity, credit conditions, convertibility, and access to exchange**.

```text
monetary infrastructure
→ conditions of exchange
→ dependency / access / allocation effects
```

KingCoin is retained as a **LEGACY-PROPOSAL / stress test** precisely because it demonstrates how monetary, economic, administrative, and political functions can stack into terminal power if they are fused.

See [`showcase/13-political-economic-monetary-power.md`](showcase/13-political-economic-monetary-power.md).

## The real multiplication effect: composition

The power of kOA is not the sum of independent modules.

```text
SemantiK / SenTient  → semantic structuration
Kristal              → structured knowledge + provenance + memory
Konnaxion            → circulation + association + discoverability
Koali / EkoH         → credibility / expertise legibility
SmartVote            → comparative judgment + decision support
Orgo                 → governed execution
AI                    → cognitive amplification
Capsules / federation→ portability + replication + substitution

                    ↓ composition ↓

Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better
```

When outputs from one capability become inputs to another, the system can create **generated collective puissance** that no component or participant possesses alone.

See [`showcase/03-composition-engine.md`](showcase/03-composition-engine.md).

## The second multiplication effect: compounding

Action can leave behind reusable assets:

```text
experience
knowledge
code
tools
documentation
data
provenance
credibility
reputation
relationships
money
infrastructure
institutional memory
```

Those returns can feed the next cycle:

> **Puissance → Action → Result → Return → Reinvestment → Greater Future Puissance**

This is why kOA can become more capable over time instead of resetting after each project.

See [`showcase/04-compounding-engine.md`](showcase/04-compounding-engine.md).


## The propagation effect: returns can activate capability elsewhere

Compounding is not the same as propagation.

```text
compounding: A acts → A receives a reusable return → A becomes more capable
propagation: A acts → a reusable return lowers the activation barrier for B
coupled propagation: A strengthens B → B strengthens C → C returns capability to A
```

This matters for kOA because different branches can return different assets — money, evidence, credibility, code, tools, operators, integrations, language resources, documentation, and institutional access — and those assets can be reused by other branches.

The result can be a network of coupled loops rather than one growth flywheel.

See [`PROPAGATION.md`](PROPAGATION.md) and [`showcase/15-propagation-engine.md`](showcase/15-propagation-engine.md).

## The third multiplication effect: cross-domain conversion

Power rarely stays in one domain.

```text
knowledge → credibility → visibility → opportunity
money → infrastructure → reach → political leverage
brand → attention → affiliation → revenue
technical control → defaults → dependency → governance leverage
network → collaborators → execution → results → credibility
```

This is where successful capability can become dangerous. A legitimate function can acquire leverage outside its mandate.

> **Capture = Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power**

Power Dynamics therefore studies both **conversion paths** and **Power Conversion Firewalls**.

See [`framework/10-cross-domain-power-conversion.md`](framework/10-cross-domain-power-conversion.md).

## The fourth multiplication effect: power routing and sharing

The strongest version of the project is not:

> **kOA becomes immensely powerful.**

It is:

> **kOA generates immense puissance while deliberately routing much of that capability, optionality, authority, and return outward.**

Examples:

- **ethical fiction as self-binding** — publish anti-domination, attribution, revision, humility, and ethical principles inside the same symbolic channel that generates attention;
- **protocolization** — convert founder mastery into explicit schemas, contracts, documentation, tests, and independently reproducible behavior;
- **Koali ownership decoupling** — design credibility capability to be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions rather than terminal personal ownership;
- **expertise → advice, not sovereignty** — preserve political equality while exposing bounded informed readings;
- **money → funding, not more votes** — let economic resources increase common capability without automatically purchasing political authority;
- **knowledge → portable artifacts, not ownership of truth** — lineage, provenance, competing Kristals, reader policies;
- **infrastructure → self-hosting / federation, not captivity** — portability, capsules, rollback, substitution, local continuity;
- **dominance → branch mobility** — alternatives can demonstrate value, accumulate support, coexist, merge, or replace incumbents.

The canonical sequence becomes:

> **Generate → Compose → Close → Preserve → Compound → Propagate → Convert → Route → Share → Bind → Contest → Fork → Evolve**

See [`POWER-ROUTING.md`](POWER-ROUTING.md).

## Academic lineage and external challenge

Power Dynamics now includes a structured [`Academic-Lineage/`](Academic-Lineage/README.md) layer connecting the framework to established work on relational power, dependency, polycentric governance, institutional grammar, modularity, standards lock-in, path dependence, exit / voice, open-source governance, network power, expertise, collective judgment, metric gaming, classification, infrastructure, social epistemology, and cybernetics.

The purpose is **not** to claim endorsement or retrospective influence. The literature is used to name known failure modes, supply established mechanisms, expose impossibility constraints, and sharpen the tests that kOA must survive.

A recurring result is that the architecture must distinguish:

- capability from dependency;
- expertise from political sovereignty;
- compatibility from constitutional subordination;
- decentralization from absence of hidden standards power;
- reputation from truth;
- forkability from practical exit;
- provenance from epistemic authority.

See [`Academic-Lineage/README.md`](Academic-Lineage/README.md) and [`Academic-Lineage/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md`](Academic-Lineage/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md).

## Power must survive adversarial use

A non-dominating architecture should not depend on benevolent actors, perfect founders, or honest knowledge producers. Power Dynamics now makes three resilience tests explicit:

- **founder opposition** — can participants reject a founder-backed proposal without abandoning the whole system?
- **epistemic corruption** — can misleading authority signals, dependent sources, and false consensus be exposed through provenance and comparison?
- **plural worldview branching** — can incompatible Kristals coexist without turning forkability into a claim of equal evidentiary support?

These are treated as power questions because symbolic authority, credibility, and reference stewardship can all convert into leverage outside their intended domain. See [`cases/founder-opposition-rehearsal.md`](cases/founder-opposition-rehearsal.md) and [`cases/epistemic-authority-corruption.md`](cases/epistemic-authority-corruption.md).

## Power Conversion Firewalls

kOA's constitutional goal is not to prevent useful forms of puissance from interacting. It is to prevent useful capability from silently becoming unrelated sovereignty.

| Legitimate conversion | Blocked automatic conversion |
|---|---|
| expertise → advice | expertise → political sovereignty |
| money → funding | money → more votes |
| brand → attention | brand → truth |
| credibility → discoverability | credibility → unquestionable authority |
| narrative → mobilization | narrative → legitimacy |
| technical administration → maintenance | technical control → political outcome control |
| execution → mandate implementation | execution → mandate redefinition |
| infrastructure → capability | infrastructure → ownership of users |

> **Composition without automatic conversion.**

## Why the constitution exists

> **The constitution exists because the Power Show succeeds.**

If kOA can structure meaning, knowledge, discovery, credibility, deliberation, decisions, allocation, execution, memory, infrastructure, and social transmission, then it can also create serious relations of power.

The normative target is:

> **Maximize distributed puissance while constraining arbitrary, cross-domain, and terminal power.**

The compressed doctrine is:

> **Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.**  
> **Stable interoperability. Evolving branches.**  
> **Plurality without fragmentation.**  
> **Verification + Contestation + Substitution + Exit.**  
> **Instance capture ≠ system capture.**

## Start here

1. [`showcase/00-why-koa-is-immensely-powerful.md`](showcase/00-why-koa-is-immensely-powerful.md) — the shortest complete answer.
2. [`POWER-SURFACE.md`](POWER-SURFACE.md) — every major domain of capability and power.
3. [`POWER-SHOW.md`](POWER-SHOW.md) — the guided kOA power show.
4. [`showcase/03-composition-engine.md`](showcase/03-composition-engine.md) — why the whole can exceed the sum.
5. [`showcase/04-compounding-engine.md`](showcase/04-compounding-engine.md) — how power regenerates.
6. [`showcase/13-political-economic-monetary-power.md`](showcase/13-political-economic-monetary-power.md) — political, economic, and monetary distinctions.
7. [`POWER-ROUTING.md`](POWER-ROUTING.md) — how generated power is redirected and shared.
8. [`constitution/README.md`](constitution/README.md) — how the architecture constrains the power it generates.
9. [`framework/README.md`](framework/README.md) — the analytical engine.
10. [`GLOSSARY.md`](GLOSSARY.md) — canonical terminology.

## Analytical engine

The broader Power Dynamics field studies how resources and capability:

> **form, mobilize, compose, convert, return, compound, concentrate, capture, contest, diversify, evolve, decay, transfer, scale, and route.**

Its precision ontology remains:

> **Resource / Asset → Potential → Capacity → Puissance → Power Relation → Effect → Return**

Its three analytical axes remain:

1. **Ontological state** — what kind of thing is this?
2. **Dynamic** — what is happening to it?
3. **Domain** — where does the capability or power operate?

See [`framework/03-three-axis-model.md`](framework/03-three-axis-model.md).

## Epistemic discipline

Every substantive document should distinguish:

- `maturity` — current core, experimental, open question, legacy proposal, etc.;
- `claim_type` — source-derived, analytical reconstruction, new concept, external research, or architect-confirmed design.

A powerful claim is not treated as an implemented fact merely because it is architecturally desirable. Koali protocolization, for example, remains an architect-confirmed design direction until the required technical evidence exists.

See [`sources/epistemic-rules.md`](sources/epistemic-rules.md) and [`sources/technical-evidence-map.md`](sources/technical-evidence-map.md).

# Power Relations

The Power Show maps **what actors become able to do**. This directory maps the next question:

> **Whose possibilities change because that capability exists or is controlled?**

Power is relational: mobilized puissance becomes power when it changes another actor's effective possibilities.

## Enabling and coordination power

Relations that expand possibilities by granting access, recognition, coordination, legal standing, interoperability, or infrastructure use. Even enabling relations can become dependency when one actor becomes the only viable gateway.

## Access, gatekeeping, and allocation power

Control over entry conditions and distribution of scarce resources, opportunities, credentials, services, funding, identity, or platform access.

## Visibility, ranking, agenda, and framing power

Pre-political relations that shape what becomes seen, prominent, discussable, interpretable, fundable, reusable, or adoptable.

## Recognition, standing, and certification power

Relations that affect who is treated as competent, trustworthy, qualified, legitimate, or institutionally relevant.

## Semantic, constitutive, and standard-setting power

Definitions, classifications, ontologies, thresholds, translations, formats, trust roots, interoperability contracts, and admissibility rules.

## Authority, administration, and enforcement power

Mandates, routing, assignment, timing, escalation, closure, sanctions, compulsion, and denial of consequences.

## Dependency, bottleneck, lock-in, and terminal power

Relational leverage created when alternatives are costly, slow, non-portable, unrecognized, or unavailable. Terminal power is the limiting case where capture or failure cannot be effectively verified, contested, substituted, reversed, or exited.

## Veto, blocking, and delay power

Preventing, slowing, queuing, withholding approval, or increasing procedural cost. Time is a political variable.

Power relations are not automatically illegitimate. Assessment depends on mandate, scope, proportionality, transparency, alternatives, dependency, counterpower, and reversibility.

See [`../showcase/07-component-power-matrix.md`](../showcase/07-component-power-matrix.md) for how these relations emerge around kOA components.

# Disciplines That Study Power

Power Dynamics is interdisciplinary. Relevant fields include political science and political theory, sociology, sociology of organizations, political economy, institutional economics, law, public administration, organizational theory, network science, game theory, social choice, media and communication, information science, knowledge management, social epistemology, science and technology studies, infrastructure studies, platform governance, cybernetics, systems theory, complexity science, anthropology, social psychology, social movement theory, diffusion research, capability theory, finance, and monetary studies.

The distinctive ambition of this repo is to connect **capability formation, relational power, feedback, conversion, and constitutional evolution** across these domains.

The structured cross-disciplinary map now lives in [`../Academic-Lineage/README.md`](../Academic-Lineage/README.md).

# Literature Roadmap

The initial literature roadmap has been promoted into a structured academic lineage.

Start with:

- [`../Academic-Lineage/README.md`](../Academic-Lineage/README.md) — crosswalk from academic problem to Power Dynamics mechanism;
- [`../Academic-Lineage/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md`](../Academic-Lineage/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md) — selective bibliography;
- [`../Academic-Lineage/06-expertise-smartvote-and-collective-judgment.md`](../Academic-Lineage/06-expertise-smartvote-and-collective-judgment.md) — SmartVote / expertise lineage;
- [`../Academic-Lineage/03-modularity-standards-and-path-dependence.md`](../Academic-Lineage/03-modularity-standards-and-path-dependence.md) — protocol power, installed-base power, lock-in and ossification;
- [`../Academic-Lineage/01-power-dependence-and-domination.md`](../Academic-Lineage/01-power-dependence-and-domination.md) — relational power, dependency, resource dependence and non-domination.

## Next research priorities

1. **Domain-scoped expertise** — expand empirical literature on transfer limits, calibration, forecasting, and expert-selection mechanisms.
2. **Protocol / standards governance** — standards bodies, Internet ossification, backwards compatibility, migration governance, and interface control.
3. **Metric gaming** — Goodhart/Campbell effects, reputation markets, adversarial optimization, sybil resistance, and cumulative advantage.
4. **Epistemic democracy** — expertise, political equality, cognitive diversity, deliberation, and institutional design.
5. **Platform power** — ranking, moderation, recommender systems, identity, app stores, payment rails, and infrastructural dependency.
6. **Digital commons** — Ostrom-inspired governance of software, data, compute, schemas, models, and shared registries.
7. **AI-mediated authority** — when model outputs, defaults, summaries, or agentic execution become hidden decision rights.

Future work should continue asking:

> **What does each theory explain that Power Dynamics misses, and what does Power Dynamics connect that each theory usually treats separately?**

# Research Layer

This directory is the entry point for external scholarship that can challenge, refine, or situate Power Dynamics.

The structured academic inventory now lives in [`../Academic-Lineage/`](../Academic-Lineage/README.md).

External literature is deliberately separated from the supplied kOA corpus so the repository does not silently replace source-derived concepts with general academic knowledge.

The governing rule is:

> **Academic correspondence is not endorsement, historical influence, or implementation validation.**

Use the literature to identify known failure modes, established mechanisms, impossibility results, and useful comparative vocabulary.

# Resources and Stocks

Resources are inputs, not power by definition. A stock becomes politically significant only through the capacities, conversions, dependencies, and relations it supports.

## Material, financial, and time resources

Money, property, energy, equipment, housing, physical infrastructure, and time are foundational because many other capacities depend on them. Time is especially political: formal rights can be unusable when work, debt, bureaucracy, care burdens, or procedure consume the time needed to exercise them. Financial resources are unusually convertible because they can purchase labor, infrastructure, attention, expertise, legal support, mobility, or time.

## Knowledge, expertise, and memory

Knowledge can be shared without consuming the original resource, which makes it strongly generative. Expertise is not identical to credentials or reputation; it includes accumulated practice, methods, understanding, and evidence. Memory includes decisions, reasons, failures, outcomes, and operational traces. When made reusable, memory lowers the cost of future action.

## Networks, reputation, and brand

Relationships, trust, reputation, affiliations, and brand recognition can lower coordination cost and open pathways to attention, collaboration, funding, and institutional access. They are not automatically authority. These resources are especially prone to positive feedback and incumbency effects.

## Technical, data, and infrastructure

Code, schemas, hardware, APIs, data, keys, registries, protocols, and operational knowledge can be small artifacts with large systemic effects. Their significance depends on custody, reproducibility, substitution, recovery, and whether others must pass through them.

## Identity, rights, and optionality

Identity, privacy, credentials, legal rights, licenses, and preserved choices condition future action. Optionality is valuable because action has not yet been irreversibly committed. A private identity or unpublished corpus can preserve future timing and strategy. Optionality is potential until it becomes effectively usable.

# Why kOA Is Immensely Powerful

> **kOA is immensely powerful because it connects the full lifecycle of collective capability and makes the outputs of one cycle usable as inputs to the next.**

## Seven multipliers

### 1. Breadth

kOA touches many domains at once: meaning, knowledge, cognition, discovery, credibility, networks, politics, economics, coordination, execution, memory, infrastructure, narrative, federation, and evolution.

### 2. Composition

Those capabilities can strengthen one another.

```text
structured knowledge
+ AI
+ credibility
+ discoverability
+ decision
+ workflow
+ memory
= generated collective puissance
```

### 3. Closure

kOA attempts to close the loop:

> **Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better**

Information can become judgment; judgment can become action; action can become reusable memory.

### 4. Memory

The system can preserve reasons, evidence, provenance, decisions, failures, outcomes, and implementation history instead of forcing every cycle to restart.

### 5. Compounding

Action can leave assets behind:

> **Action → Return → Asset → Reinvestment → Greater Future Puissance**

### 6. Replication

Portability, self-hosting, capsules, federation, and branchability can transform one functioning capability into many independently operated capabilities.

### 7. Routing

kOA does not only ask how to generate leverage. It also creates mechanisms to redirect it:

- expertise → advice rather than sovereignty;
- money → funding rather than more votes;
- narrative → mobilization plus ethical self-binding rather than unquestionable legitimacy;
- Koali → plural distributions rather than one terminal credibility owner;
- infrastructure → self-hosting and substitution rather than captivity;
- dominance → branch mobility rather than final authority.

## The master loop

```text
Distributed resources
→ meaning
→ structured knowledge
→ discovery
→ credibility
→ deliberation
→ decision / allocation
→ coordination
→ execution
→ results
→ memory / returns
→ reinvestment
→ greater capability
→ federation / replication
→ contestation / evolution
```

This is why the system can become more capable **because it has already acted**.

## The central warning

The same architecture that increases collective capability can also create:

- semantic power;
- agenda power;
- political power;
- economic and monetary leverage;
- administrative power;
- infrastructural dependency;
- narrative capture;
- founder power;
- standards and registry power.

Therefore:

> **The Power Show and the Power Constitution are the same project viewed from opposite directions.**

The first asks how much capability can be generated. The second asks whether those capabilities remain governable.

# The kOA Capability Spectrum

The Power Show begins by separating **what kOA helps actors do** from **what authority any actor has over others**.

The first category is **puissance**: effectively mobilizable capacity.

## Epistemic puissance

Create, structure, compare, verify, preserve, cite, derive, and reuse knowledge. Kristal is the primary architectural locus.

## Semantic puissance

Make categories, meanings, relations, ambiguity, translation, and ontology explicit enough to inspect and evolve. SemantiK / SenTient are central here.

## Cognitive puissance

Reason, synthesize, model, plan, generate, evaluate, and learn at greater throughput. AI can amplify this capability, but its outputs remain subordinate to explicit governance and provenance.

## Individual agency

Understand options, make informed choices, refuse, exit, learn, publish, organize, and preserve personal or institutional optionality.

## Collective puissance

Combine people who individually possess only partial knowledge, time, skills, resources, and relationships into coordinated capability.

## Network puissance

Find expertise, collaborators, communities, institutions, supporters, customers, contributors, or alternative branches. Network capability changes what can be attempted.

## Discoverability puissance

Make relevant knowledge, people, needs, proposals, branches, and evidence findable. Discoverability is empowering before it becomes a ranking or agenda relation.

## Credibility puissance

Make evidence of contribution, expertise, reliability, or domain-bounded competence legible enough to be used. `Score ≠ competence`; credibility signals are governed readings, not persons.

## Deliberative and decision puissance

Compare alternatives, preserve baseline equality, add advisory expertise without silently replacing the baseline, and make reasons inspectable.

## Political and constitutional puissance

Form agendas, organize public standing, deliberate, decide, authorize, amend, contest mandates, and connect collective judgment to binding action without reducing politics to the final vote.

## Coordination puissance

Sequence actors and information so that many partial capabilities produce coherent action.

## Operational puissance

Route work, create ownership, set escalation paths, manage time windows, review outcomes, and close loops.

## Mnemonic puissance

Preserve reasons, provenance, decisions, actions, failures, outcomes, versions, and lineage so future action begins with memory rather than amnesia.

## Economic puissance

Mobilize money, property, funding, labor, contracts, productive assets, and infrastructure for action. Economic resources become power when their control materially changes others' options or bargaining positions.

## Allocation puissance

Pool, prioritize, and direct resources toward people, projects, institutions, or goals. Economic contribution can support collective action without automatically purchasing political authority.

## Monetary and liquidity puissance

Create, access, move, settle, convert, intermediate, or finance value through payment, credit, liquidity, settlement, or currency infrastructure. Monetary capability is distinct from general economic capability and carries unusually high dependency risk when rails become unavoidable.

## Technical puissance

Build, inspect, modify, test, reproduce, repair, deploy, integrate, and maintain systems.

## Infrastructural puissance

Create shared capability that lowers the cost of future action for many actors: protocols, schemas, identity contracts, deployment mechanisms, knowledge formats, workflow primitives.

## Distribution puissance

Move knowledge and coordination across languages, communities, institutions, and geographic boundaries.

## Brand and affiliation puissance

Create recognition, public signaling, identity, community affiliation, and transmission. Brand can create attention and funding without becoming truth or sovereignty.

## Federation puissance

Operate local instances while preserving interoperability, lineage, portability, and the possibility of alternative governance.

## Exit and substitution puissance

Leave, replace a dependency, preserve assets, self-host, migrate, or continue through another implementation.

## Forking puissance

Create an alternative implementation, policy, ontology, credibility model, reader policy, governance model, or knowledge representation.

## Evolutionary puissance

Do more than fork: demonstrate improvement, gather support, remain interoperable, recombine useful ideas, and allow dominant branches to change or be replaced.

## The key distinction

A capability is not automatically a relation of power.

> **The Power Show maps what actors become able to do. Power Dynamics then asks whose possibilities change because they can do it.**

# From Individual to Federation

kOA's power show is also a **scale show**. The same architecture can increase capability at different social scales without assuming that every scale requires the same center.

## Individual

Potential gains include:

- better access to structured knowledge;
- stronger personal memory and provenance;
- AI-assisted cognition;
- better discovery of expertise and opportunities;
- easier publication and reuse;
- more informed choice;
- greater exit and optionality.

## Group

A group can gain capabilities no member has alone:

- shared context;
- distributed expertise;
- collective comparison;
- task routing;
- explicit ownership;
- coordination;
- reusable memory;
- stronger network reach.

This is **generated collective puissance**.

## Organization

At organizational scale, the architecture adds:

- roles and mandates;
- governed workflows;
- escalation;
- decision records;
- institutional memory;
- auditability;
- repeatable operating patterns;
- succession and continuity.

## Federation

A federation can add:

- local sovereignty over local operation;
- shared interoperability contracts;
- portable knowledge;
- distributed deployment;
- branchable governance;
- independent continuity;
- cross-instance learning;
- substitution of providers or implementations.

The target architecture is:

> **StrongHub + DistributedCapability + ReplicableWhole**

This permits useful centrality without making the center the sole source of truth, continuity, identity, or capability.

## Scaling without scaling sovereignty

The important design challenge is:

> **Can capability scale faster than dependency on any single sovereign node?**

That is why portability, federation, explicit authority scope, provenance, branch mobility, and local operation belong inside the power show rather than being afterthoughts.

# The Composition Engine

kOA becomes significantly more powerful when distinct capabilities **compose**.

Composition means that one subsystem's output becomes another subsystem's usable input without requiring one subsystem to own the others.

> **Composition does not require ownership.**

## The chain

```text
Contribution
→ semantic resolution
→ structured knowledge
→ discoverability
→ credibility context
→ deliberation / comparison
→ collective choice
→ governed work
→ coordinated action
→ outcome
→ reusable memory
→ new cycle
```

Each step removes a different failure mode.

## Why the whole can exceed the parts

A large knowledge base without discovery can remain unused.

A network without credible information can coordinate noise.

Credibility without deliberation can become status hierarchy.

A good decision without workflow can remain aspirational.

Execution without memory can repeatedly pay the same learning cost.

Memory without provenance can fossilize error.

Forkability without interoperability can fragment.

The composition engine matters because it joins these functions while preserving the possibility that each function remains governed separately.

## Generated collective puissance

Suppose ten people each have:

- incomplete knowledge;
- limited time;
- different skills;
- different contacts;
- different resources.

Without coordination, their resources remain partly latent.

With shared artifacts, discovery, explicit credibility context, a choice mechanism, task routing, and persistent memory, the group can perform actions unavailable to any individual participant.

That capability is **generated**, not merely transferred.

## Multiplicative intuition

A useful heuristic is:

```text
collective effectiveness
≈ knowledge quality
× discoverability
× coordination
× decision quality
× execution
× memory reuse
```

This is not a literal universal formula. It expresses a systems insight: a severe failure in one necessary transition can suppress the value of strong capabilities elsewhere.

## Constitutional consequence

Because composition increases capability, the seams among components become political boundaries.

Who controls:

- discovery;
- identity;
- credibility interpretation;
- defaults;
- routing;
- signatures;
- update channels;
- authority registries;
- memory;
- migration?

Every composition seam is therefore both a capability interface and a possible power interface.

# The Compounding Engine

The strongest form of capability is not one-time capability. It is capability that leaves behind assets that lower the cost or raise the quality of future action.

## Core loop

> **Action → Result → Return → Asset → Reinvestment → Greater Future Puissance**

Possible returns include:

- knowledge;
- experience;
- code;
- tools;
- infrastructure;
- documentation;
- provenance;
- reputation;
- credibility;
- network relationships;
- data;
- institutional access;
- financial resources;
- memory;
- optionality.

## Why memory matters

Without reusable memory:

```text
Act → learn → forget → pay again
```

With reusable memory:

```text
Act → learn → preserve → reuse → start higher
```

This is why Kristal and operational memory are not passive archives. They are future capability infrastructure.

## Tools as crystallized past puissance

A tool records prior effort in a form that can be reused.

```text
past time + knowledge + experimentation
→ code / procedure / infrastructure
→ lower future effort
→ greater future capacity
```

The same applies to documentation, standards, schemas, trusted relationships, and institutional routines.

## Regenerative vs extractive loops

An extractive loop looks like:

```text
collective activity
→ value
→ narrow owner
→ more owner capacity
```

A regenerative loop looks like:

```text
collective activity
→ value
→ participants + shared assets + common infrastructure
→ more distributed capability
```

The constitutional question is therefore:

> **Who receives the returns generated by an exercise of collective puissance?**

## kOA transition

A founder can bootstrap an ecosystem through concentrated effort. Long-term resilience requires a transition from:

> **Founder-powered → System-powered ecosystem**

The test is whether accumulated assets become usable, maintainable, forkable, and improvable by others without requiring unique founder knowledge or permission.

# Capability and Power Conversion Chains

Power rarely remains in one domain. Resources and forms of puissance can be converted into other resources, opportunities, and relations of power.

## Common chains

```text
knowledge
→ credibility
→ visibility
→ opportunity
→ resources
→ better tools
→ more knowledge
```

```text
brand
→ attention
→ affiliation
→ sales / funding
→ infrastructure
→ reach
→ stronger brand
```

```text
network
→ collaborators
→ execution
→ results
→ reputation
→ broader network
```

```text
technical control
→ control of defaults
→ user dependence
→ agenda influence
```

```text
money
→ infrastructure
→ distribution
→ attention
→ political influence
```

## Conversion is not automatically illegitimate

Systems need conversion. Expertise should influence advice. Money should fund work. Reputation can help discovery. Technical administration is needed to maintain infrastructure.

The danger is **automatic cross-domain conversion**.

Examples:

- expertise becoming political sovereignty;
- money becoming more votes;
- brand becoming truth;
- technical maintenance becoming control of political outcomes;
- credibility becoming unquestionable authority;
- infrastructure becoming ownership of users.

That is why Power Dynamics uses **conversion firewalls** rather than trying to eliminate conversion itself.

## Power stacking

When one actor controls several conversion points, legitimate functions can stack into cross-domain leverage.

```text
finance
+ identity
+ certification
+ platform access
+ philanthropy
→ high cross-domain leverage
```

The issue is not that any one function is illegitimate. The issue is what the combined position makes possible.

# The Pre-Political Chain

Power begins before formal voting or command.

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

kOA touches nearly every step.

## Meaning

Categories determine what can be described, compared, included, or excluded. Semantic infrastructure can create semantic puissance; control over definitions can create semantic power.

## Knowledge

What becomes structured, provenance-bearing, canonical, disputed, or reusable affects later reasoning.

## Visibility

What is discoverable has a higher chance of being cited, funded, reused, adopted, or debated. Discoverability is therefore both capability and governance.

## Credibility

Signals about expertise or contribution can help groups navigate complexity. They can also affect opportunity and standing.

## Agenda

What is surfaced repeatedly becomes more likely to be treated as worthy of collective attention.

## Deliberation and decision

Decision tools can expand comparative capacity. Defaults, lenses, weighting, and presentation can also shape the decision environment.

## Execution

Routing, ownership, time, escalation, and closure determine whether a nominal decision becomes an effective outcome.

## Memory

What is preserved becomes precedent, evidence, training data, institutional learning, and the starting point for future decisions.

## Why this belongs in the Power Show

The impressive capability of kOA is precisely that it links these stages. The governance challenge is that a system spanning these stages could also accumulate pre-political leverage if the same node controls too many transitions.

# kOA Component Power Matrix

Every component should be read across five questions:

1. What resource does it organize?
2. What puissance does it generate?
3. In which domain does relational power emerge?
4. Into what other domains can that power convert?
5. How is capability routed or counterpower preserved?

| Component | Primary domains | Generated / amplified puissance | Possible relational power | Cross-domain conversion | Routing / counterweights |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SemantiK / SenTient | semantic, epistemic | classification, ambiguity resolution, translation | framing, admissibility, ontology power | meaning → agenda / enforcement | explicit ontologies, provenance, local governance, alternatives |
| Kristal | epistemic, mnemonic | structured knowledge, provenance, reusable memory | validation, canon, trust-root, standard-setting | knowledge → credibility → visibility | portability, lineage, competing Kristals, reader-policy plurality |
| Konnaxion | information, attention, network | circulation, association, discoverability | ranking, moderation, visibility, agenda, access | attention → affiliation / opportunity / politics | explicit policies, export, federation, alternative discovery paths |
| Koali / EkoH | credibility, reputation | evidence and expertise legibility | standing, certification, opportunity | credibility → visibility / funding / influence | domain bounds, portable evidence, plural distributions, contestation |
| SmartVote | political, deliberative | comparative judgment, multiple readings | lens, default, weighting, agenda influence | expertise → political interpretation | baseline preservation, explicit lenses, deterministic calculation |
| Orgo | administrative, operational | assignment, routing, escalation, execution, closure | timing, workload, approval, closure | administration → de facto policy | scoped roles, audit, appeal, revocability, mandate boundary |
| Capsules / federation | technical, infrastructural, evolutionary | deployment, replication, local continuity, substitution | signing, update, registry, compatibility | infrastructure → standards / dependency | verifiable packages, rollback, self-hosting, multiple operators |
| AI | cognitive, epistemic | reasoning, synthesis, translation, modeling | hidden framing, prioritization, interpretive dependency | cognition → judgment / execution | bounded roles, provenance, human/institutional authority |
| Brand / narrative | narrative, attention, network, economic | recognition, affiliation, transmission, fundraising | symbolic legitimacy, gatekeeping, attention | brand → affiliation → revenue / adoption | ethical self-binding, brand ≠ truth, protocol/brand separation |
| Multilingual layer | semantic, distribution | cross-language access, translation, reach | language inclusion/exclusion, translation framing | language → discoverability / standing | explicit source links, review, local semantic control |
| Zero-Skim mechanisms | economic, allocation, political | pooling and allocating resources | allocation, donor/funder leverage | money → projects / infrastructure | one-member-one-vote style safeguards, capital ≠ political standing |
| Koali protocolization | technical, credibility, evolutionary | reproducible credibility capability | registry / compatibility / standing power | founder control → protocol ecology | ownership decoupling, interoperability, branchability |

## The design goal

> **Increase generated puissance aggressively, expose cross-domain conversion, route capability deliberately, and keep resulting power contestable and non-terminal.**

# Governance After Capability

Power Dynamics does not treat governance as the mechanism that prevents kOA from becoming powerful.

It treats governance as the mechanism that allows kOA to become **very capable without making successful capability terminal**.

## The sequence

```text
Generate puissance
→ compose puissance
→ amplify and preserve it
→ observe emerging power relations
→ constrain arbitrary conversion
→ preserve counterpower
→ keep alternatives viable
→ let governance itself evolve
```

## Governance can redirect power before constraining it

The constitutional architecture has two jobs:

1. **redirect useful leverage toward distributed capability where possible;**
2. **preserve counterpower where concentration still occurs.**

Examples include protocolizing founder knowledge, making Koali plural and reproducible, publishing ethical constraints in narrative, separating expertise from sovereignty, and turning central infrastructure into self-hostable local capability.

See [`11-power-routing-and-sharing.md`](11-power-routing-and-sharing.md) and [`../constitution/power-redirection-sharing.md`](../constitution/power-redirection-sharing.md).

## Four core counterpowers

> **Verification + Contestation + Substitution + Exit**

### Verification
Can affected actors independently inspect the claim, rule, calculation, provenance, signature, workflow, or authority?

### Contestation
Can they challenge it through a meaningful process?

### Substitution
Can another provider, branch, reader, model, implementation, institution, or workflow perform the function?

### Exit
Can participants leave without disproportionate loss of identity, history, knowledge, reputation, data, rights, or continuity?

## Non-terminal architecture

No architecture can guarantee that capture never occurs.

The more realistic objective is:

> **Instance Capture ≠ System Capture**

A captured organization, deployment, branch, registry, or brand should not automatically capture the whole capability ecology.

## Conversion firewalls

Useful functional control should not silently become unrelated sovereignty.

```text
expertise → advice                 yes
expertise → permanent rule         no automatic path

money → contribution               yes
money → more political votes       no automatic path

technical administration → upkeep yes
technical administration → policy no automatic path
```

## Evolutionary governance

Even the constitution is an approximation.

Forks and branches are not only emergency escape valves. They are normal mechanisms of institutional experimentation.

The final safeguard is therefore not a perfect center. It is the preservation of a system in which alternatives can remain viable enough to challenge, recombine with, or replace dominant implementations.

# Why kOA Is Not Just Software

Software is one layer of kOA's capability architecture.

The broader system combines:

- knowledge structures;
- semantic conventions;
- provenance;
- credibility interpretations;
- coordination rules;
- decision procedures;
- workflows;
- institutional memory;
- identity and affiliation;
- deployment infrastructure;
- governance;
- branching and federation;
- human and AI cognition.

That means kOA can affect not only computation but **what a collective can know, notice, compare, decide, coordinate, preserve, and reproduce**.

## Capability infrastructure

A useful definition is:

> **kOA is capability infrastructure for converting knowledge into governed collective action and action back into reusable knowledge.**

Its deeper loop is:

```text
knowledge
→ orientation
→ choice
→ execution
→ experience
→ memory
→ better knowledge
```

## Why infrastructure matters politically

Infrastructure disappears into normality when it works. Once widely adopted, technical defaults and interfaces can become de facto political boundaries.

A powerful infrastructure may therefore create:

- standard-setting power;
- compatibility power;
- identity gatekeeping;
- discoverability power;
- administrative power;
- mnemonic power;
- dependency and lock-in.

The objective is not to avoid infrastructure. It is to make infrastructural puissance **governable, portable, inspectable, and non-terminal**.

# Counterpower and Optionality as Capability

The Power Show includes not only the ability to **do more**, but also the ability to **depend less**.

A system that can coordinate, rank, certify, route, or preserve memory is powerful. A participant who can verify, contest, substitute, exit, fork, or wait also possesses important capability.

## Verification puissance

Ability to independently inspect claims, provenance, signatures, rules, calculations, policies, and technical behavior.

## Contestation puissance

Ability to challenge a decision, score, category, workflow, authority, branch, or interpretation through a process capable of producing change.

## Substitution puissance

Ability to replace a provider, implementation, trust root, reader policy, workflow, branch, or institution with a viable alternative.

## Exit puissance

Ability to leave while preserving enough identity, history, knowledge, reputation, data, rights, relationships, and continuity for departure to be meaningful.

## Forking puissance

Ability to create an alternative rather than accept the existing implementation as terminal.

## Branch mobility

Ability to move beyond a symbolic fork: attract maintainers, preserve interoperability, gain discoverability, gather support, and become institutionally viable.

## Optionality puissance

Ability to preserve future choices rather than commit them prematurely.

Examples include:

- retained financial reserves;
- unpublished knowledge;
- unused deployment capacity;
- privacy and unrevealed identity;
- multiple providers;
- portable credentials;
- branchable software;
- independent infrastructure.

Optionality is important because **unexercised capability can still change future strategic position**.

## The inversion

Traditional power analysis often asks who can compel whom.

Power Dynamics also asks:

> **Who can refuse? Who can leave? Who can reproduce? Who can create an alternative? Who can preserve the option to act later?**

These are central forms of practical freedom and therefore belong inside the Power Show itself.

# Power Routing and Sharing

The strongest version of the kOA Power Show is not:

> **kOA accumulates enormous power.**

It is:

> **kOA can generate enormous collective puissance while deliberately routing much of the resulting capability, leverage, optionality, and authority outward.**

This is a distinct design achievement. Many systems empower users while quietly increasing the terminal power of the operator. Power Dynamics therefore asks two separate questions:

1. **What new puissance does the system create?**
2. **Where does the resulting leverage go?**

## Four things can be routed

### Capability

Who gains practical ability to know, build, decide, execute, remember, reproduce, and evolve?

### Authority

Who gains rights to validate, rank, approve, route, certify, update, moderate, or decide?

### Returns

Who receives the reusable assets generated by action — knowledge, money, reputation, network, code, data, memory, infrastructure, and opportunity?

### Optionality

Who gains the practical ability to refuse, leave, substitute, self-host, fork, wait, or build an alternative?

A system may distribute one while concentrating another. For example, it may distribute information while centralizing authority, or distribute software while centralizing identity and discoverability. A power-sharing audit must inspect all four.

## Core mechanisms

### 1. Narrative self-binding

Narrative and brand can create enormous attention and affiliation puissance. One kOA mechanism is to publish ethical limits **inside the same fiction that carries symbolic power**.

This is not a guarantee of good conduct. It is a public self-binding mechanism:

```text
symbolic influence
→ explicit ethical commitments
→ public expectations
→ criticism / citation / contestation
→ reduced interpretive freedom for arbitrary drift
```

The source corpus explicitly frames its ethical code around clarity of intention, respect for living beings, vigilance against monopoly and domination, recognition of contributors, self-mockery, revision, criticism, and a living code rather than dogma.

The important Power Dynamics insight is that **the narrative channel carries not only the message, but also constraints on how the message should be used**.

### 2. Protocolization

Founder knowledge can be converted into a dependency or into shared capability.

```text
founder insight
→ architecture
→ explicit interfaces
→ schemas / documentation / testable behavior
→ independent implementation
→ distributed mastery
```

Protocolization redirects founder-centric puissance toward maintainers, institutions, branches, and users who can reproduce the function without unique personal permission.

> **Founder → Architecture → Distributed Mastery**

### 3. Koali ownership decoupling

The current design intention is that Koali should **not remain an exclusive personal property that others must depend on**. Its useful logic should be capable of duplication, sharing, variation, and plural operation around compatible structures.

That means credibility infrastructure should tend toward:

```text
common / interoperable kernel
+ plural distributions
+ portable evidence / history
+ explicit policy identity
+ alternative operators
= plurality without fragmentation
```

This redirects recognition power from a single credibility owner toward an ecology of communities and distributions.

This design intent is architect-confirmed and should be backed by a canonical Koali distribution specification as the implementation matures.

### 4. Advice without sovereignty

EkoH and SmartVote illustrate a different redirection:

```text
expertise / credibility
→ informed advice
not
→ automatic political supremacy
```

A baseline political reading can remain visible while advisory readings expose competence or ethics under explicit policies. Expertise gains influence without silently inheriting sovereignty.

### 5. Portable epistemic capability

Kristal can redirect epistemic leverage away from a platform owner by making knowledge artifacts portable, attributable, versioned, auditable, and capable of lineage.

Competing Kristals and reader policies make disagreement representable rather than requiring one final representation to own the whole epistemic field.

### 6. Infrastructure → local autonomy

Self-hosting, offline operation, capsules, federation, rollback, verification, and substitution convert infrastructure from a potential dependency into a source of local capability.

```text
central service
→ reproducible package / protocol
→ local operation
→ independent continuity
```

### 7. Capital → democratic allocation

The Zero-Skim design separates contribution from political standing. Money can supply resources without automatically purchasing more votes over allocation.

This is a direct power-redirection pattern:

> **Economic contribution → common resource pool → democratic allocation power**

rather than:

> **Economic contribution → proportional political authority**

### 8. Dominance → evolutionary alternatives

Forkability, competing Kristals, branch mobility, and recombination redirect some of the power of incumbency toward challengers.

The system does not need to assume that the dominant branch is permanently best. It needs viable pathways through which a better or complementary branch can demonstrate value and gather support.

## Power sharing is not equalization

Power sharing does not mean every actor must have identical capability, expertise, responsibility, or authority.

It means that **useful asymmetries do not silently become terminal dependencies**.

The objective is therefore not flatness. It is a governable ecology in which generated capability can circulate and where important functions can be learned, reproduced, challenged, substituted, or evolved.

## Canonical sequence

The Power Show can now be summarized as:

> **Generate → Compose → Close the loop → Preserve returns → Compound → Route → Share → Bind → Contest → Fork → Evolve**

This is how the repository reconciles two ambitions that can otherwise appear contradictory:

> **Make kOA immensely powerful.**
>
> **Make that power increasingly usable by others rather than terminally owned by one center.**

## Continue

- [`07-component-power-matrix.md`](07-component-power-matrix.md)
- [`08-governance-after-capability.md`](08-governance-after-capability.md)
- [`../constitution/power-redirection-sharing.md`](../constitution/power-redirection-sharing.md)
- [`../koa/koali-protocolization.md`](../koa/koali-protocolization.md)
- [`../cases/ethical-fiction-self-binding.md`](../cases/ethical-fiction-self-binding.md)

# Cross-Domain kOA Power Surface

kOA's largest effects appear when capability crosses domain boundaries.

## Cross-domain graph

```text
SEMANTIC
   ↓
EPISTEMIC → CREDIBILITY → VISIBILITY → AGENDA → POLITICAL
    ↓             ↓            ↓                     ↓
 COGNITIVE      NETWORK      NARRATIVE            ALLOCATION
    ↓             ↓            ↓                     ↓
 DECISION ← COLLECTIVE ← AFFILIATION ← BRAND      ECONOMIC
    ↓                                                  ↓
ADMINISTRATIVE → EXECUTION → RETURNS → MEMORY → COMPOUNDING
    ↓                                                  ↓
TECHNICAL / INFRASTRUCTURAL ← FUNDING ← MONETARY / ECONOMIC
    ↓
FEDERATION → SUBSTITUTION → FORK → EVOLUTION
```

No arrow means automatic legitimacy. Each arrow is a **possible conversion path** that must be made explicit and governed.

## High-leverage conversion chains

### Knowledge to political influence

```text
knowledge
→ credibility
→ visibility
→ agenda access
→ political influence
```

### Money to political leverage

```text
money
→ infrastructure
→ reach
→ attention
→ organizational capacity
→ political leverage
```

### Brand to economic capacity

```text
brand
→ attention
→ affiliation
→ purchases / funding
→ infrastructure
→ development capacity
```

### Technical control to de facto governance

```text
technical administration
→ defaults
→ compatibility
→ dependency
→ inability to substitute
→ quasi-sovereign leverage
```

### Memory to future power

```text
institutional memory
→ precedent
→ lower coordination cost
→ faster action
→ stronger results
→ greater credibility
```

## Why the map matters

A component can be perfectly legitimate inside its original function and still become dangerous through conversion.

This is the basis of **Power Stacking** and **Capture**:

> **Functional Control → Cross-Domain Power**

The appropriate question is not merely “is this component democratic?” but:

> **What other domains can control of this component buy?**

## Routing layer

The kOA response is to combine:

- function separation;
- conversion firewalls;
- scoped authority;
- protocolization;
- self-hosting;
- portable evidence;
- baseline/advisory separation;
- explicit ethics;
- branch mobility;
- verification, contestation, substitution, and exit.

See [`14-power-conversion-and-routing-map.md`](14-power-conversion-and-routing-map.md).

# Political, Economic, and Monetary Power

These domains interact strongly but should not be collapsed.

## Political power

Political power concerns who can shape:

- political standing;
- agenda;
- deliberation;
- decision rights;
- representation;
- mandates;
- amendment;
- vetoes;
- legitimacy;
- allocation decisions;
- execution of public decisions.

kOA touches political power across the full chain:

> **Meaning → Knowledge → Visibility → Credibility → Agenda → Deliberation → Decision → Execution → Memory**

This means a system can preserve formal equality at the ballot and still create major political asymmetries upstream.

### kOA mechanisms

- SemantiK / Kristal: what becomes intelligible and evidentially stable;
- Konnaxion: what becomes discoverable and discussable;
- Koali / EkoH: which competence or contribution becomes legible;
- SmartVote: how alternative readings of a decision become visible;
- Orgo: how a decision becomes implementation;
- memory: what becomes precedent;
- branch ecology: how institutional alternatives emerge.

## Economic power

Economic power concerns control or mobilization of scarce resources:

- money;
- property;
- productive assets;
- labor and time;
- infrastructure;
- funding;
- procurement;
- contracts;
- philanthropy;
- access to markets.

Money is a resource stock. When mobilizable, it becomes economic puissance. When used to alter another actor's options, bargaining position, access, or allocation, it becomes relational economic power.

```text
money
→ purchasing / funding / bargaining puissance
→ access / allocation / dependency effects
```

### kOA mechanisms

- Zero-Skim: common resource pooling and democratic allocation;
- brand / merchandise: symbolic → economic conversion;
- credibility: opportunity and funding access;
- networks: collaborators, customers, institutions, opportunities;
- execution: turning funding into actual capability;
- compounding: returns become future assets.

## Monetary power

Monetary power concerns the infrastructure and rules of exchange itself:

- issuance;
- unit of account;
- liquidity;
- settlement;
- payment rails;
- reserves;
- convertibility;
- credit and debt conditions;
- access to transaction systems.

This domain can create exceptional leverage because nearly every economic actor depends on exchange infrastructure.

### Legacy KingCoin as stress test

The older KingCoin material proposes highly centralized functions including monetary coordination, taxation, redistribution, sanctions, settlement, and public investment. In the current repository these are **LEGACY-PROPOSAL** material.

Their analytical value is to show the danger of:

```text
monetary authority
+ taxation
+ allocation
+ sanctions
+ governance
+ transaction infrastructure
= extreme power stacking
```

The evolution of Power Dynamics moves away from “design a sufficiently just center” toward:

> **Design powerful functions that do not become terminal arbitrary authority.**

## Conversion firewalls between the three domains

| Conversion | Status |
|---|---|
| money → funding | desirable |
| funding → infrastructure | desirable |
| infrastructure → broader capability | desirable |
| money → proportional political standing | blocked by principle |
| credibility → informed advice | desirable |
| credibility → sovereign decision right | blocked by principle |
| monetary infrastructure → arbitrary exclusion | constitutional risk |
| administrative execution → mandate redefinition | blocked by principle |

## The key question

> **How can material and monetary capability increase collective agency without converting wealth, rails, or administration into unaccountable political sovereignty?**

That is a core Power Dynamics question, not a side topic.

# Power Conversion and Routing Map

The central constitutional problem can be expressed as a routing problem:

```text
RESOURCE / CAPABILITY
        ↓
LEGITIMATE FUNCTION
        ↓
NEW LEVERAGE
        ↓
┌─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
│ governed conversion │ silent conversion       │
│                     │                         │
│ advice              │ sovereignty             │
│ funding             │ vote buying             │
│ attention           │ truth                   │
│ maintenance         │ political control       │
│ execution           │ mandate capture         │
│ local capability    │ user captivity          │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘
        ↓
ROUTING / FIREWALL / COUNTERPOWER
```

## Main conversion firewalls

| Source | Allowed / useful destination | Automatic destination to resist | Mechanisms |
|---|---|---|---|
| Expertise | advice, interpretation, warning | political sovereignty | domain bounds, parallel readings, baseline preservation |
| Money | funding, procurement, infrastructure | more votes / standing | democratic allocation rules |
| Brand | attention, affiliation, commerce | truth / legitimacy | architecture-first references, brand ≠ protocol |
| Narrative | translation, mobilization, memory | unquestionable authority | ethical self-binding, criticism, self-mockery |
| Credibility | discovery, trust cues | final authority | contestable evidence, plural distributions |
| Technical administration | maintenance, implementation | political outcome control | scoped roles, audit, revocability |
| Execution | carrying out mandate | redefining mandate | explicit decision-to-work boundary |
| Infrastructure | capability, continuity | captivity | self-hosting, substitution, federation |
| Incumbency | demonstrated reliability | permanent dominance | branch mobility, lineage, recombination |

## Main routing destinations

### Toward users

- portability;
- local operation;
- usable evidence;
- exit;
- substitution;
- personal optionality.

### Toward communities

- shared knowledge;
- local semantic governance;
- democratic allocation;
- collective memory;
- community-run distributions.

### Toward protocols

- explicit contracts;
- schemas;
- testable behavior;
- interoperability;
- independent implementations.

### Toward branches

- lineage;
- variation;
- comparison;
- discoverability;
- support accumulation;
- replacement capacity.

### Away from terminal founder ownership

- documentation;
- distributed mastery;
- protocolization;
- independent operation;
- separation of persona, brand, protocol, and governance;
- succession and maintainer plurality.

## Routing quality

A mechanism is not genuinely power-sharing merely because the license says “open” or because export exists in theory.

Power sharing should be evaluated by **effective possibility**:

> Can another actor actually reproduce, operate, understand, contest, substitute, or evolve the function under real conditions?

This is the difference between formal openness and effective counterpower.

# The Propagation Engine

kOA does not rely on one flywheel.

It can contain several partially independent loops whose outputs become one another's inputs.

```text
BUILD
  ↓
USEFUL CAPABILITY
  ↓
WORLD / DEMONSTRATION
  ↓
UNDERSTANDING
  ↓
PILOT
  ↓
EVIDENCE
  ├────────→ CREDIBILITY ───────→ INSTITUTIONAL ACCESS
  │                                  ↓
  │                              MORE PILOTS
  │
  └────────→ OPERATIONAL DEMAND ─→ MANAGED SERVICE
                                     ↓
                                   REVENUE
                                     ↓
                                DEVELOPMENT
                                     ↓
                             GREATER CAPABILITY
                                     ↺
```

Other loops attach:

```text
pilot → training material → operator → independent deployment → more pilots
```

```text
integration → more capability → more usefulness → demand for integration
```

```text
objects → SmartVote signal → better discovery → reuse → more objects
```

```text
new language → new community → contribution → better language resources
```

```text
founder work → protocols / tools / documentation → autonomous operators
→ lower founder bottleneck → more parallel work
```

The most important property is therefore not “many mechanisms.”

> **The mechanisms share returns.**

The constitutional requirement is equally important:

> **Positive feedback must increase distributed capability faster than it increases terminal dependency.**

See [`../PROPAGATION.md`](../PROPAGATION.md).

# kOA Power Show

This directory is the **front stage** of Power Dynamics.

It answers three questions in order:

1. **Why is kOA immensely powerful?**
2. **What kinds of power can that capability create across domains?**
3. **How is that power routed, constrained, shared, contested, and evolved?**

## Guided sequence

0. [`00-why-koa-is-immensely-powerful.md`](00-why-koa-is-immensely-powerful.md) — the shortest complete answer.
1. [`01-capability-spectrum.md`](01-capability-spectrum.md) — the capability spectrum.
2. [`02-individual-to-federation.md`](02-individual-to-federation.md) — capability across scale.
3. [`03-composition-engine.md`](03-composition-engine.md) — why the whole can exceed the sum.
4. [`04-compounding-engine.md`](04-compounding-engine.md) — how action creates greater future capability.
5. [`05-conversion-chains.md`](05-conversion-chains.md) — how resources and forms of puissance convert.
6. [`06-pre-political-chain.md`](06-pre-political-chain.md) — power before formal decision.
7. [`07-component-power-matrix.md`](07-component-power-matrix.md) — component-by-component power surfaces.
8. [`08-governance-after-capability.md`](08-governance-after-capability.md) — why success creates a constitutional problem.
9. [`09-why-this-is-not-just-software.md`](09-why-this-is-not-just-software.md) — kOA as capability infrastructure.
10. [`10-counterpower-and-optionality.md`](10-counterpower-and-optionality.md) — verification, contestation, substitution, exit.
11. [`11-power-routing-and-sharing.md`](11-power-routing-and-sharing.md) — how leverage is redirected outward.
12. [`12-cross-domain-power-surface.md`](12-cross-domain-power-surface.md) — the multi-domain power map.
13. [`13-political-economic-monetary-power.md`](13-political-economic-monetary-power.md) — explicit separation of political, economic, and monetary power.
14. [`14-power-conversion-and-routing-map.md`](14-power-conversion-and-routing-map.md) — conversion firewalls and routing destinations.
15. [`15-propagation-engine.md`](15-propagation-engine.md) — how returns cross branches and couple adoption, evidence, revenue, tooling, operators, language, integration, and founder decentering.

The canonical full-surface summary is [`../POWER-SURFACE.md`](../POWER-SURFACE.md).
