# Architect-Confirmed Design Notes

This file records current design intentions that have been explicitly confirmed during repository development but are not yet backed by a canonical technical specification in the supplied corpus.

They should not be cited as if they were already implemented facts.

## Koali ownership and distribution

Current intention:

> Koali should not remain the exclusive personal property or terminal credibility authority of its founder. Its useful capability should be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and capable of plural distributions around interoperable structures.

Technical evidence still needed:

- kernel / interoperability specification;
- evidence portability model;
- distribution identity;
- policy versioning;
- branch / fork procedure;
- registry / discovery design;
- brand / trademark relationship to protocol implementations.

## Ethical principles inside fiction

Current intention and source-backed practice:

> The narrative layer explicitly publishes ethical principles rather than leaving them as private founder intent.

S02 provides direct source support for a living ethical code, vigilance against monopoly and domination, public recognition, self-mockery, revision, and non-dogmatic transmission.


## Reference Kristal stewardship and worldview forks

Current intention:

> A principal encyclopedic Kristal is envisioned as an institutionally stewarded reference corpus, including a proposed UNESCO stewardship role, while remaining forkable into incompatible worldview, cultural, religious, fictional, or experimental branches.

This records an intended stewardship model, **not an existing UNESCO agreement, endorsement, or deployment**.

The intended constitutional distinction is:

```text
reference stewardship ≠ ownership of truth
forkability ≠ equal evidentiary support
```

Technical evidence still needed:

- reference-branch governance specification;
- fork and lineage rules;
- reader-policy model;
- evidentiary comparison across branches;
- institutional stewardship / handoff mechanism.

## Founder opposition rehearsal

Current intention:

> kOA should not only permit disagreement with its founder; it should establish a practiced precedent for organized, legitimate opposition to a founder-backed proposal.

A proposed civic resilience drill uses an otherwise coherent political platform containing one conspicuously abusive tattoo-prohibition / compulsory-removal policy. The intended output is a reusable counterpower pathway, not adoption of the policy.

The design objective is to test whether symbolic founder authority can be prevented from becoming truth or policy immunity.

## Epistemic authority corruption drill

Current intention:

> A deliberately false flat-Earth Kristal branch should be used as an adversarial epistemic stress test to study how weak, dependent, misleading, or fabricated authority signals can accumulate into apparent credibility and consensus.

The primary intended output is a postmortem taxonomy of corruption patterns and detection failures. This note records the threat-model direction, not an operational procedure for deceiving external institutions or audiences.


## Propagation and coupled loops

Current intention:

> kOA should be analyzed not only as a set of compounding capabilities but as a network of partially independent propagation loops whose returns can be reused across branches.

Examples include:

- revenue → shared development capacity;
- evidence → credibility → institutional access;
- training → independent operator → more deployments;
- integration → wider capability → more integration demand;
- language support → community access → contribution → improved language assets;
- documentation → lower review cost → feedback → better documentation;
- founder knowledge → protocol / tools / documentation → reduced founder bottleneck.

The design requirement is that positive feedback increase distributed capability without automatically increasing terminal authority or dependency at the same rate.

See `sources/propagation-design-notes.md` for the current detailed record.

---
maturity: "CURRENT-CORE"
claim_type: "ANALYTICAL-RECONSTRUCTION"
scope: "GENERAL"
version: "6.0"
title: "Epistemic Rules for the Repository"
source_basis:
---
# Epistemic Rules for the Repository

1. Preserve source terminology when summarizing a source.
2. Label reconstructions and new concepts explicitly.
3. Do not silently convert legacy proposals into current principles.
4. Preserve disagreements among current documents.
5. Treat technical claims about current implementation as provisional unless supported by direct specifications or code.
6. Avoid treating analytical thesis drafts as canonical technical specifications.
7. Do not infer perfect measurement of competence, ethics, credibility, legitimacy, or truth from the presence of a scoring mechanism.
8. Use source IDs for provenance and technical evidence for implementation claims.


## Architect-confirmed design notes

`USER-CONFIRMED-DESIGN` may be used for a current design intention explicitly confirmed by the project architect in the working process but not yet supported by a canonical source document or technical specification.

Rules:

- do not present it as source-derived;
- identify missing technical evidence;
- replace or supplement it with canonical documentation when available;
- distinguish design intention from deployed implementation.

source_basis:
---
# Propagation Design Notes — 2026-09-21

This source records current architect-confirmed mechanisms relevant to propagation and adoption that are not all fully represented in the earlier supplied source corpus.

It records **current capability claims and design intention**, not independent validation of scale or effectiveness.

## Cross-branch returns

Current intention:

> Successful branches should be able to return money, credibility, evidence, code, methods, relationships, operators, or other reusable assets to shared infrastructure and other branches.

A branch may therefore finance or de-risk another branch without requiring all branches to share the same business model.

## Tools that build tools

Current reported practice:

> AI-assisted development methods and internal development / diagnostic / deployment tooling are used to increase the speed and quality with which additional kOA tooling can be built.

Evidence needed for external claims:

- comparable effort measurements;
- repeatability by non-founder operators;
- regression / quality metrics;
- documented automation reuse across repositories.

## Worlds

Current reported capability:

> Konnaxion can be seeded with structured simulated or reconstructed Worlds derived from public sources, allowing a demonstration environment to begin with realistic actors, questions, positions, sources, and relationships rather than an empty platform.

Required epistemic distinction:

```text
source material
≠ reconstruction
≠ simulation
≠ live contribution
```

A World demonstrates representational and demonstration capacity. It does not by itself prove adoption or real-world impact.

## Cinematic demonstration pipeline

Current reported capability:

> A cinematic demonstration engine, combined with interface tooling and browser automation, can generate repeatable high-quality product demonstrations from scripted tours.

Propagation hypothesis:

> Lowering the marginal cost of contextual demonstrations can lower the explanation barrier for new audiences.

Evidence needed:

- production time per demonstration;
- viewer → evaluator conversion;
- evaluator → pilot conversion;
- repeatability across languages / countries / domains.

## Machine-readable documentation

Current reported practice / intention:

> Documentation is increasingly optimized for AI-assisted reading through structured Markdown, indexes, context packs, and `llms.txt`-style concise machine entry surfaces.

Propagation hypothesis:

> If external reviewers can use their own AI systems to navigate the corpus, due-diligence and explanation costs may fall.

## Multilingual scale

Current reported milestone:

> A complete Albanian implementation has been produced in a Grammatical Framework-oriented language architecture, strengthening the plausibility of systematic expansion toward much broader language coverage.

The 300-language target remains a scale objective, not a claim of current equal-quality support.

Recommended evidence model:

- language conformance tiers;
- compile / grammar tests;
- gold corpus metrics;
- native-speaker review;
- human hours and AI-assisted hours;
- coverage across language families and scripts.

## Cultural and narrative diffusion

Current assets include multiple books, albums, podcasts, web material, essays, and narrative surfaces intended to explain complex architecture through different cognitive entry modes.

These assets should remain optional to operational adoption.

`Narrative usefulness ≠ factual authority.`

## Pre-exposure / outreach

Current strategy includes earlier outreach to people and institutions in several sectors before later deployment phases.

Epistemic rule:

`contacted ≠ received ≠ read ≠ familiar ≠ supportive ≠ partner`

The only defensible propagation claim without stronger evidence is that prior contact can create a possibility of later recognition.

## Founder exposure and pseudonymity

Current practice uses a public architect identity and a separate narrative persona / pseudonym.

The persona can provide creative and exposure optionality.

It must not be treated as the reason the system is resilient.

System resilience depends on independent reproducibility, distributed mastery, public artifacts, explicit authority, branchability, and continuity during founder absence.

## Movement self-use

Current design intention:

> When multiple independent participants exist, the kOA initiative can use kOA tooling to coordinate parts of its own work, allowing the movement / community to become a real operational test environment.

The relevant threshold is not a branding declaration that a “movement” exists. It is evidence of multi-actor independent capability.

# Sources and Provenance

The supplied corpus is the repository's primary evidence base.

Source IDs are stable references to the 28 supplied files. They identify provenance, not endorsement or current status.

The repo distinguishes:

- **FOUNDATIONAL / CURRENT-CORE** source material;
- **CURRENT-EXPERIMENTAL** designs;
- **ANALYTICAL** thesis drafts and reconstructions;
- **LEGACY-PROPOSAL** material preserved for genealogy and stress testing.

The source corpus contains real internal tensions. These should not be silently reconciled.

# Source Status

`CURRENT-CORE` and `CURRENT-EXPERIMENTAL` describe recent design direction. `CURRENT-DESIGN-NOTES` records architect-confirmed current mechanisms or intentions that still require independent technical or adoption evidence. `LEGACY-PROPOSAL` identifies earlier proposals retained for historical and stress-test value. `ANALYTICAL-RECONSTRUCTION` identifies synthesis rather than primary architecture.

Status is not truth ranking. A legacy proposal can still expose a useful mechanism, and a recent analytical document is not automatically a canonical technical specification.

See [`corpus-map.md`](corpus-map.md) for per-source classification.

# Technical Evidence Map

The 28-file corpus is strong on philosophy, architecture summaries, institutional proposals, and analytical reconstructions. A rigorous next-stage audit should add direct technical specifications for the exact loci where **capacity becomes puissance and puissance becomes relational power**.

## Highest-priority evidence to import

1. **Civilizational Coherence — power/capture chapters**: capture, lock-in, verification, contestation, substitution, exit.
2. **kOA Digital Ecosystem Layer Model / Layer 0**: separation of functions and mandate boundaries.
3. **Kristal authority registry, trust roots, reader policies, federation/versioning**: epistemic puissance, authority, branch lineage.
4. **EkoH ScoreConfiguration, ScoreHistory, visibility/access policies**: who defines credibility readings and how those readings affect opportunity.
5. **SmartVote lens registry, defaults, raw/weighted storage, version governance**: decision puissance, discoverability, advisory power.
6. **Orgo RBAC, routing, escalation, closure, override rules**: operational puissance and execution power.
7. **Konnaxion moderation, discovery, identity, ranking policies**: collective puissance, attention, agenda power.
8. **Capsule Manager signing, registry, update, rollback, deployment profiles**: deployment puissance, infrastructural dependency, choke points.
9. **Koali distribution specification**: credibility portability, alternative distributions, interoperability.
10. **Brand / trademark governance and founder-control inventory**: symbolic assets, domains, repos, release keys, registries, marks, current relational control.

## Audit rule

A normative statement such as “EkoH is non-sovereign” is not sufficient technical evidence. The audit must identify the configuration, permission, key, role, registry, default, or protocol boundary that explains:

1. what resource and potential exist;
2. what becomes usable capacity;
3. what becomes effective puissance;
4. where that puissance creates relational power;
5. which counterpowers constrain that relation.

## Mobilization / mediation evidence

Also inspect release permissions, publication gates, queueing, rate limits, ranking, recommendation, translation, summarization, distribution dependencies, analytics feedback, alternative channels, moderation controls, update keys, and bottlenecks.


## Koali protocolization / ownership-decoupling evidence

To validate the current design intention that Koali be duplicable, shareable, branchable, and plural, collect:

- common-kernel / protocol specification;
- distribution identity and versioning;
- evidence / history portability;
- policy interchange contracts;
- alternative distribution discovery;
- fork / branch procedure;
- registry and signing governance;
- trademark / brand separation from protocol implementation.

# Repository Status

Version 6.3 retains the **kOA Power Show** and cross-domain Power Surface while strengthening adversarial resilience around founder authority, epistemic credibility, and plural Kristal branches.

## Presentation architecture

> **Why kOA is powerful → Power Surface → Composition → Closure → Compounding → Propagation → Cross-Domain Conversion → Power Routing → Constitution → Evolution**

The analytical engine remains:

> **ontology × dynamics × domains**

## Major v6.3 changes

- added `PROPAGATION.md` as the canonical map for propagation and coupled feedback loops;
- formally separated **compounding** from **propagation**;
- added `propagation/` with adoption barriers, coupled loops, cross-branch returns, distribution surfaces, founder-absence tests, damping, metrics, and a kOA loop catalog;
- added `dynamics/13-propagation-and-coupled-loops.md`;
- added `koa/propagation-adoption.md` and `showcase/15-propagation-engine.md`;
- added `assessment/propagation-audit.md`;
- added `feedback-loop` and `coupled-loop` schemas;
- recorded architect-confirmed propagation tooling / design notes as S29, including Worlds, cinematic demonstrations, machine-readable documentation, multilingual scale, cross-branch returns, movement self-use, and founder exposure optionality;
- made the epistemic distinction `contacted ≠ familiar ≠ supportive`, `simulation ≠ evidence`, and `persona optionality ≠ system resilience` explicit;
- added the canonical propagation condition: **healthy propagation increases distributed capability faster than terminal dependency**.

## Major v6.2 changes

- added `Academic-Lineage/` as a structured external-research layer;
- connected relational power and dependency to Dahl, Emerson, Cook et al., and Resource Dependence Theory;
- connected federation and institutional structure to Ostrom and the institutional-grammar tradition;
- added standards / compatibility failure modes from modularity, network-effects, excess-inertia, and path-dependence research;
- connected forkability and founder decentering to exit / voice, open-source governance, and peer-production research;
- added network-power, brokerage, social-capital, and cumulative-advantage lineages;
- added a dedicated SmartVote / expertise lineage covering domain-specific expertise, calibrated expert judgment, select crowds, diversity, and social-choice impossibility / manipulation results;
- added classification, infrastructure, code-as-governance, epistemic injustice, argumentation, belief revision, and cybernetic feedback references;
- added collective-action, diffusion, threshold, capability, concerted-power, and legibility lineages;
- explicitly distinguished **academic correspondence** from endorsement, historical influence, or implementation validation;
- proposed **Installed-Base Power** and **interface power** as explicit audit targets for future framework integration.

### Retained v6.1 adversarial-resilience additions

- added founder opposition as a distinct resilience test beyond founder absence;
- introduced **rehearsed counterpower**: practiced opposition pathways can be stronger than merely formal rights;
- added a founder-backed civic fire-drill case and reusable **Tattoo Protocol** concept;
- added an epistemic authority corruption case using a known-false worldview as a stress test;
- distinguished apparent source multiplicity from causal independence;
- clarified that Kristal forkability permits incompatible worldviews without implying equal evidentiary support;
- added reference-stewardship firewalls: maintained reference ≠ monopoly on truth;
- recorded architect-confirmed directions for proposed UNESCO stewardship, worldview forks, and adversarial resilience drills without presenting them as implemented facts.

## Current core

- `Resource → Potential → Capacity → Puissance → Power Relation → Effect → Return`;
- `Know → Choose → Act → Remember → Know better`;
- Breadth × Composition × Closure × Memory × Compounding × Propagation × Replication × Routing;
- political power as a pre-political-through-execution chain;
- economic puissance and allocation power;
- monetary power as rail / issuance / liquidity / settlement / credit power;
- cross-domain conversion, stacking, and capture;
- Power Conversion Firewalls;
- deliberate routing of capability, authority, returns, and optionality;
- narrative self-binding;
- Koali ownership decoupling as an architect-confirmed direction;
- verification, contestation, substitution, and exit;
- branch mobility and evolutionary governance;
- `Strong distributed puissance. Weak sovereignty.`

## Important maturity distinction

KingCoin remains **LEGACY-PROPOSAL** material. It is retained as a monetary and cross-domain power-stacking stress test, not as current kOA constitutional doctrine.

Koali plural distributions / ownership decoupling remain **architect-confirmed design direction** until current technical specifications demonstrate the intended kernel, portability, policy identity, branchability, and interoperability.

## Technical evidence still required

A concrete current-state power audit still needs direct evidence for authority registries, reader policies, trust roots, scoring configuration, SmartVote defaults/lenses, Orgo RBAC and escalation, Konnaxion ranking/moderation, signing/update keys, deployment registries, brand/trademark governance, domains, maintainer controls, and operational topology.

See [`sources/technical-evidence-map.md`](sources/technical-evidence-map.md).

# Repository Tree

The v6.3 reading hierarchy is intentionally different from the filesystem hierarchy:

> **Why kOA is powerful → Power Surface → Composition → Compounding → Propagation → Conversion → Routing → Constitution → Evolution → Audit**

```text
Power-Dynamics-v6/
├── .github/
│   ├── ISSUE_TEMPLATE/
│   │   ├── concept-proposal.md
│   │   ├── power-audit.md
│   │   └── power-surface-audit.md
│   └── PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md
├── Academic-Lineage/
│   ├── 01-power-dependence-and-domination.md
```
